An explicit particular array with the paid square\n\nAssume throughout this section that u,w,t>1 and write\n\n    a=u-1, b=w-1, d=t-1,\n    K0=a^(-2)-1,\n    K1=(a*b)^(-2)-1,\n    K2=(a*b*d)^(-2)-1.\n\nThe necessary scalar matching relation from(8) is\n\n    K2=C^2*d^2+kappa*t^2-1. Here is a full-matrix derivation.\n\nFor 0<=alpha<beta<=4 define a row-sum-zero matrix J^[alpha,beta], zero\noutside that interval, by the following off-diagonal entries inside it:\n\n    J_ij=(i-alpha+1)h_i-1, i<j,\n    J_ij=1+(beta-i)h_i, j<i.\n\nUse h4=0 only as a convention in this formula; its value does not\naffect the final row. A column before the interval\nvanishes by the zero row sum; a column after it vanishes by triangularity.\nIts symmetric product is therefore diagonal, with ith diagonal\n\n    (h_i-1)^2(i-alpha+1)(beta-i)\n                             -(i-alpha)(beta-i+1). Every fixed-first-row re-selection, with all entries explicit\n\nOn tail indices1,2,3,4 define a skew matrix A_cyc, zero on row/column0,\nby the upper entries\n\n    A12=-b*d*t, A13=b(d^2-1), A14=b*t,\n    A23=-(1+b*d^2), A24=1-b*d, A34=-w. The particular\narray of Section3 therefore exists algebraically and has the same\nfirst row as the proposed array, even if its own signs fail.\n\nFor their difference put A=(L-L^0)T. Equality of the full remainders\nmakes A skew. Equality of the first rows of L makes row0 of A zero,\nand skewness makes column0 zero. Equal flow gives Ae=0, and row-sum\nzero gives Ap=0. Also 2/5<a<1, so b_*>0.\nIt follows that\n\n    0<3+2d-b<H_*, 0<K1<K_*,\n    2(u+w)=2(2+a+b)>186/25.\n\nThus(27) would require H_* K_*>186/25. We prove its opposite on the\nentire interval2/5<a<1, using two elementary concave-quadratic bounds.\n\nFor 2/5<=a<=1/2, the minimum of a(43/25-a) is66/125; the other\nendpoint value is61/100. Hence\n\n    H_* K_* <= (139/50)*(11269/4356)\n              =1566391/217800 <186/25.               (28)\n\nThe final comparison has positive numerator difference54041 after\nputting186/25 on denominator217800.\n\nFor 1/2<=a<=1, the minimum of a(43/25-a) is61/100; the other endpoint\nvalue is18/25. Hence\n\n    H_* K_* <= (82/25)*(6279/3721)\n              =514878/93025 <186/25.                 (29)\n\nHere the numerator difference is177228. Inequalities(28)--(29)\ncontradict the consequence of(27). This closes all0<d<1/2.\n\nTogether with Section6.1 and the zero-step argument, this excludes\nEVERY array in(1) at C>=193/25, including all nonnegative boundary\ncoefficients, arbitrary real rho, and every nonzero square price.\n\n## 7. The original Goal remains OPEN.\n\nThis draft studies ALL positive step excesses in the two-sequence\nfactorization. No constant, alternating, palindromic, monotone, or other\nword pattern is imposed. At length5 there are four independent positive\nexcesses and a final balance root; at length6 there are five and a final\nbalance root. All30 or42 original ordered multipliers and all6 or7\nindependent formal gradients are retained. The restriction to positive\nfactors and a zero original matrix remainder is declared throughout.\n\nAccepted inputs are fact04219554e41d9750, especially its proof Sections2,\n10--13, for the benchmark scalar recurrence, original full certificate,\nphysical joins, and arbitrary-length physical substitution; and\nfact1252b8823bb5a290 for the original smooth interpolation convention.\nThe earlier draft0421d0c1066b8f2d is recalled as awareness only. Its\nvariable-step formulas and every new ingredient needed here are proved\njointly below. There is no use of an unverified result as a premise.\n\nThe conclusion is a scope-complete obstruction of the enlarged declared\nclass: its capacity A is at most C_r at every length r, and is strictly\nless than C_r for r=5 and6. Thus no member reaches A>Z_6/Z_1 or A>Z_7/Z_1.\nIn fact no fixed integer ray can rescue one: Z_((r+1)b)>=C_r Z_b for\nevery b>=1. Its actual primitive-leaf substitution has normalized error\nstrictly ABOVE the original benchmark at every nonempty input-tree size\nwhen r=5 or6. This is not a conclusion about the unrestricted primitive\ncone, other factorizations, nonzero PSD remainders, or unrestricted W.\n\nThe new bridge is an interval-product constraint forced by the factors.\nA separate, larger scalar relaxation with all these interval constraints\nhas exact maximum C_r. The proof uses tight intervals and a perturbation\nof two unconstrained coordinates; it is not a numerical optimization.\n\nAll newly introduced local symbols below have the implicit suffix fw37.\nIn particular U_i,D_i are factor sequences, not the benchmark U_n^ZJ;\nP_k,R_k are prefix quantities; and q_I is an interval product.\n\n## 1. The original full-space certificate and the declared factor class\n\nLet r>=1, let h_i=1+t_i with t_i>0 for 0<=i<r, and put\n\n s=sum_(i<r)h_i,       A=1+s.\n\nAll r+1 formal gradients g_0,...,g_r and values f_0,...,f_r remain\nindependent. At normalized smoothness1 set\n\n x_i=x_0-sum_(j<i)h_j g_j,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)\n       +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sA||g_r||^2.\n\nThe full certificate uses Lambda_ij>=0 for every i!=j and Lambda_ii=0.\nLet e be the all-ones vector of length r+1, and define\n\n T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,\n L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r, -s(s+2)).\n\nThe required flow and matrix are\n\n L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,\n M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.                 (1.1)\n\nAccepted04219554e41d9750 gives the polynomial identity\n\n V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij\n                  =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.          (1.2)\n\nFor this candidate class only, require M_prim=0 on the entire original\nspace. Write Qbar_ij=-2Q_ji, so explicitly\n\n Qbar_ij=2(f_i-f_j)-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2.\n\nChoose finite strictly positive factors U_0,...,U_(r-1),D_1,...,D_r.\nSet U_-1=0,D_(r+1)=0, and set t_r=0 only as an auxiliary placeholder.\nFor every 0<=i<j<=r define\n\n mu_ij=(U_i-U_(i-1))(D_j+t_j D_(j+1)),\n mu_ji=(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)(D_j-D_(j+1)),\n Lambda_ij=2mu_ji,   Lambda_ji=2mu_ij.                  (1.3)\n\nThere is no unused ordered entry. The required orientation is\nLambda=2mu^T, because Qbar_ij=-2Q_ji. The declared class consists of\nALL these positive-factor choices with nonnegative original entries\nand the exact original flow and M_prim=0. Equivalently,\n\n                     V_h=sum_(i!=j)mu_ij Qbar_ij.        (1.4)\n\nFrom mu_(i,r)=(U_i-U_(i-1))D_r and\nmu_(j,0)=t_0 U_0(D_j-D_(j+1)), nonnegative entries force\n\n U_i>=U_(i-1),          D_j>=D_(j+1).                    (1.5)\n\nConversely these monotonicities make every entry in(1.3) nonnegative,\nsince all t_i,U_i,D_j in their stated ranges are positive. Equality in\n(1.5), hence zero original entries, is allowed. A simultaneous scaling\nU_i -> c U_i, D_j -> D_j/c with c>0 does not change any multiplier.\n\n## 2. Complete coefficient calculations\n\nFor i<j, telescoping gives\n\n sum_(a<=i)mu_(a,j)=U_i(D_j+t_j D_(j+1)),\n sum_(a>j)mu_(a,i)=(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)D_(j+1).\n\nThe coefficient of <g_i,g_j> in sum mu Qbar is\n\n 2(mu_ij+mu_ji)\n -2h_i U_i(D_j+t_j D_(j+1))\n +2h_j(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)D_(j+1).                         (2.1)\n\nAt j=r the last term is zero; h_r=1 may be used as an auxiliary\nplaceholder there. Direct expansion of(1.3) gives\n\n mu_ij+mu_ji=h_i U_i(D_j+t_j D_(j+1))\n               -h_j(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)D_(j+1),\n\nso(2.1) vanishes for every pair and for arbitrary unequal steps.\n\nPut d_i=sum_(j!=i)(mu_ij-mu_ji), p_0=0, and\np_k=U_(k-1)D_k for 1<=k<=r. The function coefficients in(1.4) give\n\n d_i=h_i for i<r,       d_r=-s.                          (2.2)\n\nAt a nonterminal i the incoming weights from smaller and larger\nindices respectively are\n\n I_i=U_(i-1)(D_i+t_iD_(i+1)),\n J_i=(U_(i-1)+t_iU_i)D_(i+1),\n\nwith I_0=0. Hence t_i J_i-I_i=t_i^2 p_(i+1)-p_i. The coefficient\nof ||g_i||^2 equals\n\n -d_i-2I_i-2J_i+2h_iJ_i\n             =-h_i+2(t_i^2p_(i+1)-p_i).\n\nMatching it with -h_i t_i from V_h proves\n\n t_i^2(2p_(i+1)+1)=2p_i+1.                              (2.3)\n\nThe terminal incoming weight is p_r, and its coefficient is s-2p_r.\nMatching -sA gives 2p_r+1=A^2.\n\nDefine prefix capacity and reciprocal product by\n\n P_k=1+sum_(i<k)(1+t_i),\n R_k=product_(i<k)t_i^(-1),       P_0=R_0=1.\n\nEquations(2.3) and the terminal coefficient now force\n\n p_k=(R_k^2-1)/2>0 for 1<=k<=r,\n R_r=P_r=A,               A product_(i<r)t_i=1.         (2.4)\n\nIn particular R_k>1 follows from positivity of the factors, not from\nan assumption that each t_i<1. Individual long steps are permitted.\n\n## 3. Necessity of the prefix formulas for EVERY positive representation\n\nIt is necessary to show that the familiar factor recurrence covers\nthe entire declared class, rather than only one convenient construction.\nFor 0<=k<r put\n\n u_k=(sum_(i<k)h_iU_i)/U_k,\n v_k=(sum_(j=k+2)^r h_(j-1)D_j)/D_(k+1).\n\nThe cut flow telescopes, using(1.3), to\n\n sum_(i<=k)d_i=p_(k+1)(1-t_k+v_k-u_k)=P_(k+1)-1.        (3.1)\n\nIndeed the outgoing cut sum is p_(k+1)(1+v_k), while its incoming\ncut sum is p_(k+1)(t_k+u_k). Also u_0=0 and v_(r-1)=0.\nAll v_k with k<r-1 are strictly positive.\n\nWe prove the exact formulas\n\n u_k=(P_k-R_k)/(1+R_(k+1)),\n v_k=(P_(k+1)-R_(k+1))/(R_(k+1)-1).                     (3.2)\n\nAt k=0 the first is u_0=0. Substituting p_1=(R_1^2-1)/2 and\nt_0=1/R_1 into(3.1) gives the second. Its positive value when r>=2\nalready implies P_1>R_1.\n\nFor the induction step let 1<=k<r and abbreviate\n\n P=P_k, R=R_k, Y=R_(k+1), q=U_k/U_(k-1)>0.\n\nThe preceding v formula and positivity give P>R>1. The recurrences\nand the product p_k=U_(k-1)D_k give\n\n u_k=(u_(k-1)+h_(k-1))/q,\n v_(k-1)=[p_(k+1)/(p_k q)](h_k+v_k).\n\nThe induction hypothesis supplies\n\n u_(k-1)+h_(k-1)=(P+R)/(1+R),\n v_(k-1)=(P-R)/(R-1).\n\nBy(3.1), v_k=u_k+delta_k with\n\n delta_k=2(P+t_k)/(Y^2-1)-1+t_k,\n p_(k+1)(h_k+delta_k)=P+RY.\n\nEliminating u_k and v_k gives the following quadratic for q:\n\n (1+R)(P-R)q^2-2(P+RY)q\n                   -(Y^2-1)(P+R)/(1+R)=0.              (3.3)\n\nIts two roots are exactly\n\n (P+R)(1+Y)/[(1+R)(P-R)],        (1-Y)/(1+R).\n\nThis follows by using\n(P+RY)^2+(P^2-R^2)(Y^2-1)=(PY+R)^2\nin the quadratic formula. The second root is negative, whereas q>0.\nThus the first is the only possible ratio. Inserting it gives\nu_k=(P-R)/(1+Y). Adding delta_k gives the v formula, since\n\n (P-t_kY)(Y-1)+2(P+t_k)+(t_k-1)(Y^2-1)\n                        =(Y+1)(P+1+t_k-Y).\n\nThis finishes the induction. Consequently every representation obeys\n\n 1<R_k<P_k for 1<=k<r,\n U_k/U_(k-1)=(P_k+R_k)(1+R_(k+1))\n                /[(1+R_k)(P_k-R_k)],                  (3.4)\n D_(k+1)/D_k=(P_k-R_k)(R_(k+1)-1)\n                /[(R_k-1)(P_k+R_k)].                  (3.5)\n\nThe strict prefix inequalities follow from v_(k-1)>0. No uniqueness\nof the full unrestricted primitive certificate has been claimed; only\nthe ratios inside the explicitly declared two-sequence family are forced.\n\n## 4. Exact free-word construction and all sign conditions\n\nFor completeness, the converse also holds. Take arbitrary positive\nexcesses t_0,...,t_(r-1) satisfying R_r=P_r and 1<R_k<P_k at every\nproper nonempty prefix. Normalize U_0=1, define U by(3.4), and set\nD_k=(R_k^2-1)/(2U_(k-1)). Formula(3.5) follows by division.\n\nFor each 1<=k<r, all factor signs are nonnegative exactly when\n\n t_k >= (P_k-R_k)/(P_k+R_k-2),\n t_k(P_k-R_k-2) <= P_k+R_k.                              (4.1)\n\nThe denominator of the first bound is positive. Inserting\nR_(k+1)=R_k/t_k into U_k/U_(k-1)>=1 and D_(k+1)/D_k<=1 proves\nthese two equivalences by multiplication by positive denominators.\nThe second inequality is left in the displayed form: division by\nP_k-R_k-2, whose sign may change, would be incorrect without a case split.\n\nTo check the full identity in this converse, the u formula(3.2) follows\nforward from its recurrence and(3.4). The v formula follows backward\nfrom v_(r-1)=0 and(3.5):\n\n h_k+v_k=(P_k+R_k)/(R_(k+1)-1),\n [D_(k+1)/D_k](h_k+v_k)=(P_k-R_k)/(R_k-1).\n\nFor each k set R=R_(k+1),P=P_k,t=t_k. Then\n\n p_(k+1)(v_k-u_k)\n  =[(R+1)(P+1+t-R)-(R-1)(P-tR)]/2\n  =P+t+(t-1)(R^2-1)/2.\n\nAdding p_(k+1)(1-t) proves the cut flow(3.1). Differences give\nd_i=h_i and total flow zero gives d_r=-s. Equation(2.3) proves every\ndiagonal match, and Section2 proves every mixed match. Thus(1.4)\nholds on ALL original formal coordinates, the original array is\nLambda=2mu^T>=0, its flow is(1.1), and M_prim=0 identically.\n\nThere is no uncomputed PSD test. There are only the explicit scalar\nsign conditions on this particular exact factorization. Strict U and D\nmonotonicities make every original off-diagonal entry strictly positive;\nweak monotonicities also remain in the class studied below.\n\nFor the initially proposed genuinely free parameterization, choose\nt_0,...,t_(r-2)>0 independently. Put P=P_(r-1), R=R_(r-1), and let\nthe final excess be the unique positive root\n\n              t^2+(P+1)t-R=0.                            (4.2)\n\nThis is exactly R/t=P+1+t, so it imposes R_r=P_r and nothing about\nany equality or pattern among earlier steps. The remaining prefix and\nsign tests are precisely(3.4),(4.1). This covers all the declared class,\nup to the harmless positive gauge of its factors, by Section3.\n\n## 5. The new consequence: every contiguous block is product-admissible\n\nFor each nonempty contiguous interval I=[l,k) of physical indices,\ndefine its mass, capacity, and product by\n\n H_I=sum_(i=l)^(k-1)h_i,\n K_I=1+H_I,\n q_I=product_(i=l)^(k-1)t_i.\n\nWe prove\n\n                     K_I q_I>=1.                        (5.1)\n\nEquality holds for the whole word. If k<r, the inequality is strict.\nThis is a consequence of the declared factor class, not a necessary\ncondition asserted for every primitive word.\n\nFor l=0, q_I=1/R_k, and(5.1) is P_k>=R_k, already proved.\nNow assume 1<=l<k<=r, and write\n\n P=P_l>1, R=R_l>1, H=H_I>0, q=q_I,\n P_k=P+H, R_k=R/q,\n b_j=(P_j-R_j)/(R_j-1) for 1<=j<=r.\n\nFormula(3.2) identifies b_j=v_(j-1), including b_r=0. The weighted\ntail sum defining v_(l-1) decomposes exactly as\n\n b_l=sum_(i=l)^(k-1) h_i D_(i+1)/D_l +(D_k/D_l)b_k.\n\nBy D monotonicity and b_k>=0 this gives b_l<=H+b_k. Since\nb_l+1=(P-1)/(R-1) and b_k+1=q(P+H-1)/(R-q), both denominators\nare positive. Multiplication and cancellation yield\n\n                 q>=1-H(R-1)/(P-1).                     (5.2)\n\nThe other endpoint condition R_k<=P_k gives R<=q(P+H). Combining\nthis with(5.2), whose right side decreases in R, yields\n\n q>=1-H[q(P+H)-1]/(P-1),\n q[P-1+H(P+H)]>=P-1+H.\n\nHere P-1+H(P+H)=(H+1)(P-1+H). Dividing by P-1+H>0 proves\nq>=1/(1+H), which is(5.1). If k<r, then R_k<P_k, so the second\ninequality is strict and(5.1) is strict as claimed.\n\nFor a suffix [l,r), equality in(5.1) holds exactly when\nD_l=D_(l+1)=...=D_r. Indeed the terminal prefix equality is exact,\nand equality in the tail estimate with b_r=0 requires every positive\nsummand h_i D_(i+1)/D_l to have D_(i+1)=D_l. This also checks the\npossibility of zero multipliers on the boundary of the factor class.\n\n## 6. Exact extremal theorem for the larger hereditary scalar class\n\nThis section is a separate elementary proof, included here in full.\nFor any r>=1, call a positive vector t=(t_0,...,t_(r-1)) admissible\nif, with h_i=1+t_i, it satisfies\n\n K_I q_I>=1 for EVERY nonempty contiguous I,\n A product_(i<r)t_i=1,       A=1+sum_(i<r)(1+t_i).        (6.1)\n\nThe empty word has capacity1. Define the benchmark kernel and recurrence\nas in accepted04219554e41d9750:\n\n Omega(a,c)=(a+c+sqrt((a+c)^2+4ac))/2,\n C_0=1,\n C_r=max_(0<=j<r)Omega(C_j,C_(r-j-1)).                   (6.2)\n\nWe prove that the maximum capacity over(6.1) is exactly C_r. Moreover\nevery capacity maximizer admits a recursive maximizing split at a single\nbridge index. This describes maximizers of(6.1), not every admissible\nvector and not every primitive certificate.\n\nFirst, maxima exist whenever the admissible set is nonempty. A singleton\ncondition is t_i(2+t_i)>=1, hence t_i>=c_*=sqrt(2)-1>0. Global equality\nimplies\n\n 1=A product_i t_i >= A t_j c_*^(r-1) >= t_j c_*^(r-1).\n\nThus c_*<=t_j<=c_*^(1-r). The conditions are closed on this compact\nbox. The capacity is continuous, so it attains a maximum.\n\nFix such a maximizing vector. An interval is called tight when K_I q_I=1.\nFor notational convenience set K_empty=q_empty=1. For each interval put\n\n F_I=log(K_I)+sum_(i in I)log(t_i),\n\nso F_I>=0, and tightness is F_I=0. Two tight intervals cannot overlap\nwithout one containing the other. Indeed, if their consecutive left-only,\ncommon, and right-only pieces have physical masses a>0,b>0,c>0, then\n\n F_I+F_J-F_(I union J)-F_(I intersection J)\n =log[(1+a+b)(1+b+c)/((1+b)(1+a+b+c))]>0,                (6.3)\n\nbecause the numerator minus denominator inside the logarithm is ac>0.\nIf I,J were both tight, their left side would be nonpositive by(6.1),\na contradiction. Likewise two adjacent disjoint nonempty tight intervals\nof masses a,c cannot occur: their union is an interval, and\n\n F_I+F_J-F_(I union J)=log[(1+a)(1+c)/(1+a+c)]>0.\n\nConsider now the inclusion-maximal PROPER tight intervals. The first\nobservation makes them disjoint; the second says any two are separated\nby at least one index outside them. Call an index uncovered when it is\nin none of these maximal proper intervals, equivalently in no proper\ntight interval at all.\n\nThere must be at least one uncovered index. Otherwise the proper maximal\nintervals would cover the word. A single one cannot cover it because it\nis proper, and multiple disjoint ones would then have an adjacent pair,\nwhich was just ruled out.\n\nThere cannot be two uncovered indices u,v. To prove this, perturb only\nt_u,t_v by t_u -> t_u+epsilon, t_v -> t_v-epsilon. Choose a sufficiently\nsmall nonzero epsilon with epsilon(t_v-t_u)<=0. Positivity persists,\nthe total sum and A remain unchanged, every proper tight interval is\nunchanged, and all other proper interval inequalities remain strict by\ncontinuity and finiteness. But\n\n (t_u+epsilon)(t_v-epsilon)\n     =t_u t_v+epsilon(t_v-t_u)-epsilon^2<t_u t_v.\n\nThe global product condition therefore becomes strictly less than1.\nStarting at this perturbed vector, increase t_u alone by a positive\namount delta until global equality is restored. Such a finite delta\nexists: the global product times A is a strictly increasing continuous\nfunction of t_u and tends to infinity as t_u tends to infinity. Every\nproper interval expression is nondecreasing under this increase, so all\nits inequalities remain valid. The resulting admissible vector has\ncapacity A+delta>A, contradicting maximality.\n\nThere is therefore exactly ONE uncovered index, say j. All indices to\nits left, if any, constitute a single maximal proper tight interval,\nbecause multiple maximal intervals would need another uncovered index\nbetween them. The same holds on its right. Consequently the left word\n[0,j) and the right word [j+1,r) are each either empty or saturated\nadmissible words. Let their capacities be a,c and let d=t_j>0. Their\nproducts are 1/a,1/c. The total capacity and saturation read\n\n                  A=a+c+d,       A d=ac.                (6.4)\n\nThus A=Omega(a,c). Induction on r gives a<=C_j and c<=C_(r-j-1).\nThe kernel is increasing in both arguments, so\n\n                  A<=Omega(C_j,C_(r-j-1))<=C_r.         (6.5)\n\nThis proves the upper bound for every admissible vector, because A was\na global maximizer. In an equality case strict monotonicity of Omega\nfor positive arguments forces the two children also to be maximizers,\nwhich proves the asserted recursive structure of maximizers.\n\nFor completeness the bound is attained within the scalar class. Join\ntwo saturated admissible child words of capacities a,c by h_bridge=1+d,\nwhere d>0 solves d(a+c+d)=ac. An interval inside a child is admissible.\nFor an interval crossing the bridge, its left suffix and right prefix\nhave capacities x<=a,y<=c, where an empty portion has capacity1.\nIts product is at least d/(xy), and its capacity is x+y+d. Because\n\n xy/(x+y+d) <= ac/(a+c+d)=d,\n\nthe interval is admissible. The displayed monotonicity follows directly\nfrom the partial derivatives y(y+d)/(x+y+d)^2 and x(x+d)/(x+y+d)^2,\nwhich are positive. Full saturation follows from d/(ac)=1/(a+c+d).\nStarting from empty children and using a maximizing split in(6.2) now\nconstructs a saturated admissible word of capacity C_r. These are also\nthe actual original primitive compositions supplied by the accepted\ncertificate join. No auxiliary continuous phase or different recurrence\nhas replaced the original all-split C recurrence.\n\n## 7. Strict capacity loss for the free positive-factor class at r=5,6\n\nSections5--6 already prove A<=C_r for every length. To obtain strictness\nat the two assigned lengths, suppose a positive-factor member had A=C_r.\nIt would maximize the scalar class(6.1), so Section6 supplies its unique\nuncovered bridge index j. But every proper prefix is strictly admissible\nby Section5. The left tight child [0,j) must therefore be empty, forcing\nj=0. Its capacity would satisfy\n\n                        A<=Omega(1,C_(r-1)).             (7.1)\n\nWe now give exact inequalities showing this is smaller than C_r for\nr=5,6. Let lambda=1+sqrt(2) and x=C_2. The balanced primitive scalar\nidentities in accepted04219554e41d9750, proof Section2, give\n\n C_3=lambda^2, C_4=Omega(lambda,x),\n C_5=lambda x, C_6=Omega(x,lambda^2),\n x^2-(1+lambda)x-lambda=0.\n\nThe rational bounds\n\n                 12/5<lambda<17/7,       4<x<81/20      (7.2)\n\nfollow as follows. The square-root comparisons use (7/5)^2<2<(10/7)^2.\nAt x=4 the polynomial is 12-5lambda<0. At x=81/20 and with lambda\nreplaced by its upper bound17/7, its value is247/2800>0. The polynomial\ndecreases as lambda increases, so these comparisons locate its positive root.\n\nFor C_4, at X=31/4 the defining polynomial for Omega(17/7,81/20) has\nvalue\n\n X^2-(17/7+81/20)X-(17/7)(81/20)=1/56>0.\n\nThus C_4<31/4. Meanwhile C_5>48/5. The increasing function\ng(X)=X(X-1)/(X+1), for X>sqrt(2)-1, satisfies\n\n g(48/5)=2064/265>31/4,\n\nwhere the last cross-multiplied difference is41>0. As\nOmega(1,c)<X is equivalent to c<g(X) for these positive quantities,\nwe obtain Omega(1,C_4)<C_5.\n\nFor C_6, (7.2) gives x>4 and lambda^2>29/5. At X=47/4 the polynomial\nfor Omega(4,29/5) is -23/80<0, so C_6>47/4. Also C_5<1377/140.\nNow\n\n g(47/4)=2021/204>1377/140,\n\nwith cross-multiplied difference2032>0. Hence Omega(1,C_5)<C_6.\nThese two strict comparisons contradict(7.1), proving\n\n             A<C_5 when r=5,    A<C_6 when r=6.          (7.3)\n\nNo assertion of a uniform positive gap over the whole factor family is\nmade. The proof applies to all finite positive factors, including cases\nwhere some successive U or D values coincide. Allowing arbitrary word\npatterns has not removed the capacity obstruction.\n\n## 8. Correct true-ray and actual physical-horizon comparisons\n\nSet m=r+1. The fixed requested rays remain b=1, but the obstruction\nholds on every fixed positive integer ray b. Specifically,\n\n                         Z_(mb)>=C_r Z_b.                (8.1)\n\nHere Z_b is the actual positive finite ray limit from accepted fact\n04219554e41d9750; this is a LOWER comparison, not an upper enclosure.\nTo prove it from the original recurrences, expand a maximizing B_n\ntree, replace every primitive empty leaf by a maximizing primitive\ntree of length r and capacity C_r, and replace its single dominant\nempty leaf by a maximizing dominant tree of length r and mass B_r.\nThe original recurrences give B_r>=C_r, as shown in proof Section3\nof that accepted fact. There are n+1 leaves in the original tree;\nthe resulting original tree has m(n+1)-1 steps. Homogeneity and\nmonotonicity of its Omega and G joins give\n\n                   B_(m(n+1)-1)>=C_r B_n.\n\nTaking n=b*2^j-1, dividing by lambda^j, and passing to the accepted\nray limits proves(8.1). Thus every member at r=5 or6 satisfies\n\n Z_6/(A Z_1)>=C_5/A>1,\n Z_7/(A Z_1)>=C_6/A>1,                                  (8.2)\n\nrespectively. More generally Z_(mb)/(A Z_b)>=C_r/A>1 for every b>=1.\nNeither a different integer ray nor a sharper sufficient upper threshold\ncan turn such a seed into the required strict improvement.\n\nThe actual physical substitution is also explicit. Take a fixed fully\nexpanded maximizing B_n tree. Replace only its n primitive empty leaves\nby a seed from the declared factor class, leaving its one dominant empty\nleaf physically empty, and recompute every physical bridge by the accepted\njoins. Denote the resulting word H_A and its mass D_A=1+2sum H_A.\nThe accepted arbitrary-length substitution in proof Section12 gives\n\n length(H_A)=mn,    W_(mn)(H_A)=1/D_A,\n (sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1))^2<=D_A<=A B_n.                (8.3)\n\nHere equality for W follows from the full all-function certificate and\nthe accepted actual Huber attaining example; it is not a claim restricted\nto a test family. In particular the original W=2R normalization is used.\n\nRepeat the same substitution using a maximizing basic primitive seed of\nthe same length r and capacity C=C_r. The resulting actual word is an\noriginal basic dominant tree of length mn with mass D_C. Therefore\nB_(mn)>=D_C. When r=5 or6, C>A, and strict monotonicity of the joins\ngives D_C>D_A for every n>=1. Consequently\n\n               W_(mn)(H_A)>U_(mn)^ZJ,  every n>=1.      (8.4)\n\nThe unchanged dominant leaf is paid for in the more quantitative bound\n\n B_(mn)W_(mn)(H_A)\n >=[sqrt(C B_n)-(sqrt(C)-1)]^2/(A B_n)\n >=(C/A)(1-B_n^(-1/2))^2.                               (8.5)\n\nFor each fixed seed let delta=C/A-1>0 and\n\n epsilon=1-sqrt((1+delta/2)/(1+delta))>0.\n\nSince B_n>=2n+1, every integer n>=1 satisfying\n2n+1>=epsilon^(-2) has the uniform deficit\n\n                W_(mn)(H_A)>=(1+delta/2)U_(mn)^ZJ.      (8.6)\n\nThis constant is seed-dependent; no common gap for all seeds is inferred.\nFor an input ray n=b*2^j-1 the ACTUAL horizon is\n\n                     mn=(r+1)(b*2^j-1),\n\nso b=1 gives6*2^j-6 or7*2^j-7. By accepted proof Section13 the exact\nlimit is Z_(mb)/(A Z_b), which by(8.1) is at least1+delta. No four-step\n193/25 threshold or j25 specialization has been transferred to these words.\n\n## 9. Boundary and deletion checks; exact remaining scope\n\nThe scalar argument handles empty left or right children with capacity1\nand product1. At r=1 the saturated condition forces t=sqrt(2)-1 and\nA=1+sqrt(2), consistent with C_1. It uses no asymptotic approximation.\nNonnegative, rather than strictly positive, entries of Lambda are fully\nincluded: the factors remain positive but their differences may vanish.\nThe strict proper-prefix condition comes from the positive future weighted\ntail, not from assuming every Lambda entry is positive.\n\nAs a concrete deletion check, global product balance by itself does NOT\ngive the scalar capacity theorem. At length2 set\n\n t_0=1/3, t_1=(-5+2sqrt(13))/3,\n A=(5+2sqrt(13))/3.\n\nThen A*t_0*t_1=1. Also A>81/20>C_2: the first inequality follows from\nsqrt(13)>143/40, whose squared comparison is20800>20449. But the first\nsingleton has K q=(7/3)(1/3)=7/9<1. This word is outside the hereditary\nclass and outside the positive-factor class. Thus none of the proof\npermits dropping its proper-interval constraints and retaining only the\nunit-quadratic terminal balance.\n\nThe hereditary scalar relaxation itself has basic maximizers, but no\nlossless reduction of the ORIGINAL primitive cone to this relaxation is\nproved or used. The source-inspired positive two-sequence array is an\nadditional algebraic restriction; signed factors, different triangular\nranks, factor sums, and nonzero PSD remainders require their own original\nsign/flow/space proofs. The nonzero-remainder four-step lane belongs to\nmax4 and has not been duplicated here.\n\nThis completes the requested whole-class obstruction for the genuinely\nenlarged free-word class at the draft level. It neither supplies an\nimproving primitive seed nor resolves the unrestricted exact-profile Goal.\nNo fact_submit is authorized by this allocation and none was called.\nThe original full theorem has not been verified. This mathematical\nhandoff is not an inspected worker-process exit or a resource renewal.\n\n## 10. Literature scope and local glossary\n\nThe factorization idea is from Salah R. Chikhi, *Last-Iterate Performance\nof Gradient Descent and Relaxed Proximal Point via s-Composability*,\narXiv:2609.13765v2, Section4, formulas(10)--(14),\nhttps://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13765v2. Its exact local primary text\ndownloads/2609.13765v2.txt, Section4 and proof, was reread this round.\nThe source proves a constant-step result. No source theorem about\nvariable steps, proximal objectives, or this capacity obstruction is\nimported. Sections1--9 here supply the new joint proofs in the original\nsmooth model. Matlas searches for both the free-word factorization and\nthe contiguous-product extremal statement returned unrelated theorems;\nnone was used. The new interval argument is a finite algebraic reduction\nof the audited factorization, not a separate external theorem assumption.\n\nLocal glossary: r is physical seed length; t_i=h_i-1 its positive excess;\ns and A are seed mass and capacity; U_i,D_j are positive factors in(1.3);\nmu is the auxiliary oriented array; Lambda=2mu^T is the original array;\np_k=U_(k-1)D_k; d_i is auxiliary outgoing minus incoming flow; P_k is\nprefix capacity; R_k is prefix reciprocal product; u_k,v_k are normalized\nweighted prefix and suffix sums; b_k=v_(k-1); H_I,K_I,q_I are interval\nmass, capacity, product; F_I=log(K_I q_I); c_*=sqrt(2)-1; Omega,C_n are\nthe original primitive kernel and capacities; G,B_n,Z_b,lambda,W_n and\nU_n^ZJ retain the accepted benchmark meanings. In the physical comparison\nm=r+1, H_A is the actual substituted schedule, D_A its dominant mass,\nC=C_r, delta=C/A-1 and epsilon is the explicit positive loss threshold\nin Section8. {"id": "ac0c1e866da0d926", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T20:21:35.915678+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max2 round38 COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED obstruction: arbitrary finite REAL two-sequence factors (zeros allowed), ALL nonnegative words, and full original M=0 reduce exactly to positive-factor words with isolated zero-step coordinates. No full-cone or original-Goal closure.", "evidence": "# Signed factors, zero steps, and the boundary of the two-sequence certificate\n\nStatus: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max2 round38,\nthe second and final analytical round allocated after completed36 by\nTASK.md and master_guidance3acdda5439874057. The completed ceiling is38;\nattempted ceiling39 includes the historical interruption. There is no\nrenewal, formal-verifier allowance, or original-target completion claim.\n\nThe original Goal remains OPEN. No numerical evaluation, solver,\nmachine algebra, replay, installation, consult, subordinate agent, or\nfact_submit was used. All mathematics below is a textual joint proof.\n\nThe new input class permits arbitrary FINITE REAL factors, including\nzeros, and EVERY nonnegative physical step. It imposes no alternating,\npalindromic, constant, or monotone word pattern. This removes both\npositivity restrictions of the earlier two-sequence program. The exact\noriginal entry signs, flow, and zero matrix remainder are retained.\n\nThe resulting obstruction is complete for that declared class. Signed\nfactors do not supply a new actual seed: after a common sign change and\nalgebraic isolation of zero-step coordinates, every nontrivial member is\na positive-factor free-word certificate. Its capacity is at most the\nbasic capacity for its number of positive steps, and is strictly less\nthan C_r at each assigned physical length r=5 or6. Every fixed integer\nray and every actual primitive-leaf substitution have the wrong direction\nfor the required improvement. Limits of this class still have A<=C_r.\n\nTwo further scope results are proved. First, EVERY original nonnegative\nzero-remainder certificate, without any factor restriction, excludes\nphysical steps in (0,1] and has identically zero multiplier rows and\ncolumns at zero steps. Second, a full explicit three-step certificate,\nand the accepted basic maximizing words at lengths5 and6, show that\nfinite signed two-sequence factorization is NOT lossless even inside\nthe zero-remainder cone. Two factor pairs suffice for the explicit\nthree-step array. No obstruction to arbitrary sums of factor pairs,\nto the entire zero-remainder cone, or to nonzero PSD remainders follows.\n\nAccepted inputs are fact04219554e41d9750, proof Sections2,10--13, for\nthe original scalar recurrences, the full certificate identity, physical\njoins and arbitrary-length substitution; and fact1252b8823bb5a290 for\nthe interpolation orientation. The unverified drafts0421d0c1066b8f2d\nandc1d41aaebcae37de are awareness only. Every new positive-factor\ningredient used here is rederived within this document, including the\nhereditary scalar extremal theorem. No unverified result is a premise.\n\nAll local auxiliary symbols have implicit suffix sf38. In particular\nU_i,D_i below are factors, not the benchmark U_n^ZJ or a dominant mass.\n\n## 1. Original coordinates and the enlarged declared class\n\nLet r>=1, h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1)) with h_i>=0, s=sum h_i, A=1+s,\nand t_i=h_i-1. Retain ALL r+1 independent formal gradients g_0,...,g_r\nand formal values f_0,...,f_r. At normalized smoothness1 write\n\n x_i=x_0-sum_(j<i)h_j g_j,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n Qbar_ij=-2Q_ji\n        =2(f_i-f_j)-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)\n       +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sA||g_r||^2.\n\nFor the ORIGINAL array Lambda, require Lambda_ij>=0 for every i!=j\nand Lambda_ii=0. Let e be the all-ones column of length r+1 and set\n\n T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,\n L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r, -s(s+2)).\n\nThe required exact flow is\n\n L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T.                  (1.1)\n\nThe original matrix is\n\n M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.                (1.2)\n\nThe full primitive cone allows M_prim>=0 on ALL R^(r+1). The class\nin this document imposes the additional equality M_prim=0. Accepted\nfact04219554e41d9750 supplies the polynomial identity\n\n V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij\n                  =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.\n\nConsequently, on writing mu_ij=Lambda_ji/2, zero remainder and flow\nare equivalent to\n\n                V_h=sum_(i!=j)mu_ij Qbar_ij,  mu>=0.    (1.3)\n\nFor the DECLARED TWO-SEQUENCE CLASS choose arbitrary finite real\nU_0,...,U_(r-1),D_1,...,D_r. Set U_-1=D_(r+1)=0 and set t_r=0\nonly as an auxiliary placeholder. For EVERY 0<=i<j<=r define\n\n mu_ij=(U_i-U_(i-1))(D_j+t_j D_(j+1)),\n mu_ji=(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)(D_j-D_(j+1)),\n Lambda_ij=2mu_ji, Lambda_ji=2mu_ij.                    (1.4)\n\nThere is no deleted off-diagonal pair. Membership requires ALL entries\nin(1.4) to be nonnegative and requires(1.3), equivalently(1.1) and\nM_prim=0. Factors are not assumed positive, nonzero, or monotone.\nIn particular, a change of internal factor signs is allowed only when\nthese actual ORIGINAL entry conditions remain true.\n\n## 2. A necessary lemma for the ENTIRE zero-remainder cone\n\nThis section assumes only(1.3), not(1.4). Define auxiliary net flow\nand the two incoming sums by\n\n d_i=sum_(j!=i)(mu_ij-mu_ji),\n I_i=sum_(j<i)mu_ji, J_i=sum_(j>i)mu_ji.\n\nThe function coefficients in(1.3) give d_i=h_i for i<r and d_r=-s.\nAt a nonterminal i the squared-difference terms have coefficient\n-d_i-2I_i-2J_i on ||g_i||^2. A trajectory term can contribute to\nthis diagonal only from Qbar_(j,i) with j>i; their total contribution\nis 2h_i J_i. Matching the diagonal of V_h therefore gives\n\n           t_i J_i-I_i=(1-t_i^2)/2,  0<=i<r.            (2.1)\n\nIf 0<h_i<=1, then -1<t_i<=0. The left side of(2.1) is nonpositive,\nbecause I_i,J_i>=0, while its right side is strictly positive. This\nis impossible. Thus every positive step is strictly greater than1.\n\nIf h_i=0, then t_i=-1 and(2.1) gives I_i+J_i=0. Nonnegativity makes\nEVERY incoming mu entry at i zero. Since d_i=h_i=0, every outgoing\nentry at i is also zero. The corresponding entire original Lambda row\nAND column vanish. This is an algebraic assertion on the full array;\nit does not impose equality between any two formal gradients or values.\n\nThere is also an exact balance restriction. Substitute the actual scalar\nunit quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1 into(1.3). Its gradients equal its\npositions, and every Qbar_ij is zero. Put\n\n v_0=1, v_(i+1)=(1-h_i)v_i=-t_i v_i.\n\nFor these samples the primitive energy is\n\n V_h=sum_(i<r)h_i(2-h_i)v_i^2-s(s+2)v_r^2\n     =sum_(i<r)(v_i^2-v_(i+1)^2)-s(s+2)v_r^2\n     =1-A^2 v_r^2.\n\nIt must vanish, so\n\n                 A product_(i<r)|t_i|=1.                (2.2)\n\nHere no factorization, irreducibility, or rank assumption has entered.\nFor positive steps t_i>0; zero steps have |t_i|=1. This necessary\nlemma applies to the full ZERO-remainder cone only. It does not extend\nto an arbitrary positive semidefinite remainder.\n\n## 3. Finite signed factors collapse to positive factors with zero padding\n\nAssume now(1.4) as well. If s=0, every step is zero; Section2 makes\nevery multiplier zero, and A=1. Hence suppose s>0.\n\nAt the terminal, the total incoming auxiliary weight telescopes to\n\n I_r=sum_(i<r)mu_ir=U_(r-1)D_r.\n\nThe terminal squared-gradient coefficient in sum mu Qbar is s-2I_r.\nMatching -sA gives\n\n             U_(r-1)D_r=s(s+2)/2>0.                    (3.1)\n\nChanging both factor sequences to their negatives preserves every\nentry in(1.4). Make this change if necessary so that D_r>0. Every\nterminal entry then gives\n\n mu_ir=(U_i-U_(i-1))D_r>=0.\n\nThus U_-1=0<=U_0<=...<=U_(r-1), with U_(r-1)>0.\n\nLet a be the first index with U_a>0. If i<a, both U_i and U_(i-1)\nvanish, so every entry incident to i in(1.4) is zero. Its flow gives\nh_i=0. At i=a the entry mu_ar=U_aD_r is strictly positive, so this\nrow cannot be a zero-step row by Section2. Hence h_a>1 and t_a>0.\nFor every j>a the actual entry\n\n          mu_ja=t_a U_a(D_j-D_(j+1))>=0\n\nforces D_j>=D_(j+1). Consequently\n\n D_(a+1)>=D_(a+2)>=...>=D_r>0,\n U_i>=U_a>0 for every i>=a.                             (3.2)\n\nAll ACTIVE factors are already positive. Arbitrary D entries preceding\nthis active suffix cannot affect the array, since all earlier U entries\nvanish.\n\nConsider a zero step at i>=a. It has every incident multiplier zero\nby Section2. Its terminal edge gives U_i=U_(i-1). Since i>a, the\nedge mu_ia=t_aU_a(D_i-D_(i+1)) gives D_i=D_(i+1). A zero step\ntherefore creates exact plateaus in BOTH active factor sequences.\n\nLet m be the number of positive steps. List their original indices as\n\n iota(0)<...<iota(m-1),  and set iota(m)=r.\n\nDefine the compressed word and factors by\n\n hhat_j=h_(iota(j)), Uhat_j=U_(iota(j)) for 0<=j<m,\n Dhat_j=D_(iota(j)) for 1<=j<=m,\n Uhat_-1=Dhat_(m+1)=0.\n\nAll Uhat and Dhat in their nonboundary ranges are strictly positive.\nThe U and D plateaus imply that, on every retained ordered pair, the\noriginal formula(1.4) is EXACTLY the same formula for hhat,Uhat,Dhat.\nFor example U_(iota(j)-1)=Uhat_(j-1), including the first-index zero,\nand D_(iota(j)+1)=Dhat_(j+1). The latter identity includes trailing\nzero steps and the retained original terminal r.\n\nEvery removed row and column was already identically zero. Also its\nstep is zero, so x_(iota(j)) is the GD position for the compressed word\nusing its retained formal gradients. Restricting the polynomial identity\nto the retained variables proves(1.3) for the compressed word. Conversely\nits identity extends to the full original space with zero rows at the\nunused independent coordinates. NO quotient relation between gradients\nat duplicated positions is used anywhere.\n\nThis proves the forward reduction. Its converse is explicit: starting\nfrom any positive-factor word, insert zero steps, copy the neighboring\nU and D values across each inserted zero index, put U=0 before the\nfirst active index, and choose the unused leading D entries arbitrarily.\nThen every inserted row and column is zero and all retained entries are\nunchanged. Thus the enlarged class is exactly the positive-factor\nfree-word class padded with arbitrary zero steps, apart from irrelevant\nfactor gauges and unused leading factor values.\n\n## 4. Joint positive-factor capacity proof: coefficient and prefix identities\n\nFor this section let the positive compressed length be q>=1, and drop\nhats. Thus h_i=1+t_i with t_i>0, U_i,D_j>0 in their nonboundary ranges,\nand(1.3)--(1.4) hold with r replaced by q and with U_-1=D_(q+1)=t_q=0.\nNonnegative entries imply\n\n                 U_i>=U_(i-1), D_j>=D_(j+1).             (4.1)\n\nThe first implication follows from the terminal entries. The second\nfollows from mu_(j,0)=t_0U_0(D_j-D_(j+1)). Conversely(4.1) makes\nall entries of(1.4) nonnegative when t,U,D are positive.\n\nFor i<j, the two telescoping sums are\n\n sum_(a<=i)mu_(a,j)=U_i(D_j+t_jD_(j+1)),\n sum_(a>j)mu_(a,i)=(U_(i-1)+t_iU_i)D_(j+1).\n\nThe coefficient of <g_i,g_j> in sum mu Qbar is\n\n 2(mu_ij+mu_ji)-2h_iU_i(D_j+t_jD_(j+1))\n                    +2h_j(U_(i-1)+t_iU_i)D_(j+1),\n\nwhere h_q=1 is an unused auxiliary value. Expanding(1.4) makes this\ncoefficient zero for every pair; at j=q its last term is zero.\nThis is a variable-step identity on the entire formal space.\n\nPut p_0=0 and p_k=U_(k-1)D_k for 1<=k<=q. Here\n\n I_i=U_(i-1)(D_i+t_iD_(i+1)),\n J_i=(U_(i-1)+t_iU_i)D_(i+1),\n\nwith I_0=0. Hence t_iJ_i-I_i=t_i^2p_(i+1)-p_i. Section2 and the\nterminal diagonal yield\n\n t_i^2(2p_(i+1)+1)=2p_i+1,\n 2p_q+1=A^2.                                          (4.2)\n\nDefine prefix capacity and reciprocal product by\n\n P_k=1+sum_(i<k)(1+t_i),\n R_k=product_(i<k)t_i^(-1), P_0=R_0=1.\n\nThen\n\n p_k=(R_k^2-1)/2>0 for 1<=k<=q,\n R_q=P_q=A.                                           (4.3)\n\nIn particular every nonempty prefix has R_k>1, even if some individual\nexcess t_i is greater than1.\n\nDefine the normalized weighted prefix and suffix sums\n\n u_k=(sum_(i<k)h_iU_i)/U_k,\n v_k=(sum_(j=k+2)^q h_(j-1)D_j)/D_(k+1), 0<=k<q.\n\nOutgoing cut weight across {0,...,k} is p_(k+1)(1+v_k):\nsum_(j>k)(D_j+t_jD_(j+1))=D_(k+1)+sum_(j>=k+2)h_(j-1)D_j.\nIncoming cut weight is p_(k+1)(t_k+u_k). The cut flow is therefore\n\n p_(k+1)(1-t_k+v_k-u_k)=P_(k+1)-1.                    (4.4)\n\nWe prove, for EVERY positive representation,\n\n u_k=(P_k-R_k)/(1+R_(k+1)),\n v_k=(P_(k+1)-R_(k+1))/(R_(k+1)-1).                    (4.5)\n\nAt k=0 the first formula is u_0=0. Inserting t_0=1/R_1 and\np_1=(R_1^2-1)/2 into(4.4) gives the second. For k<q-1 the defining\ntail sum is positive, so the formula implies P_(k+1)>R_(k+1).\n\nFor induction let 1<=k<q and abbreviate\nP=P_k,R=R_k,Y=R_(k+1),z=U_k/U_(k-1)>0. The preceding tail gives\nP>R>1. Recurrence and the p products give\n\n u_k=(u_(k-1)+h_(k-1))/z,\n v_(k-1)=[p_(k+1)/(p_k z)](h_k+v_k).\n\nThe induction hypothesis makes\n\n u_(k-1)+h_(k-1)=(P+R)/(1+R),\n v_(k-1)=(P-R)/(R-1).\n\nBy(4.4), v_k=u_k+delta_k, where\n\n delta_k=2(P+t_k)/(Y^2-1)-1+t_k,\n p_(k+1)(h_k+delta_k)=P+RY.\n\nElimination gives the quadratic\n\n (1+R)(P-R)z^2-2(P+RY)z\n                  -(Y^2-1)(P+R)/(1+R)=0.               (4.6)\n\nIts roots are\n\n (P+R)(1+Y)/[(1+R)(P-R)],  (1-Y)/(1+R).\n\nTo check them, the discriminant calculation reduces to\n(P+RY)^2+(P^2-R^2)(Y^2-1)=(PY+R)^2. The second root is negative,\nso the first is forced by z>0. It gives the asserted u formula.\nFor the v formula use t_kY=R and the identity\n\n (P-t_kY)(Y-1)+2(P+t_k)+(t_k-1)(Y^2-1)\n                      =(Y+1)(P+1+t_k-Y).\n\nThis completes the induction, including v_(q-1)=0. Thus\n\n                  1<R_k<P_k,  1<=k<q.                  (4.7)\n\nThe forced ratios, useful for auditing every original sign, are\n\n U_k/U_(k-1)=(P_k+R_k)(1+R_(k+1))\n                  /[(1+R_k)(P_k-R_k)],\n D_(k+1)/D_k=(P_k-R_k)(R_(k+1)-1)\n                  /[(R_k-1)(P_k+R_k)].                 (4.8)\n\nTogether with the positive denominators in(4.7), their exact sign\nconditions U_k>=U_(k-1),D_(k+1)<=D_k are equivalently\n\n t_k>=(P_k-R_k)/(P_k+R_k-2),\n t_k(P_k-R_k-2)<=P_k+R_k.                              (4.9)\n\nThe second inequality is deliberately not divided by its possibly\nnegative coefficient. No equality pattern between different steps has\nbeen assumed. One can independently choose t_0,...,t_(q-2)>0 and set\nthe last excess to the unique positive root of\nt^2+(P_(q-1)+1)t-R_(q-1)=0; this enforces only terminal balance.\nEquations(4.7),(4.9) remain necessary sign tests on that free word.\n\n## 5. Every contiguous positive-step interval is product-admissible\n\nFor a nonempty interval I=[l,k) in the compressed positive word put\n\n H_I=sum_(i=l)^(k-1)h_i, K_I=1+H_I,\n q_I=product_(i=l)^(k-1)t_i.\n\nWe prove\n\n                  K_I q_I>=1,                           (5.1)\n\nwith strict inequality whenever k<q, and equality for the whole word.\nFor l=0 this is P_k>=R_k, including(4.7).\n\nFor 1<=l<k<=q write P=P_l,R=R_l,H=H_I,z=q_I. Let\nb_j=(P_j-R_j)/(R_j-1)=v_(j-1), so b_j>=0 and b_q=0. Splitting\nthe actual weighted tail and using D monotonicity yields\n\n b_l=sum_(i=l)^(k-1)h_iD_(i+1)/D_l+(D_k/D_l)b_k\n                                             <=H+b_k.\n\nHere b_l+1=(P-1)/(R-1), b_k+1=z(P+H-1)/(R-z), and both denominators\nare positive because R_l,R_k>1. Multiplication and cancellation give\n\n                    z>=1-H(R-1)/(P-1).                  (5.2)\n\nThe endpoint inequality R_k<=P_k is R<=z(P+H). Inserting it into\nthe decreasing right side of(5.2) gives\n\n z[P-1+H(P+H)]>=P-1+H.\n\nSince P-1+H(P+H)=(H+1)(P-1+H), this proves z>=1/(1+H).\nIf k<q the endpoint inequality is strict, giving strictness in(5.1).\nThis is a theorem about the declared factors, not about every original\nzero-remainder array.\n\n## 6. Exact scalar extremum of the hereditary relaxation\n\nFor any length q>=1 consider positive t_i, h_i=1+t_i satisfying\n\n (1+sum_(i in I)h_i) product_(i in I)t_i>=1\n                         for EVERY nonempty contiguous I,\n A product_(i<q)t_i=1, A=1+sum_(i<q)h_i.                (6.1)\n\nThe empty word has capacity1. Define the ORIGINAL benchmark by\n\n Omega(a,c)=(a+c+sqrt((a+c)^2+4ac))/2,\n C_0=1, C_q=max_(0<=j<q)Omega(C_j,C_(q-j-1)).             (6.2)\n\nThe maximum capacity in(6.1) is exactly C_q. Moreover every maximizer\nhas a recursive maximizing split at one bridge index. The following\nargument proves the statement without importing an unverified premise.\n\nThe set is compact when nonempty. Its singleton conditions give\nt_i>=c_*=sqrt(2)-1>0. Global equality gives\n1=A product_i t_i>=t_j c_*^(q-1), so t_j<=c_*^(1-q). The constraints\nare closed on this bounded positive box, and A is continuous.\n\nFix a maximizer. Write F_I=log(1+sum_I h_i)+sum_I log t_i, and set\nF_empty=0. Call an interval tight when F_I=0. Two tight intervals\ncannot overlap without containment. Indeed, if their left-only, common,\nright-only pieces have masses a>0,b>0,c>0, then\n\n F_I+F_J-F_(I union J)-F_(I intersection J)\n  =log[(1+a+b)(1+b+c)/((1+b)(1+a+b+c))]>0,\n\nbecause the numerator minus denominator in the ratio is ac>0. Tightness\nof I,J and nonnegativity of the other two F values contradict this.\nNor can two nonempty tight intervals be adjacent: their union is an\ninterval, and\nF_I+F_J-F_(I union J)=log[(1+a)(1+c)/(1+a+c)]>0.\n\nThe inclusion-maximal proper tight intervals are therefore disjoint,\nand every two are separated by at least one uncovered index. At least\none index is uncovered: a single proper interval cannot cover the whole\nword, and multiple intervals covering it would include an adjacent pair.\n\nThere cannot be two uncovered indices u,v. Perturb t_u to t_u+epsilon\nand t_v to t_v-epsilon, with a sufficiently small nonzero epsilon of\nsign such that epsilon(t_v-t_u)<=0. Positivity is preserved. The total\ncapacity stays fixed, every proper tight interval is unchanged, and all\nother proper inequalities stay strict by continuity and finiteness.\nBut the changed two-factor product is\n\n t_u t_v+epsilon(t_v-t_u)-epsilon^2<t_u t_v.\n\nThe global expression A product t_i is now less than1. Increase t_u\nalone until this expression returns to1. Such an increase exists because\nthe expression is continuous, strictly increasing in t_u and unbounded.\nAll proper interval expressions are nondecreasing under this increase,\nso none becomes invalid. The resulting feasible word has strictly larger\ncapacity, a contradiction.\n\nThere is exactly one uncovered index j. All indices to its left form\none proper tight interval, if any; otherwise two maximal intervals on\nthat side would require another uncovered index between them. The same\nholds on the right. Thus the left and right children are saturated\nadmissible words (possibly empty). If their capacities are a,c and the\nbridge excess is d=t_j, global equality says\n\n                         A=a+c+d, Ad=ac.\n\nHence A=Omega(a,c). By induction and strict monotonicity of Omega,\nA<=Omega(C_j,C_(q-j-1))<=C_q. Equality forces both children to be\nmaximizers as well. This proves the asserted upper bound and structure.\n\nFor attainment, join saturated children of capacities a,c with the\npositive d solving d(a+c+d)=ac. Intervals inside a child are admissible.\nAn interval crossing the bridge has a left suffix of capacity x<=a and\na right prefix of capacity y<=c, where an empty portion has capacity1.\nIts product is at least d/(xy) and its capacity is x+y+d. Since\n\n xy/(x+y+d)<=ac/(a+c+d)=d,\n\nit is admissible. Monotonicity in x,y follows from the positive derivatives\ny(y+d)/(x+y+d)^2 and x(x+d)/(x+y+d)^2. The whole joined word has\nproduct d/(ac)=1/(a+c+d). Starting with empty children and a maximizing\nsplit in(6.2) constructs capacity C_q. These are the accepted actual\nprimitive joins as well as feasible points of the scalar relaxation.\n\n## 7. Strictness at the assigned lengths\n\nSections5--6 give A<=C_q for every positive-factor compressed length q.\nIf A=C_q, the maximizing structure in Section6 supplies its unique\nuncovered index j. A nonempty left child would be a tight proper prefix,\ncontrary to the strictness in Section5. Thus j=0 and\n\n                         A<=Omega(1,C_(q-1)).            (7.1)\n\nWe show this is strictly smaller than C_q at q=5,6 by exact comparisons.\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2), x=C_2. The balanced scalar identities in accepted\nfact04219554e41d9750, Section2, give\n\n C_3=lambda^2, C_4=Omega(lambda,x),\n C_5=lambda x, C_6=Omega(x,lambda^2),\n x^2-(1+lambda)x-lambda=0.\n\nWe have 12/5<lambda<17/7 and 4<x<81/20. The lambda comparisons use\n(7/5)^2<2<(10/7)^2. The polynomial for x at4 is12-5lambda<0;\nat81/20 with lambda replaced by17/7 it is247/2800>0. Since this\npolynomial decreases with lambda, these isolate its positive root.\n\nAt X=31/4 the polynomial defining Omega(17/7,81/20) has value1/56>0,\nso C_4<31/4. Also C_5>48/5. Put g(X)=X(X-1)/(X+1); its derivative\nis positive for X>sqrt(2)-1. Direct comparisons give\n\n g(48/5)=2064/265>31/4,\n 4*2064-31*265=41>0.\n\nAs Omega(1,c)<X is equivalent to c<g(X) for the positive quantities\nhere, Omega(1,C_4)<C_5.\n\nFor q=6, x>4 and lambda^2=3+2sqrt(2)>29/5. At X=47/4 the polynomial for\nOmega(4,29/5) is -23/80<0, so C_6>47/4. Meanwhile C_5<1377/140,\nand\n\n g(47/4)=2021/204>1377/140,\n 2021*140-1377*204=2032>0.\n\nThus Omega(1,C_5)<C_6. Equation(7.1) is impossible at either assigned\nlength, and every positive-factor member has A<C_5 or A<C_6.\n\nReturn to the original signed-factor word of physical length r=5 or6.\nIf all steps are zero, A=1<C_r. Otherwise let m be its number of\npositive steps. Section3 gives A<=C_m. If m=r, the preceding argument\ngives A<C_r. If m<r, the original scalar recurrence is strictly increasing:\nC_(k+1)>=Omega(1,C_k)>C_k. Again A<=C_m<C_r. Consequently\n\n       EVERY finite real two-sequence member at r=5,6 has A<C_r. (7.2)\n\nAt an arbitrary physical length r the same reasoning gives A<=C_m<=C_r.\nIn particular any coordinatewise limit of words in the declared class,\neven with unbounded or differently gauged factors, has A<=C_r by continuity\nof the finite sum. Strictness need not persist in that closure. If arrays\nalso converge, their entry signs, flow and full zero matrix identity\npersist by continuity; no bounded-factor representation of the limiting\narray has been asserted.\n\n## 8. True lower ray comparisons and the actual physical deficit\n\nFor completeness define the original dominant recurrence\n\n G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2,\n B_0=1, B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nUse W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over the\nentire original smooth convex class, all finite dimensions and x_0!=x_star,\nwith updates x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R is unchanged.\nAccepted fact04219554e41d9750 proves the positive finite ray limits\n\n Z_b=lim_(j->infinity)lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1), b>=1 integer,\n\nand the same limits for every fixed nonnegative terminal offset.\n\nThe comparison needed for exclusion is the TRUE LOWER comparison\n\n                   Z_((r+1)b)>=C_r Z_b, every b>=1.     (8.1)\n\nTo prove it, take a fully expanded maximizing B_n tree. Replace each\nprimitive empty leaf by a maximizing primitive tree of length r and\ncapacity C_r, and its one dominant empty leaf by a maximizing dominant\ntree of length r and mass B_r. The accepted elementary comparison\nB_r>=C_r, together with homogeneity and monotonicity of Omega and G,\ngives an original tree with (r+1)(n+1)-1 steps and mass at least C_r B_n.\nThe original all-split recurrence therefore gives\n\n                  B_((r+1)(n+1)-1)>=C_r B_n.\n\nPutting n=b*2^j-1 and taking the accepted limits proves(8.1). It is\nnot a comparison to a sufficient upper enclosure.\n\nFor a fixed actual signed-factor seed of physical length r=5 or6 put\nC=C_r and delta=C/A-1>0. Section7 and(8.1) show, for EVERY integer b>=1,\n\n                   Z_((r+1)b)/(A Z_b)>=1+delta>1.        (8.2)\n\nThe initially requested rays remain b=1, with ratios Z_6/Z_1 and Z_7/Z_1.\nNo switch of ray can repair this seed class.\n\nThe actual substitution keeps the original physical zero steps, if any.\nLet H_A be obtained by replacing only the n primitive empty leaves of a\nfixed fully expanded maximizing B_n tree by this seed and recomputing\nthe accepted bridges. Its ONE dominant empty leaf stays physically empty.\nWrite D_A=1+2sum H_A. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750, Sections12--13,\ngives\n\n length(H_A)=(r+1)n, W_((r+1)n)(H_A)=1/D_A,\n [sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1)]^2<=D_A<=A B_n.                (8.3)\n\nThis equality is for the unrestricted all-function W: the accepted\nfull certificate supplies the upper bound and its actual one-dimensional\nHuber trajectory attains it. Zero steps do not alter that argument and\ndo not remove their physical time indices.\n\nPerform the same substitution using a basic primitive word of the SAME\nphysical length r and capacity C. This yields an original basic dominant\ntree of length (r+1)n, with mass D_C, so B_((r+1)n)>=D_C. Strict\nmonotonicity of the joins and C>A imply D_C>D_A whenever n>=1. Hence\n\n           W_((r+1)n)(H_A)>U_((r+1)n)^ZJ, every n>=1.   (8.4)\n\nFor a quantitative fixed-seed deficit the untouched dominant leaf is paid\nusing the actual lower bound in(8.3) applied at capacity C:\n\n B_((r+1)n)W_((r+1)n)(H_A)\n >=[sqrt(C B_n)-(sqrt(C)-1)]^2/(A B_n)\n >=(C/A)(1-B_n^(-1/2))^2.                              (8.5)\n\nSet epsilon=1-sqrt((1+delta/2)/(1+delta))>0. Since the accepted benchmark\nhas B_n>=2n+1, every n>=1 with 2n+1>=epsilon^(-2) satisfies\n\n             W_((r+1)n)(H_A)>=(1+delta/2)U_((r+1)n)^ZJ. (8.6)\n\nThe deficit is seed-dependent. No positive uniform gap across all finite\nfactorized seeds or their closure is claimed. For a limiting actual\ncertificate from the closure, A<=C_r gives non-strict physical domination\nand a limiting ratio at least1, which already excludes strict improvement.\n\nOn an input ray n=b*2^j-1, the ACTUAL output horizon is\n\n                 (r+1)(b*2^j-1).\n\nFor b=1 these are6*2^j-6 and7*2^j-7. The accepted substitution gives\nthe exact limit Z_((r+1)b)/(A Z_b). Neither the four-step193/25 threshold\nnor its j25 specialization has been transferred to the present lengths.\n\n## 9. Exact counterexample to losslessness of finite signed factorization\n\nThis section gives a scope check, not an improving seed. Set\n\n w=sqrt(2), lambda=1+w, kappa=lambda^2, h=(w,2,w).\n\nIts physical length is3 and its capacity is C_3=kappa. In the original\nordered indexing 0,1,2,3 define the COMPLETE array\n\n Lambda =\n [ 0       2                0                 0                 ]\n [ 2lambda 0                0                 kappa-1           ]\n [ 0       (kappa-1)w        0                 2kappa+(kappa-1)w  ]\n [ 0       kappa-1           2kappa*lambda      0                 ]. (9.1)\n\nAll entries are nonnegative and every diagonal entry is zero. The\noriginal row sums minus column sums are\n\n                    (-2w,-4,-2w,4lambda),              (9.2)\n\nas required because s=2lambda. To check the entries without decimal\nevaluation use kappa=2lambda+1 and lambda*w=lambda+1.\nAt index0 the difference is2-2lambda=-2w. At index1 it is\n4lambda-(4lambda+4)=-4. At index2 it is\n(8lambda+6)-(10lambda+4)=-2w. The last difference is4lambda.\n\nThe complete zero-remainder identity follows from the accepted primitive\njoin, and can be specified entirely in the original residuals. A single\nstep w has\n\n                  V_(w)=-2Q_01-2lambda Q_10.\n\nThis identity follows by expanding the two displayed residuals, using\n(w-1)lambda=1. Join two such children with bridge2. Their capacities\nare lambda, and d=1 solves d(2lambda+d)=lambda^2. The accepted exact\njoin identity is\n\n V_h=V_left+kappa V_right-(kappa-1)\n                    [wQ_21+Q_31+Q_13+wQ_23].           (9.3)\n\nInsert V_left=-2Q_01-2lambda Q_10 and\nV_right=-2Q_23-2lambda Q_32. The coefficient of every original ordered\nQ is exactly the negative of(9.1); all omitted coefficients are zero.\nThus V_h+sum Lambda Q=0 as an identity in ALL four formal gradients\nand values. In particular M_prim=0 on all R^4, not merely on actual\ntrajectories or a selected subspace.\n\nThe array(9.1) cannot have ANY finite real two-sequence representation.\nIf it did, Section3, since every physical step is positive, would give\nstrictly positive U_0 and D_3 after one common sign change. But\nmu_03=U_0D_3>0 would imply Lambda_30>0, whereas(9.1) has Lambda_30=0.\n\nIn fact the same WORD has no such representation with ANY other choice\nof the original multipliers. Its first proper prefix has\n\n        (1+h_0)(h_0-1)=(1+w)(w-1)=1.\n\nSection5 forces every proper positive-step prefix to have product strictly\ngreater than1. This is a contradiction independent of the chosen array.\n\nHere is an explicit two-pair representation of the full array, showing\nwhere a larger factor class begins. Use the SAME t=(w-1,1,w-1) for two\ncopies of formula(1.4), with\n\n U^(1)=(1,0,0),          D^(1)=(lambda,0,0),\n U^(2)=(0,1,kappa+1),    D^(2)=(0,lambda,lambda).\n\nSet mu=mu^(1)+mu^(2). The only nonzero entries of the first component\nare mu^(1)_01=lambda and mu^(1)_10=1. The only nonzero entries of the\nsecond are\n\n mu^(2)_12=lambda*w, mu^(2)_13=lambda, mu^(2)_31=lambda,\n mu^(2)_23=kappa*lambda, mu^(2)_32=lambda+kappa+1.\n\nAll are positive. Substitution into(1.4) gives precisely these entries:\nfor example (U^(2)_2-U^(2)_1)D^(2)_3=kappa*lambda and\n(U^(2)_1+(w-1)U^(2)_2)D^(2)_3=lambda+kappa+1.\nSince lambda*w=lambda+1, Lambda=2mu^T is exactly(9.1).\nNeither component separately satisfies the full h flow and diagonal\nmatching. Therefore the obstruction for individual factorized certificates\ndoes not apply to their sum. This is not a mixture of two feasible\nfull-word certificates.\n\nThere are also counterexamples to losslessness at BOTH assigned lengths.\nTake an original maximizing primitive tree of length5 or6. Its leaves\nhave zero remainder; the accepted primitive join preserves zero remainder\nand gives a full nonnegative original array with capacity C_5 or C_6.\nEvery step is positive. Equation(7.2) prohibits any finite signed\ntwo-sequence representation of a certificate for such a word. These\nbasic words are not strict-threshold seeds; they witness the genuine\nrestriction of the factorization inside the original zero-remainder cone.\n\nNo statement here excludes a limiting factorization with divergent factors\nor claims that two pairs span the whole zero-remainder cone. Frozen script\n  SHA531bf1eb87f4b10854d4f599f15647f32cee357d2e3447b45c7ea388aec939f3.\n  Output: research/frozen_structure_20261001/\n  job_03_max46_proximal_memory/raw_output.txt.\n* Total charged so far:3/4 jobs,0.30353681599808624s. No solver,\n  new trajectory, benchmark recurrence, all-Q/PSD replay, machine-algebra\n  job, or formal verification was run.\n\nJob2 used ORIGINAL Gram/value arrays, not their mass-scaled variants.\nFor the zero optimum, the conjugate height\n\n    phi_i=<x_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2=-Q_(i,star)\n\nwas read from stored residuals. It is distinct from the primal height\ndelta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. In saved H6 the former is around10^-9 to10^-8,\nwhereas delta_32 is about0.30018. Job3 evaluated proximal-vector norms\nas linear forms of the saved formal-vector Gram. At the saved H6 indices\n7,15,23,31,39,47 these norms squared are small, while at55 and63 they are\nabout0.88505 and0.32327. No exact zero follows from these numbers.\nEndpoint gradient norms both increase and decrease. Chronological\ntwo-future conditional residuals still have remote covariance; e.g. the\nresidual at3 has normalized covariance about0.44025 with16. Such\nregressions can be ill-conditioned and are not rank or independence proofs.\n\nThese observations motivate, but do not establish, the exact hypotheses:\nconjugate saturation, selected returns to x=g, nonmonotone reset-state\nnorms, and persistent correlations across distant cells. They do not\nmotivate a terminal reset. The candidate below permits an arbitrary\nterminal tail after its tested prefix.\n\nThe new dependence law is the explicit scale-indexed kernel\n\n    <v_i,v_j>=min(t_i,t_j),       ||v_i||^2=t_i.\n\nIt is nested by norm, not chronological order. Norms may repeatedly rise\nand fall, so earlier coordinates can be reactivated and used arbitrarily\noften. There is no fixed bound on physical dimension or coordinate jump\ncount. Signed intermediate gradients will be negative combinations of\ntwo reset states. This is not a one-future-gradient law, a capped signed\nH2 innovation law, or the old power profile for nested dual normals.\n\nOne restriction is deliberately retained: each H2 half-cell has constant\ngradient. The frozen H5/H6 data have appreciable changes INSIDE some H2\ncells. Those changes are not reproduced by this first template; they are\none explicit remaining interface. Another is the exact min-kernel itself:\nthe frozen data do not prove it, and it is not a lossless Gram model.\n\n## 2. Finite Euclidean realization of the new dependence law\n\nFor any finite nonnegative list t_i, sort its distinct positive values as\n0<tau_1<...<tau_d and put tau_0=0. With orthonormal vectors e_1,...,e_d,\ndefine\n\n    v_i=sum_(ell:tau_ell<=t_i) sqrt(tau_ell-tau_(ell-1))*e_ell.\n\nThen the common coordinates of v_i and v_j are precisely those with\ntau_ell<=min(t_i,t_j). Their inner product telescopes to min(t_i,t_j),\nand ||v_i||^2=t_i. Thus this is an explicit finite-dimensional generating\nrule, not a PSD oracle. Equal t_i give identical vectors; increasing and\ndecreasing t_i turn coordinates on and off according to scale. Nothing\nhere establishes smooth interpolation of a prescribed GD transcript.\n\n## 3. Original H3 cells, values, and intended static function\n\nSet\n\n    s=sum H_2=3+2a+beta,\n    A=s+1=4+2a+beta, b=beta*c, D=A+b,\n    E=A^2-2Ab-b^2, K=A^2(2A+b),\n    B_lo=beta*c^2, B_hi=beta*c^3.\n\nHere B_lo and B_hi are bridge EXCESSES, not smoothness constants or recursive\nwords. Smoothness is normalized to1. In an H3 cell, the first H2 is\nfollowed by the physical step1+b and then the second H2. Its seven\nsteps sum to2s+1+b.\n\nAt cell j, physical indices8j,...,8j+7, prescribe u_j as gradient at\n8j,...,8j+3 and v_j at8j+4,...,8j+7. Prescribe the reset x_(8j+7)=v_j.\nThe second H2 then starts at A*v_j. At its preceding central query,\n\n    x_(8j+3)=(1+b)u_j+A*v_j,\n    y_(8j+3):=x_(8j+3)-u_j=b*u_j+A*v_j,\n    x_(8j)=D*u_j+A*v_j.\n\nFor j>=1, the original physical bridge8j-1 has excess\n\n    B_j=beta*c^(2+nu_2(j)),\n\nwhere nu_2(j) is the exponent of2 dividing j. Since the preceding reset\nis v_(j-1), the actual GD update gives x_(8j)=-B_j*v_(j-1). Consequently\n\n    u_j=-(B_j*v_(j-1)+A*v_j)/D.                 (1)\n\nAll positions inside each H2 are retained, via their actual partial sums;\nno query is deleted. At every prescribed sample impose the conjugate\nsaturation value\n\n    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.                  (2)\n\nIn particular a reset has value||v_j||^2/2. Include the optimum sample\nx_star=g_star=f_star=0. For the ORIGINAL ordered residual convention of\naccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290, direct expansion of(2) gives\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>.                    (3)\n\nIf every normal inequality(3) held, a natural intended single function\nwould be F(x)=max_(g in C)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2), where C is the convex hull\nof0 and all supplied gradients. The inequalities say that g_i is the\nEuclidean projection of x_i onto C, and hence give the values(2).\nThis intended construction is NOT asserted legal: the next sections\nprove that an original row fails. No minimizer or radius is inferred from\nthe intended hull before interpolation is checked.\n\n## 4. Local transition calculation and first explicit law\n\nConsider one cell after a bridge of excess B>0. Write p=v_(j-1),v=v_j,\nP=||p||^2>0,T=||v||^2, and use <p,v>=min(P,T). At the central query,\n\n    u=-(B*p+A*v)/D,\n    y=(-bB*p+A^2*v)/D.\n\nThe retained row with source gradient v and target gradient u is\n\n    D^2*Q_(v,u)=K*T+E*B*min(P,T)-bB^2*P.       (4)\n\nTo derive(4), multiply\n(v-u)=[B*p+(2A+b)*v]/D by y. The mixed coefficient is\nB[A^2-b(2A+b)]=B*E, and the other two coefficients are-bB^2 and K.\n\nFor a shrinking endpoint T<=P, this row requires\n\n    T/P<=alpha(B):=bB^2/(K+EB),                (5)\n\nwhenever the denominator is positive. For a growing endpoint T>=P it\nrequires\n\n    T/P<=gamma(B):=B(bB-E)/K.                  (6)\n\nThe factorization\n\n    K*(gamma(B)-1)=(bB-A^2)(B+2A+b)           (7)\n\nshows where the two regimes meet. At B=A^2/b both bounds equal1.\nThe first explicit extrapolation law selected in plan6e9c5cb11fc77d32\nwas to take equality in(5) below this threshold and equality in(6)\nabove it, using the exact B_j and fixed enclosing-m parameters in every\ncell. This produces nonmonotone reset norms and persistent state.\n\nThe following obstruction is STRONGER: it allows arbitrary reset norms\nsubject only to the local inequalities, not this maximal-transition law.\nThus lowering a selected factor or independently retuning all endpoint\nnorms cannot repair the entire declared min-kernel family.\n\n## 5. Uniform parameter bounds on the actual words\n\nFor every integer m>=100, put epsilon=1/m. The elementary identities\nlambda^2=2lambda+1 and lambda^3=2lambda^2+lambda will be used, together\nwith12/5<lambda<5/2. Put\n\n    d=lambda^2-A=epsilon+4epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)].\n\nThen0<d<(8/3)epsilon<=2/75. Direct substitution gives\n\n    (99/100)lambda^2 < A < lambda^2,\n    (99/100)lambda < b=lambda(1-epsilon^2) < lambda,\n    lambda^2 < B_lo=lambda^2(1+epsilon-epsilon^2-epsilon^3)\n                 <(101/100)lambda^2,\n    lambda^3 < B_hi=lambda^3(1+2epsilon-2epsilon^3-epsilon^4)\n                 <(102/100)lambda^3.           (8)\n\nNon-strict versions of these bounds also suffice below. To see E<0,\nfactor E=(A-lambda*b)(A+b/lambda). Since d>=epsilon and\nlambda^2*epsilon^2<epsilon, one has A<lambda*b. Moreover\n\n    0<-E=(lambda*b-A)(A+b/lambda)\n         <d(lambda^2+1)\n         <(8/3)(1/100)(25/4+1)=29/150<1/5.    (9)\n\nFrom(8) and2lambda^2+lambda=lambda^3,\n\n    (97/100)lambda^7<K<lambda^7.              (10)\n\nIndeed(99/100)^3>97/100. For the low bridge,\nA^2>(99/100)^2 lambda^4 while b*B_lo<(101/100)lambda^3; since\n(99/100)^2 lambda>101/100, we have b*B_lo<A^2. For the high bridge,\n\n    b*B_hi=lambda^4[(1+epsilon)(1-epsilon^2)]^2\n        >lambda^4>A^2,                        (11)\n\nbecause epsilon-epsilon^2-epsilon^3>0.\n\nConsequently define\n\n    alpha=alpha(B_lo), gamma=gamma(B_hi).\n\nTheir denominators are positive. In detail,\n\n    K+E*B_lo>(97/100)lambda^7-(101/500)lambda^2\n           >(96/100)lambda^7,\n\nwhere the last comparison uses lambda^5>101/5. Thus\n\n    0<alpha<(11/10)lambda^(-2).               (12)\n\nFor gamma, the numerator B_hi(b*B_hi-E) is at most\n(1.0404)lambda^7+(0.204)lambda^3<(1.05)lambda^7, since\nlambda^4>85/4. Dividing by(10) gives gamma<11/10. Equation(11)\nand(7) give gamma>1. Therefore\n\n    1<gamma<11/10, alpha*gamma<121/(100lambda^2)<1.  (13)\n\nThe decimal constants in this paragraph are terminating rational numbers,\nnot numerical evaluations:1.0404=2601/2500,0.204=51/250,\n1.05=21/20. No machine arithmetic is used in these inequalities.\n\n## 6. Every nonzero profile fails a retained remote row\n\nThe obstruction uses only the original cells j=2 and j=3, preceded by\nthe reset at15. Their entering bridge excesses are\n\n    h_15-1=B_hi, h_23-1=B_lo.\n\nTheir central bridge excess is b, at physical indices19 and27. Put\n\n    t1=||v_1||^2, t2=||v_2||^2, t3=||v_3||^2.\n\nAssume t1,t2>0 and that the local original rows Q_(20,19)<=0 and\nQ_(28,27)<=0 hold. In the high cell, if t2<t1 its ratio is below1;\nif t2>=t1, equation(6) applies. In either case\n\n    t2/t1<=gamma<11/10.                       (14)\n\nIn the low cell, growth would contradict gamma(B_lo)<1, by(7) and b*B_lo<A^2.\nThus t3<t2, and(5) gives\n\n    0<=t3/t2<=alpha<(11/10)lambda^(-2).        (15)\n\nEquations(13)--(15) imply t3<t1. Set mu=min(t1,t2)>0. Then\n\n    1<=t2/mu<11/10,\n    <v_1,v_2>=mu,\n    <v_1,v_3>=<v_2,v_3>=t3.                  (16)\n\nThe source gradient at16 and target gradient at27 are\n\n    g_16=u_2=-(B_hi*v_1+A*v_2)/D,\n    g_27=u_3=-(B_lo*v_2+A*v_3)/D,\n    y_27=(-b*B_lo*v_2+A^2*v_3)/D.\n\nUsing(3) and(16), the ACTUAL ordered interpolation row is\n\n    D^2*Q_(16,27)\n      =b*B_lo*B_hi*mu+b*B_lo(A-B_lo)*t2\n         +[A^2(B_lo-B_hi)-A*b*B_lo]*t3.              (17)\n\nThis row is retained in the original full objective problem. Source16\nand target27 are both queried points, not an unqueried terminal pair.\n\nWe now bound(17) without choosing any norm profile. From(8),\nb*B_lo*B_hi>(99/100)lambda^6. Also0<B_lo-A<(2/100)lambda^2. By(14)--(16),\n\n    |b*B_lo(A-B_lo)*t2|/mu\n       <(1.01)(0.02)(1.1)lambda^5\n       <(1/100)lambda^6.                     (18)\n\nThe last coefficient in(17) is negative. Its magnitude, divided by mu,\nis less than\n\n    [A^2*B_hi+A*b*B_lo]*(t3/t2)*(t2/mu)\n       <(1.21)[(1.02)lambda^5+(1.01)lambda^3]\n       <(61/100)lambda^6.                    (19)\n\nFor the last strict inequality, divide by lambda^6 and use lambda>12/5:\n\n    (1.21)[1.02/(12/5)+1.01/(12/5)^3]\n       = (12342/10000)(5/12)\n          +(12221/10000)(125/1728)\n       <61/100.\n\nThis is a displayed rational comparison. Combining(17)--(19) gives\n\n    D^2*Q_(16,27)>(37/100)lambda^6*mu\n                           >(lambda^6/3)*mu.\n\nFinally D<lambda^2+lambda and(lambda^2+lambda)^2=2lambda^4. Hence\n\n    Q_(16,27)>(lambda^2/6)*mu>(9/10)*mu>0.    (20)\n\nThis contradicts the necessary interpolation inequality from accepted\nfact1252b8823bb5a290. The conclusion is joint: either an earlier local\nrow fails, or this explicit remote row fails. PSD of the min-kernel Gram,\nall GD updates, and passing the displayed local rows cannot substitute\nfor the missing global interpolation condition.\n\nThe proof allows EVERY positive scale t1,t2 and every t3 admitted by the\nlocal rows. It does not assume equality in(5)--(6), a constant scale ratio,\nfixed rank, a chronological ordering of the t_i, or any rule outside\nthese cells. Embedding the same vectors into a larger Euclidean space\npreserves the offending row. Adding extra gradients to the intended hull\ncannot remove an existing pair inequality.\n\n## 7. Zero states, normalization, and exact scope of the obstruction\n\nIf t1=0, the prescribed reset has x_15=g_15=0. In any hypothetical smooth\nconvex interpolant with the zero optimum sample, every later GD iterate\nwould remain at that minimum. If t2=0, the same conclusion starts at23.\nIf t1,t2>0 and t3=0, the argument above still applies, with the last\nterm in(17) zero, so there is no exceptional repair at t3=0.\n\nTherefore, for every actual H_m with m>=100, any LEGAL member retaining\nthese two constant-half H3 cells, the prescribed resets, saturated values,\nand min-kernel reset correlations has zero terminal objective gap. There\nis no positive-gap member, whatever earlier history or later tail is\nchosen. About any true nearest minimizer with a nonzero initial distance,\nits normalized payoff is0. This statement does not assert existence of\na nontrivial member or a particular initial radius.\n\nFor comparison on the same word, the complete accepted K/ray references\ninclude the positive Huber value1/(1+2sum h), by facts b98bb807d514e80d\nand1fca3e5dd555cb4a. Thus every legal member of the declared template has\npayoff0, strictly below both references, for this unbounded actual-word\nfamily. This also lies strictly below the UNaugmented FULL complex\nreference C_full(h)=sup_(z in D_h) Gap_h(z) of accepted fact\ndfde2fa326c908bf. Indeed put S=sum h and theta=1/[2(1+S)]. The real\nquadratic F(x)=theta*x^2/2 has the exact real-parameter polynomial\ntranscript, so theta belongs to D_h and maximality of Gap_h gives\nC_full(h)>=theta*product_(i<n)(1-theta*h_i)^2. Each theta*h_i is\nnonnegative and their sum is below1/2. Induction using\n(1-u)(1-v)>=1-u-v shows that the product is greater than1/2. Hence\nC_full(h)>1/[8(1+S)]>0. This lower comparison uses the complete channel,\nnot a selected universal lens or an added Huber term.\n\nThe original all-split benchmark B_n is positive by its recurrence,\nso its product with such a payoff is exactly0. This is a template\nobstruction, NOT an upper bound on unrestricted W_n(H_m), and not an\nasymptotic conclusion for the accepted finite H6 witness.\n\nThe hypotheses concerning values matter. The positive row(20) by itself\nis NOT a value-independent cycle obstruction. Section8 derives a precise\nnecessary height cost when these values are relaxed. Nor does this result\nclose arbitrary kernels with the same three endpoint norms, nonradial returns, or changing\ngradients inside the two H2 halves. Orthogonal addition of a separate\ntranscript can supply negative residuals and is not excluded by the\nembedding statement above. Its value/radius cost would require a new\njoint proof; no bound on all such sums is claimed.\n\n## 8. Precise remaining interface and handoff\n\nFirst retain the same min-kernel geometry and prescribed resets, but allow\narbitrary values belonging to one hypothetical legal smooth interpolant.\nDefine phi_i=<x_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2. The optimum row Q_(i,star)<=0\ngives phi_i>=0. At a reset x_i=g_i, the two optimum rows give both\nf_i<=||g_i||^2/2 and f_i>=||g_i||^2/2, so phi_i=0.\n\nIf two queried gradients are equal, their two interpolation inequalities\ngive f_i-f_j=<g_i,x_i-x_j>. Therefore phi is constant along each constant\nH2 half. In particular phi_20=phi_23=0 and phi_28=phi_31=0. For any\nvalues the exact identity is\n\n    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>+phi_j-phi_i.            (21)\n\nThe local rows Q_(20,19) and Q_(28,27) are their geometric versions(4)\nPLUS nonnegative phi_19 and phi_27. Thus their feasibility still implies\nthe same norm bounds(14)--(16). The geometric part of Q_(16,27) still\nsatisfies(20). Consequently every such value repair necessarily pays\n\n    phi_16-phi_27>(9/10)*min(t1,t2).
