Reading files,\noperational hashing and persistence do not evaluate new benchmark data.\n\n## Definitions and accepted interfaces\n\nFor a nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over finite-dimensional convex\ndifferentiable L-smooth functions with a minimizer x_star and x_0!=x_star,\nunder x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i), with L>0. This is W=2R.\n\nFor a,d>=0 define\n\n    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,\n    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2,\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(C_k,C_l),\n    B_n=max_(k+l=n-1) G(C_k,B_l),\n    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda).\n\nEvery split uses nonnegative integer k,l; no split is removed. Accepted\nfact04219554e41d9750 supplies the following precise ingredients:\n\n* Section2: U_N=C_(N-1) for N>=1, U_0=0,\n  U_(2a)=lambda U_a and U_(2a+1)=Omega(U_a,U_(a+1)). These concern C only.\n* Sections3 and7: 1<rho<4/3, B_n>=C_n, B is increasing, and the positive\n  finite limit Z_b=lim_j lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1) exists for every integer\n  b>=1, with lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1)<=Z_b for every integer j>=0.\n* Sections8--9: the specific lower endpoint for B_3071 quoted in item3,\n  and the explicit convergence error quoted in item5.\n* Sections10--13: the full general-length primitive seed condition,\n  physical joins, one-untouched-leaf substitution, and shifted-ray\n  consequence quoted in items6--7.\n\nWrite Gamma_5=7977607/806000 and Gamma_6=387153999/32240000 in this\naudit; these abbreviate C107's Gamma5_c107 and Gamma6_c107. Write\nq_j=(2/3)^j. The new claims under attack are\n\n    Z_18/Z_3<Gamma_5<99/10,\n    Z_21/Z_3<Gamma_6<1201/100,\n\nand their conditional finite consequences for ACTUAL full length-five\nor length-six primitive seeds. The fixed ray is b=3 throughout.\n\n## 1. Original recurrence, normalization and all-split comparison\n\nVerdict: SURVIVES ANALYTICAL ATTACK, including both zero arguments and\nthe larger-root condition. C107 preserves the accepted W=2R convention\nand the original C and B recurrences.\n\nWhen a=d=0, G(a,lambda d)=lambda Omega(a,d)=0. Otherwise set\nw=Omega(a,d)>0 and t=a/w. The positive-root identity gives\n\n    0<=t<=1,\n    d/w=(1-t)/(1+t).\n\nThe polynomial P(v)=v^2-(4a+lambda d)v+4a^2 has larger root\nG(a,lambda d), since its discriminant is lambda^2 d^2+8lambda ad.\nUsing lambda^2=2lambda+1, the substitution is exactly\n\n    P(lambda w)/w^2\n      =[t/(1+t)]*[2lambda^2-4lambda+(4-4lambda)t+4t^2]\n      =[t/(1+t)]*[2-4sqrt(2)t+4t^2]\n      =4t(t-1/sqrt(2))^2/(1+t)>=0.\n\nThe branch check is essential and valid: w>=a+d, and\n\n    lambda(a+d)-(2a+lambda d/2)\n       =(lambda-2)a+lambda d/2>=0.\n\nThus lambda w lies to the right of the vertex, so P(lambda w)>=0\nplaces it at or beyond the larger root, not below the smaller one.\nConsequently G(a,lambda d)<=lambda Omega(a,d).\n\nThe direct boundary checks agree. For a=0 the two sides equal lambda d;\nfor d=0 they are 2a and lambda a. The nonzero equality case also survives:\ntake a=1, d=3sqrt(2)-4>0. Then w=sqrt(2), lambda d=2-sqrt(2), and\nsqrt((2-sqrt(2))^2+8(2-sqrt(2)))=3sqrt(2)-2>0. Therefore both\nsides are 2+sqrt(2). This tests the internal zero of the factored\npolynomial as well as the correct square-root branch.\n\nBoth displayed kernels are coordinatewise nondecreasing on the\nnonnegative quadrant, including the axes. Actual full seed and physical substitution interface\n\nVerdict: SURVIVES ANALYTICAL ATTACK as a conditional statement about\nthe complete original arrays. No actual seed has been supplied.\n\nLet r be5 or6, h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1))>=0, s=sum h_i and A=1+s>Gamma_r.\nBoth Gamma values exceed1, so A>1. The required multipliers are every\nLambda_ij>=0 for distinct i,j in{0,...,r}, with Lambda_ii=0. They number\nr(r+1), namely30 for r=5 and42 for r=6. With e the all-ones column,\n\n    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,\n    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,\n    D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)),\n    L_flow^T e=omega,\n    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2>=0 on R^(r+1).\n\nThese are exactly accepted Section10 for a general length r. To check\nthe signs and orientation explicitly, normalize L=1, put\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)\n         +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_r||^2.\n\nHere f_i=f(x_i), g_i=grad f(x_i), I is the identity matrix,\nout_i=sum_j Lambda_ij and in_i=sum_j Lambda_ji.\nThe coefficient of f_i in sum Lambda_ij Q_ij is out_i-in_i,\nwhich equals (L_flow^T e)_i. Thus the displayed omega cancels the\nfunction-value coefficients of V_h. The position terms contribute\nsym(L_flow(T-I)), where sym(K)=(K+K^T)/2. The squared differences\ncontribute sym(L_flow)-diag(omega)/2. Their sum with V_h's diagonal\nis precisely M_prim: the nonterminal entries become h_i(2-h_i),\nand the terminal entry becomes -s(s+2). Hence\n\n    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij\n       =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.\n\nHere Gram is the matrix of all gradient inner products and the matrix\npairing is the entrywise inner product. Accepted Section10 gives\nQ_ij<=0 on actual trajectories, so full PSD and all nonnegative\nmultiplier signs imply V_h>=0. All r+1 formal gradients remain, even\nwhen h_i=0. No duplicated-position quotient, rank assumption, support\nrestriction or zero-remainder assumption enters this implication.\n\nFor a primitive child its capacity is one plus its step sum; for a\ndominant child its mass is one plus twice its step sum. A_left,A_right\ndenote the primitive child capacities, and D_right the dominant right\nchild mass. Accepted Sections11--12 give the actual physical joins:\n\n* A primitive join uses bridge1+d with\n  d(A_left+A_right+d)=A_left*A_right; its capacity is Omega(A_left,A_right).\n* A dominant join uses bridge1+A_left/zeta, with\n  zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A_left/D_right))/2; its mass is G(A_left,D_right).\n\nAll capacities and masses here are positive. Those sections retain\nthe original interpolation coordinates and arbitrary child PSD\nremainders, so the stated full seed is a valid input for either length.\n\nIn a fully expanded B_n tree there are n bridge nodes, n primitive\nempty leaves and exactly one dominant empty leaf. This follows from\nthe split k+l=n-1: C_k contributes k+1 primitive leaves, B_l contributes\nl primitive leaves and its one dominant leaf. Substitution of a\nlength-r seed adds nr physical steps; the n original bridges remain.\nThe last dominant leaf stays empty. Thus the actual length is (r+1)n.\nCall the resulting physical word Hsub_n and put Dsub_n=1+2sum Hsub_n.\n\nHomogeneity multiplies each primitive branch capacity by A. The\nauxiliary scalar operation of also changing the last dominant leaf\nfrom mass1 toA would give root mass A B_n. The actual word does not\nmake that change. Accepted Section12 propagates its square-root loss\nby the increasing, 1-Lipschitz right-mass map and yields\n\n    sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1)<=sqrt(Dsub_n)<=sqrt(A B_n),\n    Dsub_n>=A B_n(1-ell_n)^2,\n    ell_n=(1-A^(-1/2))/sqrt(B_n).\n\nThe lower square-root bound is positive since B_n>=1. The same\naccepted section gives the full all-function certificate and matching\nHuber witness, so\n\n    W_((r+1)n)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n.\n\nConsequently there is no missing massA at the final leaf and no\nunjustified use of a scalar capacity surrogate for an actual word.\n\n## 7. True offsets, finite starting depth and uniform margin\n\nVerdict: SURVIVES ANALYTICAL ATTACK for every stated depth and every\nA above its stated convenient threshold. Thus the lengths are18*2^j-6 for r=5 and21*2^j-7\nfor r=6, and the accepted shifted limit is exactly Z_(3(r+1)).\nFor the finite upper comparison, accepted monotonicity and the\nLOWER ray enclosure give the correctly oriented bound\n\n    B_(N_j)<=B_(3(r+1)2^j-1)<=lambda^j Z_(3(r+1)). All calculations below are textual.\n\nAccepted inputs are facts04219554e41d9750, Sections10--13, for the original\nfull primitive certificate and physical composition, and1252b8823bb5a290 for\nthe interpolation orientation. The scalar drafts c1d41aaebcae37de and\nac0c1e866da0d926 and interval draft cd0f93f08a50828f were recalled as awareness,\nnot premises. Every new identity used here is proved jointly. The constant-step\ninterval proof in Zhao, arXiv:2609.11784v1, Sections3.1--3.3, was downloaded,\nextracted, and read as an analogy; no external theorem is imported.\n\n## 1. Exact scope and outcome\n\nLet r>=2 and take h_i=1+t_i with t_i>0 for0<=i<r. Define\n\n s=sum_(i<r)h_i, A=1+s, H_i=sum_(j<i)h_j,\n B_i=sum_(i<=j<r)h_j=s-H_i,\n R_0=1, R_k=product_(i<k)t_i^(-1), p_k=(R_k^2-1)/2.\n\nHere B_i is a local suffix mass, not the benchmark denominator B_n. All\nauxiliary symbols in this document are local and have implicit suffix add108.\n\nThe declared family uses an ADDITIVE ordered kernel\n\n K_ij=alpha_i+beta_j, 0<=i<j<=r.                         (1)\n\nThis has two row-times-column channels, alpha_i*1+1*beta_j, on the ordered\ndomain. Boundary values are K_(-1),j=K_i,(r+1)=0. Set t_r=0 only as an\nauxiliary terminal convention. For EVERY0<=i<j<=r define\n\n mu_ij=K_ij-K_(i-1),j+t_j(K_i,(j+1)-K_(i-1),(j+1)),\n mu_ji=K_(i-1),j-K_(i-1),(j+1)+t_i(K_ij-K_i,(j+1)),\n Lambda_ij=2mu_ji, Lambda_ji=2mu_ij, Lambda_ii=0.       (2)\n\nMembership requires every FINAL multiplier in(2) nonnegative, the original\nflow, and M_prim=0 on all r+1 original formal gradient coordinates. Neither\nchannel is required separately to satisfy the full-word flow or diagonal\ntarget. No reduction to a smaller gradient space is made.\n\nThe results are:\n\n* For every positive-excess word, all matching arrays within(1)--(2) are\n  determined explicitly by(16)--(17) below; their exact feasibility tests are\n  R_r=A and x_i,y_i,q>=0. No PSD or flow equation remains unspecified.\n* In the ENTIRE reflected length6 family\n  t=(a,b,c,c,b,a), a,b,c>0, any member has A<23/2<C_6.\n* In the ENTIRE reflected length5 family\n  t=(a,b,c,b,a), a,b,c>0, any member has A<C_5.\n  In particular neither family can meet ANY true strict integer-ray threshold,\n  not only the provisional C107 targets1201/100 and99/10.\n* A concrete balanced length6 word above1201/100 fails an explicitly identified\n  original coefficient. An actual basic length5 word at C_5, with a full\n  certificate from the accepted join, also lies outside this additive family.\n\nThe exclusions impose BOTH the additive kernel and the displayed word\nreflection. They do not exclude all two-channel kernels, all zero-remainder\narrays, positive semidefinite remainders, arbitrary words, or the original\nexact-profile Goal. No claim that this ansatz is lossless is made. Physical\nzero steps are outside the declared t_i>0 family, not identified or deleted.\n\n## 2. Original identity and full mixed-coefficient cancellation\n\nAt normalized smoothness1 retain independent formal values and gradients,\n\n x_i=x_0-sum_(j<i)h_j g_j,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n Qbar_ij=-2Q_ji\n        =2(f_i-f_j)-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)\n       +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sA||g_r||^2.\n\nWith e the all-ones column, put T_ii=1,T_ij=h_j for j<i, and zero above\nthe diagonal. Define\n\n L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r,-s(s+2)),\n M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.\n\nAccepted04219554e41d9750 gives\n\n V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>\n\nwhen L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T. Thus our zero-remainder\ntarget is precisely the polynomial identity\n\n                 V_h=sum_(i!=j)mu_ij Qbar_ij.           (3)\n\nFor the moment K can be any kernel on i<j, not necessarily additive.\nFormula(2) telescopes to\n\n sum_(a<=i)mu_(a,j)=K_ij+t_j K_i,(j+1),\n sum_(a>j)mu_(a,i)=K_(i-1),(j+1)+t_i K_i,(j+1).         (4)\n\nFor i<j the coefficient of <g_i,g_j> in the right side of(3) is\n\n 2(mu_ij+mu_ji)\n -2h_i[K_ij+t_j K_i,(j+1)]\n +2h_j[K_(i-1),(j+1)+t_i K_i,(j+1)].                   (5)\n\nAt j=r the last term vanishes by the boundary convention. Expanding(2)\ngives\n\n mu_ij+mu_ji=h_i K_ij+(t_j-t_i)K_i,(j+1)\n                         -h_j K_(i-1),(j+1).\n\nSince h_i t_j-h_j t_i=t_j-t_i, expression(5) is zero for EVERY pair.\nThis is cancellation on the full original formal space, for both channels\ntogether, with no gradient relations imposed.\n\nWrite d_i=sum_(j!=i)(mu_ij-mu_ji). The function coefficients require\n\n                  d_i=h_i (i<r), d_r=-s.               (6)\n\nLet p_i=K_(i-1),i for1<=i<=r, and p_0=0. The incoming sums from smaller\nand larger indices at a nonterminal index respectively are\n\n I_i=p_i+t_i K_(i-1),(i+1),\n J_i=K_(i-1),(i+1)+t_i p_(i+1).\n\nThe coefficient of ||g_i||^2 is\n\n -d_i-2I_i-2J_i+2h_i J_i\n                   =-d_i+2(t_i^2 p_(i+1)-p_i).        (7)\n\nThe terminal coefficient is -d_r-2p_r. Equating these with(3), under(6),\nis equivalent to\n\n t_i^2(2p_(i+1)+1)=2p_i+1 (0<=i<r),\n                    2p_r+1=A^2.                      (8)\n\nConsequently p_i=(R_i^2-1)/2 and R_r=A are necessary. Conversely(6),(8)\nand the cancellation just proved match every function and gradient\ncoefficient, giving(3) exactly. In particular all original flow and matrix\nconditions follow, not merely tests on actual trajectories.\n\n## 3. All original signs and the interior flows\n\nFor the additive kernel define\n\n x_i=alpha_i-alpha_(i-1), y_i=beta_i-beta_(i+1)\n                      (1<=i<r), q=alpha_0+beta_r.\n\nThen\n\n K_ij=q+sum_(1<=k<=i)x_k+sum_(j<=k<r)y_k.               (9)\n\nFormula(2) gives the following COMPLETE list, covering every ordered pair:\n\n mu_ij=h_j x_i, mu_ji=h_i y_j   (1<=i<j<r),\n mu_0j=h_j(q+sum_(j<k<r)y_k)+y_j,\n mu_j0=t_0 y_j                                      (1<=j<r),\n mu_ir=x_i,\n mu_ri=h_i(q+sum_(1<=k<i)x_k)+t_i x_i                 (1<=i<r),\n mu_0r=q, mu_r0=t_0 q.                                (10)\n\nBecause every h_i,t_i is positive, all signs in(10) are nonnegative if\nand only if\n\n                  x_i>=0, y_i>=0, q>=0.               (11)\n\nNecessity uses mu_ir=x_i, mu_i0=t_0 y_i and mu_0r=q, so it does not\nassume that the channels themselves are certificates or positive factors.\n\nThe required adjacent values are equivalent to\n\n p_1=q+sum_(1<=i<r)y_i,\n                   p_(i+1)-p_i=x_i-y_i.               (12)\n\nFor1<=i<r let w_i=q+sum_(k<i)x_k+sum_(k>i)y_k=p_i-y_i.\nThe outgoing sums to smaller and larger indices at i are\n(H_i-1)y_i and (1+B_i-h_i)x_i. The corresponding incoming sums are\nh_i w_i+y_i and h_i w_i+t_i x_i. Therefore\n\n d_i=(B_i-2t_i)x_i+(H_i+2t_i)y_i-2h_i p_i.            (13)\n\nPut delta_i=p_(i+1)-p_i. Since H_i+B_i=s, substituting x_i=y_i+delta_i\ninto d_i=h_i yields the UNIQUE increments\n\n y_i=[h_i(1+2p_i)-(B_i-2t_i)delta_i]/s,\n x_i=[h_i(1+2p_i)+(H_i+2t_i)delta_i]/s.                (14)\n\nHere delta_i=R_i^2(1-t_i^2)/(2t_i^2). Using h_i=1+t_i gives\n\n 2h_i t_i^2+(H_i+2t_i)(1-t_i^2)\n                =h_i[H_i+(2-H_i)t_i],\n 2h_i t_i^2-(B_i-2t_i)(1-t_i^2)\n                =h_i[(B_i+2)t_i-B_i].                 (15)\n\nHence the explicit matching formulas are\n\n x_i=R_i^2 h_i[H_i+(2-H_i)t_i]/(2s t_i^2),\n y_i=R_i^2 h_i[(B_i+2)t_i-B_i]/(2s t_i^2),            (16)\n                   q=p_1-sum_(1<=i<r)y_i.             (17)\n\nTogether with(9)--(10), these specify every original coefficient directly\nfrom the word. There is no unselected skew parameter or remaining feasibility\noracle inside the declared family.\n\n## 4. The endpoint flows are consequences, with proof\n\nSuppose R_r=A, set p_i as in Section1, and construct(16)--(17). Then(12)\nholds and hence every required adjacent kernel value holds. Section3 proves\nall interior flows d_i=h_i for1<=i<r. We now check BOTH endpoints.\n\nEvery array has sum_i d_i=0. By(7),(8), the sum of its gradient-square\ncoefficients, all mixed coefficients already being zero, is\n\n sum_(i<r)(1-t_i^2)-2p_r=-s^2-sum_(i<r)h_i^2.         (18)\n\nIn the polynomial identity for sum mu Qbar, substitute the formal scalar\ndata g_i=1,x_i=-H_i,f_i=-H_i, with H_0=0,H_r=s. Each Qbar_ij is zero\nby its displayed definition. This is an algebraic substitution, not an\nassumption identifying any gradients in the certificate. Thus(18) implies\n\n sum_i d_i H_i=-(s^2+sum_(i<r)h_i^2)/2.                (19)\n\nThe target flow d_i^*=h_i for i<r,d_r^*=-s has the same moment, because\n\n sum_(i<r)h_i H_i=(s^2-sum_(i<r)h_i^2)/2.\n\nOnly its endpoints can differ from d. If d_0-d_0^*=z, total flow zero\nmakes d_r-d_r^*=-z. Their moment difference is -s z; by(19) it is zero.\nSince s>0, z=0. Thus every endpoint flow is correct. Section2 now proves\nM_prim=0 on the entire original space.\n\nConversely, every matching additive kernel had to satisfy(8),(12),(13),\nso(16)--(17) were forced. Factor gauges alpha_i->alpha_i+c,\nbeta_j->beta_j-c change no kernel entry. We have therefore parameterized\nthe ENTIRE stated additive family, not one sufficient coefficient choice.\n\n### A genuine two-channel calibration and the failed scalar division\n\nThe recalled Silver example can be checked inside this exact kernel family.\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2), kappa=lambda^2, and h=(sqrt(2),2,sqrt(2)). Set\n\n q=0, x_1=y_1=lambda, x_2=kappa lambda, y_2=0.\n\nThen the six ordered kernel entries are\n\n K_01=lambda, K_02=K_03=0,\n K_12=K_13=lambda, K_23=lambda(kappa+1).\n\nTheir two additive factors can be written\nalpha_i=(0,lambda,lambda(kappa+1)) and beta_j=(lambda,0,0).\nFormula(10) gives the nonzero mu entries\n\n mu_01=lambda, mu_10=1,\n mu_12=lambda sqrt(2), mu_13=lambda, mu_31=lambda,\n mu_23=kappa lambda, mu_32=lambda+kappa+1.\n\nEvery listed entry is positive. The corresponding complete Lambda=2mu^T\nis exactly the coefficient array obtained by joining two one-step\nsqrt(2) children with bridge2 in the accepted primitive identity:\n\n V_h=V_left+kappa V_right-(kappa-1)\n                [sqrt(2)Q_21+Q_31+Q_13+sqrt(2)Q_23],\n V_left=-2Q_01-2lambda Q_10,\n V_right=-2Q_23-2lambda Q_32.\n\nThe relations kappa=2lambda+1 and lambda sqrt(2)=lambda+1 check the\ndisplayed coefficients. This is full flow and zero remainder on all four\nformal gradients, not a reduced-space calculation.\n\nThis ordered kernel cannot be one separable pair U_i D_j: K_01!=0 forces\nU_0!=0, K_13!=0 forces D_3!=0, yet K_03=0. This uses only entries on\ntheir ordered domain, not the ordinary rank of a zero-padded matrix.\nThe beta-only component has support just on the pair0,1, so it cannot\nsupply the full word's nonzero flow at index2. The alpha-only component\nhas no incidence at index0, so it cannot supply that node's nonzero flow.\nThe components are therefore not two valid full-word certificates.\n\nFor this word, the first prefix capacity and reciprocal product are BOTH\nlambda. Consequently the scalar positive-factor argument requiring a\nstrict prefix inequality cannot be reused by division in the combined\nkernel. This explains a genuine additional freedom; it does not refute\nthe separate non-strict hereditary inequality, which this basic word\nsatisfies. The calibration is recalled for this scope check, not claimed\nas a new improving seed or new Silver certificate.\n\n## 5. Necessary tail signs for either reflected word\n\nIn both length5 and length6 reflected words write their positive excesses\nas indicated in Section1. Define\n\n alpha=sqrt(2)-1,\n beta=the positive root of z^2+(3+alpha)z-(2+alpha)=0,\n D=3+alpha+beta, lambda=1+sqrt(2)=2+alpha.\n\nThese alpha,beta are scalar constants in this section, distinct from the\nkernel factor sequences alpha_i,beta_j.\n\nAt the last physical step, nonnegativity of y_(r-1) in(16) implies\n\n                    a^2+2a-1>=0,\n\nand hence a>=alpha. In particular a>2/5, since\n(2/5)^2+2(2/5)-1=-1/25<0. Also alpha<1/2.\n\nAt the penultimate step the suffix mass is 2+a+b. Its y sign gives\n\n                    b^2+(3+a)b-(2+a)>=0.              (20)\n\nThe root beta lies strictly between3/5 and2/3: the polynomial defining it\nhas values (2/5)(2/5-alpha)<0 at3/5 and4/9-alpha/3>0 at2/3, and is\nstrictly increasing on the nonnegative line. If b>=1 then b>beta. If b<1,\nthe left side of(20) decreases with a; since a>=alpha its value at alpha\nis at least its nonnegative value at a. Thus b>=beta in this case also.\nConsequently b>3/5 and a+b>1.\n\nAt the first central c step, the suffix mass is 3+a+b+c in length5,\nand4+a+b+2c in length6. Each is greater than4. The y sign is\n\n                       c>=B_i/(B_i+2),\n\nso in both cases c>2/3. We have proved the simultaneous necessary bounds\n\n             a>=alpha>2/5, b>=beta>3/5, c>2/3.         (21)\n\nNo q or x inequality has been discarded as a feasibility assertion:\n(21) consists only of necessary consequences of named ORIGINAL multiplier\nsigns Lambda_(0,i)=2t_0 y_i. Keeping fewer necessary conditions can only\nweaken the upper bounds that follow.\n\n## 6. Entire reflected length6 family has A<23/2\n\nHere A=7+2(a+b+c) and R_6=A requires\n\n                         A a^2 b^2 c^2=1.             (22)\n\nFor a>=2/5,b>=3/5,c>=2/3, write a=2/5+u,b=3/5+v,c=2/3+w with u,v,w>=0.\nExpansion of the product has nonnegative terms of degree at least2 in\nu,v,w, and its linear coefficients are2/5,4/15,6/25, respectively.\nEvery one is at least6/25. Its constant term is4/25. Therefore\n\n abc>=4/25+(6/25)(u+v+w)\n     =(6/25)(a+b+c-1)=3(A-9)/25.                       (23)\n\nIf A>=23/2, the right side of(23) is positive. The function\nA(A-9)^2 is strictly increasing for A>9, since its derivative is\n(A-9)(3A-9)>0. Equations(22)--(23) give\n\n 1>=9A(A-9)^2/625\n   >=[9(23/2)(5/2)^2]/625=207/200>1,\n\na contradiction. Hence A<23/2 for every member of the entire reflected\nsix-step family, regardless of all its other coefficient choices.\n\nTo compare with the ORIGINAL primitive benchmark, C_1=lambda>12/5.\nLet D=C_2=Omega(1,lambda); D>4 follows by evaluating\nz^2-(1+lambda)z-lambda at z=4: its value is12-5lambda<0.\nThe recurrence also has C_3>=Omega(lambda,lambda)=lambda^2>29/5.\nBy monotonicity and the split2+3,\n\n C_6>=Omega(C_2,C_3)>Omega(4,29/5)>23/2.               (24)\n\nFor the last inequality, the positive-root polynomial\nz^2-(49/5)z-116/5 has value -73/20<0 at z=23/2.\nThus the claimed comparison uses an actual original all-split lower\nbound, with no numerical benchmark evaluation.\n\n## 7. Entire reflected length5 family has A<C_5\n\nHere A=6+2a+2b+c and the necessary balance is\n\n                         A a^2 b^2 c=1.               (25)\n\nThe definitions in Section5 give beta D=2+alpha=lambda and\nalpha lambda=1. Hence\n\n                 alpha beta D=1,\n                 D=Omega(1,lambda)=C_2.               (26)\n\nWrite a=alpha+u,b=beta+v with u,v>=0. Since c>2/3>beta>alpha, expanding\n(1+u/alpha)^2(1+v/beta)^2 and keeping its nonnegative linear terms gives\n\n a^2 b^2 c\n >=alpha^2 beta^2[c+2c u/alpha+2c v/beta]\n >=alpha^2 beta^2(c+2u+2v)\n                    =(A-2D)/D^2.                     (27)\n\nIn particular A-2D=c+2u+2v>0. Combining(25)--(27) yields\n\n A^2-2DA-D^2<=0, hence A<=lambda D.                    (28)\n\nEquality in(28) forces u=v=0, because c>beta>alpha makes the second\ninequality of(27) strict whenever u+v>0. Thus it would require\na=alpha,b=beta. At the penultimate index the y numerator then vanishes,\nby(20). At the EARLIER occurrence of b, its suffix mass exceeds the\npenultimate suffix mass by 2+b+c>0. Its y numerator is\n2b-(1-b)B_i. Since beta<1, increasing B_i makes that numerator strictly\nnegative. This contradicts the original sign at that earlier index.\nConsequently A<lambda D.\n\nThe original recurrence permits the split2+2, so C_5>=Omega(D,D)=lambda D.\nFor completeness equality C_5=lambda D can also be obtained from accepted\nbalanced-splitting results, but is not needed: the proved strict inequality\nA<lambda D<=C_5 already excludes a useful seed from this family.\n\nThe provisional target99/10 is also strictly larger than lambda D.\nOne elementary enclosure is lambda<17/7 and\nD<Omega(1,17/7)<81/20. The latter positive-root polynomial has value\n247/2800>0 at81/20, above its positive vertex. Thus\nlambda D<1377/140<99/10. No ingredient of C107 is used in this proof.\n\n## 8. Actual targets and exact limitations of the family\n\nTake the proposed nonbasic length6 word with\n\n a=1/2, b=1/3,\n c=the positive root of 3c^3+13c^2-54=0.\n\nThe polynomial increases strictly for c>0 and its value at17/10 is\n-1691/1000. Hence c>17/10 and\n\n A=26/3+2c>181/15>1201/100.\n\nIts defining polynomial gives A a^2 b^2 c^2=1 exactly. The singleton\nstep excess b violates the scalar hereditary condition, since\n(2+b)b=7/9<1. This is why it was a concrete nonbasic target, not a\nrescaled sum of feasible one-pair certificates.\n\nThe coupled additive family cannot certify it. At i=4, B_4=17/6,t_4=1/3,\nand (B_4+2)t_4-B_4=-11/9. Formula(16) gives\n\n y_4=-22 R_4^2/(3s)<0,\n                    Lambda_04=2t_0 y_4=y_4<0.         (29)\n\nThis is failure of an explicitly identified ORIGINAL interpolation\ncoefficient. All full-flow additive re-selection has already been exhausted\nby Sections3--4. It is not a claim that this word lacks a different full\ncertificate. Nor does balancing and exceeding the provisional capacity\nmake it an actual seed.\n\nThere is also an actual full certificate that the present family misses.\nUse the primitive join in accepted04219554e41d9750. A single step sqrt(2)\nhas V=-2Q_01-2lambda Q_10 by expansion. Joining it to an empty word on\neither side gives the length2 words (sqrt(2),1+beta) and\n(1+beta,sqrt(2)), each of capacity D, because beta D=lambda.\nJoin these words using central step1+alpha D: the bridge excess d=alpha D\nsatisfies d(2D+d)=D^2. This gives the actual length5 word\n\n h=(sqrt(2),1+beta,1+alpha D,1+beta,sqrt(2))              (30)\n\nof capacity lambda D, with all nonnegative original multipliers and full\nzero remainder supplied by the accepted exact join identity. Yet the\nstrict result of Section7 excludes any additive representation(1)--(2)\nfor(30), regardless of coefficient re-selection. This is a concrete scope\ncheck against calling additive two-channel matching lossless.\n\n## 9. True rays and the actual physical substitution\n\nDefine the original recurrences\n\n C_0=B_0=1,\n C_n=max_(k+l=n-1)Omega(C_k,C_l),\n B_n=max_(k+l=n-1)G(C_k,B_l),\n Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2,\n G(x,d)=2x+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8xd)/2.\n\nFor b>=1 integer, accepted04219554e41d9750 supplies the positive ray limits\nZ_b=lim_j lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1), including fixed nonnegative offsets.\nWe need a TRUE lower comparison, not an upper sufficient threshold:\n\n                       Z_((r+1)b)>=C_r Z_b.            (31)\n\nHere is its full short proof. One has G(a,d)>=Omega(a,d) for a,d>=0:\ntwice their difference is\n3a+sqrt(d^2+8ad)-sqrt(a^2+6ad+d^2)>=0, because squaring the first\ntwo nonnegative terms gives excess8a^2+2ad+6a sqrt(d^2+8ad)>=0.\nInduction through the all-split recurrences gives B_r>=C_r.\n\nTake a maximizing fully expanded B_n tree. Replace each primitive empty\nleaf by a maximizing C_r tree and its unique dominant empty leaf by a\nmaximizing B_r tree. There are n+1 leaves in total. The resulting original\ntree has (r+1)(n+1)-1 steps. The positive homogeneity and monotonicity of\nOmega and G, together with B_r>=C_r, give its mass at least C_r B_n.\nThus B_((r+1)(n+1)-1)>=C_r B_n. Set n=b*2^j-1, divide by lambda^j,\nand take the accepted limits to prove(31).\n\nEvery actual member in either reflected family has A<C_r by Sections6--7.\nTherefore, for EVERY positive integer b,\n\n                    Z_((r+1)b)/(A Z_b)>1.              (32)\n\nThe same conclusion is visible before taking limits. Substitute an actual\nsuch seed into all n primitive leaves of an original maximizing B_n tree,\nleave its single dominant empty leaf empty, and recompute every physical\nbridge by the accepted formulas. Its length is (r+1)n and its actual mass\nis D_A. Accepted04219554e41d9750 supplies W_((r+1)n)=1/D_A for that\nschedule, with the full objective certificate and the matching Huber witness.\nSubstituting a basic primitive word of the SAME length r and capacity C_r\ninstead gives mass D_C>D_A for n>=1, by strict monotonicity of every join.\nIt is an original basic dominant tree at the same horizon, so\n\n B_((r+1)n)>=D_C>D_A,\n                    W_((r+1)n)>1/B_((r+1)n).          (33)\n\nThese statements retain W=2R, the unchanged dominant leaf, and the actual\nhorizons (r+1)(b*2^j-1); at b=3 these are18*2^j-6 or21*2^j-7.\nThey do not depend on the unverified C107 upper threshold package. No\nray switch rescues an actual seed from either declared reflected family.\n\n## 10. Remaining allocation and precise next freedom\n\nThe first-round construction has been solved, including all42 or30 original\nmultipliers, complete flows, and full-space zero remainder. The mixed\ncoefficient cancellation in Section2 remains exact. The NEW changes\ngamma_2-gamma_1 and gamma_4-gamma_3 enter the actual ordered coefficients\nand the interior flows, so formula(16) and the failed sign(29) must be\nrecomputed; they cannot be imported as necessities for(34). This is a\nspecified channel interaction, not a generic PSD/skew optimization or a\nsum of full-word weak certificates. The existence of any useful member,\neven for the balanced high-capacity target in Section8, is OPEN.\n\nThis mutation is a plan for the already allocated final round, not a\nself-renewal. The broader additive family without reflection also remains\nunclassified by the capacity argument here. The original exact-profile\nGoal is OPEN. K is the ordered kernel; alpha_i,beta_j are its additive\nchannel factors; x_i,y_i are their increments; q is its remote corner.\nmu=Lambda^T/2 uses Qbar_ij=-2Q_ji. d_i is net mu outflow; I_i,J_i are incoming\nmu sums; w_i is the indicated interior kernel value; delta_i=p_(i+1)-p_i.\nThe scalars alpha,beta,D,lambda in Section5 are separately defined constants.\nC_n and B_n in Section9 are the ORIGINAL benchmark sequences, and Z_b their\ndominant ray limit. (2)\n\nThe exact original multipliers are still Lambda_ij=2mu_ji, where\n\n mu_ij=K_ij-K_(i-1),j+t_j(K_i,(j+1)-K_(i-1),(j+1)),\n mu_ji=K_(i-1),j-K_(i-1),(j+1)+t_i(K_ij-K_i,(j+1)). (7)\n\nMoreover,\n\n (B_i-t_i)beta_(i+1)-V_i\n =sum_(k=i+1)^(r-1)h_k(beta_(i+1)-beta_(k+1))\n =sum_(j=i+1)^(r-1)B_j y_j.                           (8)\n\nInsert(6) into(7), use(8), and impose the ORIGINAL net mu flow d_i=h_i.\nThe result is the explicit backward recursion\n\n D_i y_i=N_i+delta_gamma_i sum_(j>i)B_j y_j,\n D_i=S_i+B_i gamma_(i-1)>0,\n N_i=h_i R_i^2-(B_i-2t_i)delta_p_i\n    =R_i^2 h_i[(B_i+2)t_i-B_i]/(2t_i^2).               (9)\n\nThus y_(r-1),...,y_1 are uniquely determined, in that order, with no\nlinear-system or PSD oracle. Together with(2), every original coefficient\nin(4)--(5) is fixed. Formula(9) exhibits\nprecisely the new transfer between later column increments and earlier\nflow signs. In particular N_i<0 need no longer force y_i<0.\n\nFor clarity, the full original identity follows directly, not by accepting\nan unverified local lemma. Formula(3) gives\n\n sum_(a<=i)mu_(a,j)=K_ij+t_j K_i,(j+1),\n sum_(a>j)mu_(a,i)=K_(i-1),(j+1)+t_i K_i,(j+1).\n\nExpanding mu_ij+mu_ji gives h_i K_ij+(t_j-t_i)K_i,(j+1)\n-h_j K_(i-1),(j+1), so every mixed gradient coefficient in sum mu Qbar\nvanishes. For a this follows\nfrom a^2+2a-1=1/4900>0; for b it follows from b>47/28>1. However the\ninterior interval containing physical indices2,3 has\n\n (1+h_2+h_3)t_2 t_3=(3+2a)a^2\n                            =112694/171500<1. Profile(13)\ninstead couples BOTH small middle steps to later y terms. Its nonzero\ninterior changes are delta_gamma_2=1,delta_gamma_3=2,delta_gamma_4=-3.\n\nFor(13), the full original nonnegativity test is EXACTLY\n\n alpha_0>=0, x_i>=0 and y_i>=0 for1<=i<=5,\n                         h_5 x_4>=3y_5.              (14)\n\nIndeed every backward coefficient in(4) is a positive factor times y_j.\nEvery terminal forward coefficient is x_i, and alpha_0=mu_06. They are not benchmark capacities,\ndenominator rays, or the factors in the closed one-pair program.\n\n## 1. Original full array and polynomial identity\n\nFix a physical length r>=1 and h_i>=0 for 0<=i<r. Set\n\n s=sum_(i<r)h_i, A=1+s,\n T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,\n e=ones(r+1), L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n D=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r, -s(s+2)).\n\nThe hypotheses under examination are exactly\n\n Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j, Lambda_ii=0,\n L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,\n D+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2=0 on ALL R^(r+1).       (1)\n\nAll original formal gradients and every ordered pair are retained.\nDefine\n\n x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n Qbar_ij=-2Q_ji\n        =2(f_i-f_j)-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2,\n mu_ij=Lambda_ji/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)\n       +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sA||g_r||^2.\n\nThe accepted original identity in fact04219554e41d9750, Section10,\nturns (1) into the polynomial identity\n\n                 V_h=sum_(i!=j)mu_ij Qbar_ij.          (2)\n\nEquation (2) holds on the full formal space, for arbitrary formal values\nand gradients. Actual interpolation is not needed when evaluating this\npolynomial identity. Conversely, value and quadratic coefficient matching\nin (2) gives the exact flow and full zero matrix in (1).\n\n## 2. Algebraic isolation of zero steps\n\nThis section supplies the required zero-boundary bookkeeping; it is not\nadvertised as a new result beyond the earlier round38 necessary conditions.\n\nPut t_i=h_i-1 for i<r and define\n\n d_i=sum_(j!=i)(mu_ij-mu_ji),\n I_i=sum_(j<i)mu_ji, J_i=sum_(j>i)mu_ji.\n\nValue matching gives d_i=h_i for i<r and d_r=-s. At a nonterminal i,\nthe coefficient of ||g_i||^2 on the right of (2) is\n\n             -d_i-2I_i-2J_i+2h_i J_i.\n\nIndeed each squared difference contributes its two incident weights;\nonly Qbar_(j,i) with j>i contributes a trajectory diagonal, with\ncoefficient 2h_i. Matching V_h therefore gives\n\n                 t_i J_i-I_i=(1-t_i^2)/2.             (3)\n\nIf 0<h_i<=1, its left side is nonpositive and its right side is positive.\nThus every positive h_i exceeds1. If h_i=0, equation (3) gives\nI_i+J_i=0. Nonnegative entries make the entire incoming mu column zero;\nd_i=0 then makes its entire outgoing row zero as well. Hence the entire\noriginal Lambda row AND column vanish at every zero-step index.\n\nList the positive-step indices as iota(0)<...<iota(m-1) and retain the\nORIGINAL terminal index by setting iota(m)=r. Restrict Lambda to these\nindices and relabel h_(iota(j)) as the jth positive step. Every removed\nstep is zero and every incident multiplier was already zero. The\nretained principal T is precisely the prefix matrix for this relabeling.\nMoreover, if h_i=0, the ith column of original T is the coordinate column\ne_i. Since the corresponding column and row of L_flow are zero, both\nthe ith column and row of the full remainder vanish. Thus (1) restricts\nto the retained array and extends back to every independent original\nzero-step gradient without imposing a relation among any gradients.\n\nAll-zero words have the zero array and no nonempty positive interval.\nOtherwise Sections3--7 use this purely algebraic positive relabeling,\nwith its length again denoted r. This changes no physical horizon and\nis not a deletion of actual GD iterations in a benchmark comparison.\n\nFor completeness, evaluating (2) on the scalar unit quadratic, with\ng_0=1 and g_(i+1)=-t_i g_i, makes every Qbar_ij zero and gives\n\n                    A product_(i<r)t_i=1.             (4)\n\nTo check it, V_h telescopes to 1-A^2 g_r^2; all t_i are now positive.\nNeither (3) nor (4) supplies the missing interval implication.\n\n## 3. Exact mixed matching with every crossing entry\n\nFor a<b, one half of the coefficient of <g_a,g_b> on the right of (2) is\n\n mu_ab+mu_ba-h_a sum_(j<=a)mu_jb+h_b sum_(j>b)mu_ja.\n\nHere h_r is assigned the auxiliary value0 only when b=r. The first two\nterms are from the squared differences; the next two are respectively\nfrom trajectory segments ending at b and starting at a. Consequently\n\n t_a mu_ab=mu_ba-h_a sum_(j<a)mu_jb\n                           +h_b sum_(j>b)mu_ja.        (5)\n\nThe sum preceding a has a MINUS sign. In particular it cannot be\ndiscarded as an additional nonnegative supply.\n\nOne useful cumulative form is obtained by setting\n\n F_ab=sum_(j<=a)mu_jb, F_(-1)b=0,\n R_ab=sum_(j>=b)mu_ja, R_a,(r+1)=0,   a<b.\n\nEquation (5) becomes\n\n t_a F_ab+F_(a-1)b=R_ab+t_b R_a,(b+1),                 (6)\n\nwhere t_r=0 is only an auxiliary terminal placeholder. The original\nsigns imply F_ab>=F_(a-1)b>=0 and R_ab>=R_a,(b+1)>=0.\nThey do not give a separable expression for either cumulative array.\nNo factorization has been imposed in (5)--(6).\n\n## 4. First attempted mechanism: an isolated interval quadratic\n\nFix ANY nonempty interval I=[l,k), with 0<=l<k<=r. Let\n\n H=sum_(i=l)^(k-1)h_i,\n v_l=1, v_(i+1)=-t_i v_i for l<=i<k,\n p=v_k, q=|p|=product_(i=l)^(k-1)t_i,\n A_i^out=sum_(j<l)mu_ji,\n B_i^out=sum_(j>=k)mu_ji,       l<=i<k.\n\nThese last two quantities are the actual incoming auxiliary weights\nfrom the two exterior sides. Set formal g_i=v_i on I and g_i=0 outside\nI, including the terminal, and set every f_i=0. Up to an arbitrary\ntranslation, positions inside are v_i, positions on the left are1,\nand positions on the right are p.\n\nFor an internal ordered pair, Qbar_ij=-(v_i^2-v_j^2). An incoming edge\nfrom the left to i has residual v_i^2-2v_i; an incoming edge from the\nright has residual v_i^2-2p v_i; every outgoing edge from i to the\nexterior has residual -v_i^2. All-exterior residuals vanish. Using\nd_i=h_i, the right side of (2) is therefore\n\n -sum_(i in I)h_i v_i^2\n           -2sum_(i in I)v_i(A_i^out+p B_i^out).\n\nThe left side is sum_(i in I)h_i(1-h_i)v_i^2. Since\nh_i(2-h_i)v_i^2=v_i^2-v_(i+1)^2, this gives the exact identity\n\n sum_(i in I)v_i(A_i^out+p B_i^out)=(p^2-1)/2.         (7)\n\nThis is a factor-free interval cut identity. Its exterior terms remain\nsigned through v_i and p. Nonnegative A_i^out,B_i^out alone give no\nupper bound on the alternating weighted sum that would compare q with\n1/(1+H). Deleting A_i^out for an interior interval is unjustified.\nThus this first direct attempt stops at the signed boundary moment (7).\n\n## 5. Second mechanism: an exact interval-defect identity\n\nThe next test retains the exterior times, rather than replacing the\nexterior gradients by zero. Define global prefix sums S_i=sum_(t<i)h_t\nand put P=S_l. Retain H,v,p,q from Section4 and define formal gradients\n\n psi_i=1                         if i<l,\n psi_i=v_i                       if l<=i<k,\n psi_i=p                         if k<=i<=r.\n\nChoose x_l=1. The complete original GD equations then give\n\n x_i=1+S_l-S_i                   if i<l,\n x_i=v_i                         if l<=i<k,\n x_i=p(1-(S_i-S_k))              if k<=i<=r.\n\nUse these formal values:\n\n f_i=1/2+S_l-S_i                 if i<l,\n f_i=v_i^2/2                     if l<=i<k,\n f_i=p^2/2-p^2(S_i-S_k)          if k<=i<=r.           (8)\n\nValues and positions agree at the two boundaries. This is a FORMAL\npolynomial test. It is not claimed to be interpolable by one static\nconvex smooth function, and is not used as a primal witness. Indeed\nsome of its residuals below can be negative.\n\nAll within-region residuals vanish. The complete list of possibly\nnonzero residuals is\n\n Qbar_ij=2(S_l-S_i)(1-psi_j),    i<l, j>=l,\n Qbar_ji=2(S_j-S_k)p(psi_i-p),   j>=k, i<k.            (9)\n\nThe reverse orientation between the left and middle regions, and the\nreverse orientation between the middle and right regions, is zero.\nFor a left/right pair, both expressions in (9) apply to its respective\norientations, so no crossing pair is lost or counted twice.\n\nFor example, for i<l and l<=j<k, put d=S_l-S_i. Direct substitution is\n\n Qbar_ij=1+2d-v_j^2-2v_j(1+d-v_j)-(1-v_j)^2\n         =2d(1-v_j),\n Qbar_ji=v_j^2-1-2d-2(v_j-1-d)-(v_j-1)^2=0.\n\nFor l<=i<k and j>=k, put b=S_j-S_k. Direct substitution gives\nQbar_ij=0 and Qbar_ji=2b p(v_i-p). For a left/right pair the same\nsubstitution gives 2d(1-p) in the first orientation and 2b p(1-p)\nin the second. This proves the full list (9).\n\nDefine the two ACTUAL weighted boundary sums\n\n C_L=sum_(i<l,j>=l)mu_ij(S_l-S_i)(1-psi_j),\n C_R=sum_(j>=k,i<k)mu_ji(S_j-S_k)p(psi_i-p).            (10)\n\nHere empty sums, including C_L when l=0 and C_R when k=r, are zero.\nEquation (2) evaluated on (8) is exactly\n\n            (P+1)^2-(P+H+1)^2 p^2=2(C_L+C_R).         (11)\n\nFor an explicit check of its left side, write R=s-P-H. The prefix\ncontribution to 2sum h_i f_i+sum h_i(1-h_i)psi_i^2 is P^2+2P;\nthe interval contribution is 1-p^2; the suffix contribution is\np^2(2R-R^2). The remaining terminal terms are\n-s(s+2)p^2+2sR p^2. Their sum is (P+1)^2-(P+H+1)^2p^2.\n\nDefine the hereditary defect\n\n                    Delta_I=(1+H)^2p^2-1.\n\nSince q>0 and 1+H>0, the requested inequality is equivalent to\nDelta_I>=0. Rearranging (11) gives the precise original-array formula\n\n Delta_I=P[P+2-(P+2H+2)p^2]-2(C_L+C_R).              (12)\n\nThus the remaining coefficient inequality, with ALL original signs,\nflow, and mixed matching retained, is\n\n 2(C_L+C_R) <= P[P+2-(P+2H+2)p^2]                    (13)\n\nfor every 0<=l<k<=r. This report does not prove (13).\n\nAt the first singleton l=0,k=1, C_L=0, p=-t_0<0, and the only i<k\nis i=0 with psi_i=1. Consequently every term of C_R is nonpositive,\nso (12) proves that boundary case. At a general prefix, C_L remains\nzero but psi_i alternates; the same entrywise argument is invalid.\nAt an interior interval both C_L and C_R must remain in (13).\n\n### 5.1 What summing the prefix crossing flows does and does not provide\n\nFor l=0 write H=S_k and, for k<=j<=r, set\n\n F_j=sum_(i<k)mu_ij, b_j=sum_(i<k)mu_ji,\n B_j=sum_(i<k)mu_ji v_i, w_j=-B_j/p,\n e_j=S_j-S_k.\n\nAll F_j,b_j are nonnegative. The signed quantity w_j is not a factor\nand is not assumed nonnegative. Multiplying (5), rearranged in its\ncumulative form, by v_i and summing i=0,...,k-1 telescopes because\nt_i v_i=-v_(i+1). The exact result, including j=r with h_r=0, is\n\n             w_j+h_j sum_(m>j)w_m=F_j.                (18)\n\nMore explicitly, the left cumulative sum in (6) telescopes to\n-p F_j, and the right one is B_j+h_j sum_(m>j)B_m.\nThis proves (18) without a separation of the two indices.\n\nThe quadratic cut (7), now with no incoming left boundary, gives\n\n             sum_(j>=k)w_j=(p^(-2)-1)/2.              (19)\n\nThe original value flow over the prefix gives\n\n             sum_(j>=k)(F_j-b_j)=H.                   (20)\n\nSince e_j=sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t, summing (18) yields\n\n sum_(j>=k)e_j w_j=sum_(j>=k)F_j-sum_(j>=k)w_j.\n\nAlso, directly from (10),\n\n                  C_R=-p^2 sum_(j>=k)e_j(w_j+b_j).    (21)\n\nSubstitute these formulas in (11). After (19)--(20) cancel the signed\ntotal, the remaining nonnegative boundary moment is exactly\n\n                   sum_(j>=k)(1+e_j)b_j=H^2/2.       (22)\n\nFor clarity, this last cancellation reads\n1-(1+H)^2p^2=-2p^2[H+sum(1+e_j)b_j-(p^(-2)-1)/2].\nSubtract1 and divide by -p^2 to obtain (22).\n\nThus prefix flow and the quadratic cut fix two totals, (19) and (22),\neven in the full unfactored array. The remaining prefix inequality is\n\n                   sum_(j>=k)e_j(w_j+b_j)>=0.         (23)\n\nBackward substitution in (18) is w_j=F_j-h_j sum_(m>j)w_m;\nnonnegativity of F_j alone does not provide the sign in (23).\nThe additional original constraints on the same array have not been\nshown to force it here. At k=r-1 only j=r has positive e_j and\nw_r=F_r>=0, so (23) holds for that boundary case. This observation is\nnot an induction through arbitrary intervals: eliminating a suffix\nwould change the other crossing entries and their equations.\n\n## 6. Minimal-violation screening does not fix the missing sign\n\nIf heredity fails, choose an inclusion-minimal nonempty violating\ninterval I. It changes the missing mechanism to a quantitative\nbalance between those ACTUAL edges. This is the precise obstruction\nleft by the second direct attempt, not a claim that (13) is false.\n\n## 7. (15)\n\nEvery entry is nonnegative, including the two stated off-diagonal zeros.\nThe root equation implies dC=4 and\n\n                    K(4-d)=2(1+d).                   (16)\n\nIdentity (16) follows from the accepted join identity with child\ncapacities1 and4; it is also checked directly as follows:\n\n (C^2-16)(4-d)-32(1+d)\n = (9+10d+d^2)(4-d)-32-32d\n = 4-d-6d^2-d^3\n = -(1+d)(d^2+5d-4),\n\nwhich is zero. Therefore its FULL original remainder M is identically zero;\nno restricted Gram test, quotient, or unidentified gradient is involved.\n\nNow take the prefix interval I=[0,2). Its local data are\n\n v_0=1, v_1=-d, p=d/2, H=5/2+d,\n S_3-S_2=3/2, mu_30=Lambda_03/2=Kd/2.\n\nThe (j,i)=(3,0) summand of C_R is\n\n                 (3Kd^2/8)(1-d/2)>0.                (17)\n\nThus even an actual full M=0 array does not give a nonpositive sign\nto each right-boundary term. Every new argument used below is reproduced here.\nEarlier unverified closures are motivation, not correctness premises.\n\n## 1. What changed, and what the evidence does and does not say\n\nThe second and final declared template is T2_recurrent_signed_carrier\n(plan4af8cb8007ee35f8). It changes the dependence law: a persistent\ncarrier direction returns after an intervening excursion, while two\nreused branch directions have negative inner product. Declared scope and results\n\nFor any integer r>=2 let h_i=1+t_i with t_i>0, 0<=i<r. Put\n\n s=sum_(i<r)h_i, A=1+s, H_i=sum_(k<i)h_k,\n sigma_i=sum_(i<=k<r)h_k, R_i=product_(k<i)t_k^(-1),\n p_i=(R_i^2-1)/2.\n\nThe empty product is1, so R_0=1 and p_0=0. (2)\n\nThe conclusions proved jointly below are:\n\n* All original zero-remainder matching equations in this entire family\n  have an explicit backward solution, and its full sign test is stated.\n* Every NONNEGATIVE original array in this family must satisfy every\n  positive suffix product inequality\n\n       (1+sigma_i) product_(i<=k<r)t_k >=1, 1<=i<r. This is a\n  necessary condition only for the declared family, not the full cone.\n* The C108 word t=(a,b,a,a,b,a), a=29/70 and\n  a^4 b^2(7+4a+2b)=1, violates (3) at i=2. Thus the positive-profile restriction is demonstrably not\n  lossless, even for full zero-remainder arrays.\n\nNo all-interval theorem, capacity cap for every word in this family,\ngeneral higher-rank obstruction, or original-target answer is asserted.\nThe separate factor-free interval question assigned to max2 is not used.\n\n## 2. Original full-space identity and adjacent matching\n\nRetain independent formal gradients g_0,...,g_r and values f_0,...,f_r.\nUse x_i=x_0-sum_(k<i)h_k g_k and the accepted residual orientation\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n Qbar_ij=-2Q_ji\n        =2(f_i-f_j)-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2.\n\nDefine\n\n V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)\n        +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sA||g_r||^2.\n\nFact04219554e41d9750 says that the original flow and full M_prim=0\ncondition are exactly the polynomial identity\n\n                  V_h=sum_(i!=j)mu_ij Qbar_ij.         (4)\n\nMore explicitly, e is the all-ones column, T has T_ii=1 and T_ij=h_j\nfor j<i and0 otherwise, L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, and\n\n L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,\n M_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r,-s(s+2))\n                   +(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2=0.       (5)\n\nFor arbitrary K, formulas (2) telescope to\n\n sum_(a<=i)mu_(a,j)=K_ij+t_j K_i,(j+1),\n sum_(a>j)mu_(a,i)=K_(i-1),(j+1)+t_i K_i,(j+1). Complete matching for every positive profile\n\nSet gamma_(-1)=alpha_(-1)=0. Define\n\n y_i=beta_i-beta_(i+1), 1<=i<r,\n xi_i=alpha_i-alpha_(i-1), 0<=i<r.\n\nHere xi_i is a row-coefficient difference and is distinct from the\nphysical point x_i. Equation (8) and the gauge give\n\n beta_i=sum_(i<=j<r)y_j,\n alpha_i=p_(i+1)-gamma_i beta_(i+1). (11)\n\nThese include ALL original pairs, with no hidden zeros or identifications.\nFor1<=i<r put\n\n S_i=sum_(k<i)h_k gamma_k,\n D_i=S_i+sigma_i gamma_(i-1)>0,\n delta_gamma_i=gamma_i-gamma_(i-1),\n delta_p_i=p_(i+1)-p_i,\n N_i=h_i R_i^2-(sigma_i-2t_i)delta_p_i\n    =R_i^2 h_i[(sigma_i+2)t_i-sigma_i]/(2t_i^2). The full backward\nmatching recursion is\n\n D_i y_i=N_i+delta_gamma_i sum_(j>i)sigma_j y_j,\n                                    i=r-1,...,1. The outgoing\nsum to later nodes, including r, is\n\n (sigma_i-t_i)xi_i+delta_gamma_i V_i,\n V_i=sum_(i<j<r)(beta_j+t_j beta_(j+1)).\n\nThe incoming sums from earlier nodes and from later nodes are\n\n h_i alpha_(i-1)+gamma_(i-1)(beta_i+t_i beta_(i+1)),\n (gamma_(i-1)+t_i gamma_i)beta_(i+1)\n                                     +alpha_(i-1)+t_i alpha_i.\n\nSubtract them and use alpha_(i-1)=p_i-gamma_(i-1)beta_i. This gives\n\n d_i=(sigma_i-2t_i)xi_i+(S_i+2t_i gamma_(i-1))y_i\n                -2h_i p_i+delta_gamma_i(V_i-t_i beta_(i+1)).\n\nEquation (9) gives\nxi_i=delta_p_i+gamma_(i-1)y_i-delta_gamma_i beta_(i+1). Moreover,\n\n (sigma_i-t_i)beta_(i+1)-V_i\n =sum_(k>i)h_k(beta_(i+1)-beta_(k+1))\n =sum_(j>i)sigma_j y_j.\n\nSubstitution yields (13) exactly when d_i=h_i. This removes ALL row-profile\nparameters from the numerator, including arbitrary sign changes of\ndelta_gamma_i. It is stronger than bounding one transfer term.\n\nThe source terms also telescope. From (12) and R_i=t_i R_(i+1),\n\n 2N_i=(sigma_i+2)R_i^2-(sigma_i-2t_i)R_(i+1)^2,\n sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i-1-t_i.\n\nConsequently\n\n 2sigma_i N_i=(1+sigma_i)^2 R_i^2\n                              -(1+sigma_(i+1))^2 R_(i+1)^2,\n E_i=[(1+sigma_i)^2 R_i^2-A^2]/2.                    (16)\n\nFor an explicit check of the first identity, use\n\n (1+sigma_(i+1))^2=(sigma_i-t_i)^2\n                    =sigma_i(sigma_i-2t_i)+t_i^2.\n\nTogether with (1+sigma_i)^2=sigma_i(sigma_i+2)+1 and\nR_i^2=t_i^2 R_(i+1)^2, the added terms cancel. This displays the\ncoefficient check without invoking a machine expansion.\n\nCombining (10) and (16) gives the original-entry identity\n\n sum_(j=i)^(r-1)sigma_j Lambda_(0,j)\n    =(2t_0 gamma_0/D_i) E_i, 1<=i<r. Nonnegative original multipliers therefore imply E_i>=0.\nTaking positive square roots in (16), and using\nR_i/A=product_(i<=k<r)t_k, proves (3).\n\nThis is not an assertion that suffixes inherit certificates. The proof\nretains their crossing contributions through the complete original flow\nand then uses the special form (1). It provides no factor-free interval\ntheorem and says nothing about arbitrary nonzero M_prim.\n\n## 5. (23)\n\nThis proves that the original fixed candidate is not a surviving array.\nThe whole-profile obstruction (20) is independent of this sharper\npair-specific calculation.\n\n## 7. This complete joint\nadds the original full-cone witness to the earlier draft414e7c5c10faff4e,\nwhose source file and shared evidence remain intact.\n\nAccepted inputs: fact04219554e41d9750, Sections10--11, for the full primitive\nidentity and actual primitive joins; fact1252b8823bb5a290 for interpolation\norientation. The C108 cursor1a13389962b4ba78 is awareness only. All new\nmatching and sign arguments needed below are reproduced jointly.\n\n## 1. Declared scope and results\n\nFor any integer r>=2 let h_i=1+t_i with t_i>0, 0<=i<r. Put\n\n s=sum_(i<r)h_i, A=1+s, H_i=sum_(k<i)h_k,\n sigma_i=sum_(i<=k<r)h_k, R_i=product_(k<i)t_k^(-1),\n p_i=(R_i^2-1)/2.\n\nThe empty product is1, so R_0=1 and p_0=0. All symbols in this document\nother than the cited project notation are local to C109. In particular\nsigma_i is a suffix step mass, not a benchmark denominator.\n\nThe family consists of kernels on the ORDERED domain i<j of the form\n\n K_ij=alpha_i+gamma_i beta_j, 0<=i<j<=r,                (1)\n\nwhere every gamma_i>0, 0<=i<r. The profile gamma is arbitrary and need\nnot be monotone, symmetric, or constant. The two channels need not\nindividually be certificates. Set beta_r=0 by the harmless gauge change\nbeta_j -> beta_j-beta_r, alpha_i -> alpha_i+gamma_i beta_r. Boundary\nentries K_(-1),j and K_i,(r+1) are0; t_r=0 is a terminal convention only.\nEvery original ordered coefficient is defined by\n\n mu_ij=K_ij-K_(i-1),j+t_j(K_i,(j+1)-K_(i-1),(j+1)),\n mu_ji=K_(i-1),j-K_(i-1),(j+1)+t_i(K_ij-K_i,(j+1)),\n Lambda_ij=2mu_ji, Lambda_ji=2mu_ij, Lambda_ii=0.       (2)\n\nThe conclusions proved jointly below are:\n\n* Strongest candidate-specific result (Section8): the exact C108 physical\n  word fails an original global cut. An explicit one-dimensional convex\n  differentiable1-smooth function, with every ordered interpolation row\n  and true minimum checked, has V_h<-184/7623. Thus no full certificate\n  for this word exists, even with arbitrary PSD remainder or kernel.\n\n* All original zero-remainder matching equations in this entire family\n  have an explicit backward solution, and its full sign test is stated.\n* Every NONNEGATIVE original array in this family must satisfy every\n  positive suffix product inequality\n\n       (1+sigma_i) product_(i<=k<r)t_k >=1, 1<=i<r.   (3)\n\n  A profile-independent telescoping invariant gives an explicit positive\n  weighted sum of original Lambda_(0,j) for each suffix. This is a\n  necessary condition only for the declared family, not the full cone.\n* The C108 word t=(a,b,a,a,b,a), a=29/70 and\n  a^4 b^2(7+4a+2b)=1, violates (3) at i=2. Consequently NO positive\n  profile gamma can repair it within (1)--(2). This closes its entire\n  positive-profile enlargement, not merely gamma=(1,1,2,4,1,1).\n* At the originally fixed profile gamma=(1,1,2,4,1,1), the specific\n  original coefficient Lambda_03 is strictly negative. No untallied\n  sign or PSD test is needed to reject that candidate.\n* If equality holds in (3), the corresponding column differences all\n  vanish and impose a strong tail rigidity. An explicit actual basic\n  length4 full M=0 certificate with a Silver3 suffix violates that\n  rigidity. Thus the positive-profile restriction is demonstrably not\n  lossless, even for full zero-remainder arrays.\n\nNo all-interval theorem, capacity cap for every word in this family,\ngeneral higher-rank obstruction, or original-target answer is asserted.\nThe separate factor-free interval question assigned to max2 is not used.\n\n## 2. Original full-space identity and adjacent matching\n\nRetain independent formal gradients g_0,...,g_r and values f_0,...,f_r.\nUse x_i=x_0-sum_(k<i)h_k g_k and the accepted residual orientation\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n Qbar_ij=-2Q_ji\n        =2(f_i-f_j)-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2.\n\nDefine\n\n V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)\n        +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sA||g_r||^2.\n\nFact04219554e41d9750 says that the original flow and full M_prim=0\ncondition are exactly the polynomial identity\n\n                  V_h=sum_(i!=j)mu_ij Qbar_ij.         (4)\n\nMore explicitly, e is the all-ones column, T has T_ii=1 and T_ij=h_j\nfor j<i and0 otherwise, L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, and\n\n L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,\n M_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r,-s(s+2))\n                   +(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2=0.       (5)\n\nFor arbitrary K, formulas (2) telescope to\n\n sum_(a<=i)mu_(a,j)=K_ij+t_j K_i,(j+1),\n sum_(a>j)mu_(a,i)=K_(i-1),(j+1)+t_i K_i,(j+1).       (6)\n\nFor i<j the coefficient of <g_i,g_j> in the right side of (4) is\n\n 2(mu_ij+mu_ji)-2h_i[K_ij+t_j K_i,(j+1)]\n                    +2h_j[K_(i-1),(j+1)+t_i K_i,(j+1)].\n\nAt j=r the last bracket is0, so its h_r factor can be omitted. Expanding\n(2) gives\n\n mu_ij+mu_ji=h_i K_ij+(t_j-t_i)K_i,(j+1)\n                                      -h_j K_(i-1),(j+1).\n\nThe mixed coefficient is0 because h_i t_j-h_j t_i=t_j-t_i. The boundary\ncase j=r follows by the same expansion with t_r=0 and h_r=1 only as\nalgebraic terminal conventions. No physical terminal step is added.\n\nLet d_i=sum_(j!=i)mu_ij-sum_(j!=i)mu_ji be the outgoing minus incoming\nmu flow. Its value coefficient is2d_i. For i<r its diagonal coefficient\nis\n\n -d_i-2sum_(j<i)mu_ji+2t_i sum_(j>i)mu_ji\n             =-d_i+2(t_i^2 K_i,(i+1)-K_(i-1),i).      (7)\n\nThe equality follows from (6), including the endpoint i=0. At the\nterminal index it is -d_r-2K_(r-1),r. Matching the original flow\nd_i=h_i (i<r), d_r=-s, and the diagonals of V_h therefore forces\n\n t_i^2 K_i,(i+1)-K_(i-1),i=(1-t_i^2)/2.\n\nStarting with K_(-1),0=0, this determines\n\n             K_(i-1),i=p_i, 1<=i<=r.                 (8)\n\nThe terminal diagonal also imposes 2p_r=s(s+2), or R_r=A since both\nare positive. Thus balance is necessary, not an omitted extra test.\nConversely, if (8), R_r=A, and all flows match, every coefficient of\n(4) matches on the full original space by (6)--(7).\n\n## 3. Complete matching for every positive profile\n\nSet gamma_(-1)=alpha_(-1)=0. Define\n\n y_i=beta_i-beta_(i+1), 1<=i<r,\n xi_i=alpha_i-alpha_(i-1), 0<=i<r.\n\nHere xi_i is a row-coefficient difference and is distinct from the\nphysical point x_i. Equation (8) and the gauge give\n\n beta_i=sum_(i<=j<r)y_j,\n alpha_i=p_(i+1)-gamma_i beta_(i+1).                  (9)\n\nFor every 0<=i<j<r and every0<=i<r, respectively, (2) becomes\n\n mu_ij=h_j xi_i+(gamma_i-gamma_(i-1))\n                                      *(beta_j+t_j beta_(j+1)),\n mu_ji=(gamma_(i-1)+t_i gamma_i)y_j,                  (10)\n mu_ir=xi_i, mu_ri=alpha_(i-1)+t_i alpha_i.            (11)\n\nThese include ALL original pairs, with no hidden zeros or identifications.\nFor1<=i<r put\n\n S_i=sum_(k<i)h_k gamma_k,\n D_i=S_i+sigma_i gamma_(i-1)>0,\n delta_gamma_i=gamma_i-gamma_(i-1),\n delta_p_i=p_(i+1)-p_i,\n N_i=h_i R_i^2-(sigma_i-2t_i)delta_p_i\n    =R_i^2 h_i[(sigma_i+2)t_i-sigma_i]/(2t_i^2).       (12)\n\nAlso define S_r=sum_(k<r)h_k gamma_k and D_r=S_r. The full backward\nmatching recursion is\n\n D_i y_i=N_i+delta_gamma_i sum_(j>i)sigma_j y_j,\n                                    i=r-1,...,1.    (13)\n\nHere is a direct derivation retaining every contribution to d_i. The\noutgoing sum to earlier nodes is (S_i-gamma_(i-1))y_i. The outgoing\nsum to later nodes, including r, is\n\n (sigma_i-t_i)xi_i+delta_gamma_i V_i,\n V_i=sum_(i<j<r)(beta_j+t_j beta_(j+1)).\n\nThe incoming sums from earlier nodes and from later nodes are\n\n h_i alpha_(i-1)+gamma_(i-1)(beta_i+t_i beta_(i+1)),\n (gamma_(i-1)+t_i gamma_i)beta_(i+1)\n                                     +alpha_(i-1)+t_i alpha_i.\n\nSubtract them and use alpha_(i-1)=p_i-gamma_(i-1)beta_i. This gives\n\n d_i=(sigma_i-2t_i)xi_i+(S_i+2t_i gamma_(i-1))y_i\n                -2h_i p_i+delta_gamma_i(V_i-t_i beta_(i+1)).\n\nEquation (9) gives\nxi_i=delta_p_i+gamma_(i-1)y_i-delta_gamma_i beta_(i+1). Moreover,\n\n (sigma_i-t_i)beta_(i+1)-V_i\n =sum_(k>i)h_k(beta_(i+1)-beta_(k+1))\n =sum_(j>i)sigma_j y_j.\n\nSubstitution yields (13) exactly when d_i=h_i. Its positive denominators\ngive a unique backward solution. In particular no linear-system, PSD,\nor sign oracle is being left in the definition.\n\nThe endpoint flows also match when R_r=A. To prove this without an\nextra assumption, substitute the formal scalar data g_i=1 and\nx_i=f_i=-H_i into sum mu Qbar. Every Qbar is0. Using (7)--(8) yields\n\n sum_i d_i H_i=-(s^2+sum_(i<r)h_i^2)/2.               (14)\n\nIndeed the total diagonal is sum_(i<r)(1-t_i^2)-2p_r\n=-s^2-sum h_i^2, and sum d_i=0. The target vector\n(h_0,...,h_(r-1),-s) has the same moment, because\nsum_(i<r)h_i H_i=(s^2-sum h_i^2)/2. All interior flows already match.\nAny residual flow is therefore (z,0,...,0,-z), and its moment is -sz.\nSince s>0, equation (14) forces z=0. Equations (4)--(5) now hold on\nevery original formal coordinate.\n\nThe exact final sign test is\n\n xi_i>=0 for0<=i<r; y_j>=0 for1<=j<r;\n h_j xi_i+delta_gamma_i(beta_j+t_j beta_(j+1))>=0\n                                     for0<=i<j<r.   (15)\n\nNecessity of the first two sets follows from mu_ir=xi_i and\nmu_(j,0)=t_0 gamma_0 y_j. If they hold then alpha_i=sum_(k<=i)xi_k>=0,\nso all reverse terminal coefficients in (11) are nonnegative. The other\nreverse coefficients in (10) are nonnegative because gamma and t are\npositive. The last set of (15) is exactly the remaining forward entries.\nThus (15) is sufficient as well. A positive delta_gamma_i makes its\nlast-set entries automatic, but none is deleted from the definition.\n\n## 4. A profile-independent suffix invariant in original multipliers\n\nFor1<=i<=r define\n\n F_i=sum_(i<=j<r)sigma_j y_j, F_r=0,\n E_i=sum_(i<=j<r)sigma_j N_j, E_r=0.\n\nThe definitions give\n\n D_(i+1)=D_i+sigma_i delta_gamma_i.\n\nMultiplying (13) by sigma_i and adding D_i F_(i+1) therefore gives\n\n D_i F_i=sigma_i N_i+D_(i+1)F_(i+1).\n\nBackward induction proves D_i F_i=E_i. This removes ALL row-profile\nparameters from the numerator, including arbitrary sign changes of\ndelta_gamma_i. It is stronger than bounding one transfer term.\n\nThe source terms also telescope. From (12) and R_i=t_i R_(i+1),\n\n 2N_i=(sigma_i+2)R_i^2-(sigma_i-2t_i)R_(i+1)^2,\n sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i-1-t_i.\n\nConsequently\n\n 2sigma_i N_i=(1+sigma_i)^2 R_i^2\n                              -(1+sigma_(i+1))^2 R_(i+1)^2,\n E_i=[(1+sigma_i)^2 R_i^2-A^2]/2.                    (16)\n\nFor an explicit check of the first identity, use\n\n (1+sigma_(i+1))^2=(sigma_i-t_i)^2\n                    =sigma_i(sigma_i-2t_i)+t_i^2.\n\nTogether with (1+sigma_i)^2=sigma_i(sigma_i+2)+1 and\nR_i^2=t_i^2 R_(i+1)^2, the added terms cancel. This displays the\ncoefficient check without invoking a machine expansion.\n\nCombining (10) and (16) gives the original-entry identity\n\n sum_(j=i)^(r-1)sigma_j Lambda_(0,j)\n    =(2t_0 gamma_0/D_i) E_i, 1<=i<r.                (17)\n\nEvery weight sigma_j and the prefactor on the right are strictly\npositive. Nonnegative original multipliers therefore imply E_i>=0.\nTaking positive square roots in (16), and using\nR_i/A=product_(i<=k<r)t_k, proves (3).\n\nThis is not an assertion that suffixes inherit certificates. The proof\nretains their crossing contributions through the complete original flow\nand then uses the special form (1). It provides no factor-free interval\ntheorem and says nothing about arbitrary nonzero M_prim.\n\n## 5. Exact rejection for EVERY positive profile of the C108 word\n\nSet a=29/70 and let b be the unique positive solution of\n\n                a^4 b^2(7+4a+2b)=1.                 (18)\n\nThe left side is strictly increasing on b>0, starts at0, and tends to\ninfinity, so this definition is unambiguous. Let\n\n        t=(a,b,a,a,b,a), A=7+4a+2b.\n\nEquation (18) gives R_6=A. For the lower bound on b, at b=47/28 the\ncapacity is841/70 and\n\n 70^5*28^2-29^6*47^2=3704083911>0.\n\nThus b>47/28>5/3 and A>841/70>12 (also A>1201/100). Original identity and actual projection functions\n\nNormalize smoothness to1. For a word of length n, retain every original\nsample and write\n\n s=sum_(i<n)h_i, A=1+s,\n x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)\n       +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nThe optimum is x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Every actual smooth convex\ntrajectory satisfies all original Q_ij<=0, including both orientations\nof the terminal and optimum comparisons, by1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nAccepted04219554e41d9750, Section10, gives the following identity under\nthe full original flow equations:\n\n V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij\n     =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>.                         (1)\n\nHere Lambda has zero diagonal and nonnegative off-diagonal entries,\nL_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, e is the all-ones column,\nL_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T, and\n\n T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,\n D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)),\n M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.\n\nThus an actual negative V_h contradicts every such M_prim>=0 on the\nwhole R^(n+1). This implication needs no primitive dual-attainment or\ncompleteness assertion. No formal coordinate is identified or removed.\n\nFor a compact convex set K containing0, define\n\n F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.                    (2)\n\nCompleting the square gives the unique maximizer g=P_K(x), the Euclidean\nprojection onto K. The projection normal condition is\n\n <x-P_K(x),v-P_K(x)><=0 for all v in K.\n\nAdding this condition at two points gives\n||P_K(x)-P_K(y)||^2<=<P_K(x)-P_K(y),x-y>; hence P_K is1-Lipschitz.\nThe two maximizing inequalities give\n\n 0<=F_K(x+z)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),z>\n      <=<P_K(x+z)-P_K(x),z><=||z||^2.\n\nTherefore F_K is convex and differentiable, with 1-Lipschitz gradient\nP_K. If0 is interior to K, F_K(0)=0 and F_K(x)>0 for x!=0: substitute\na sufficiently small positive multiple of x in(2). Thus0 is its unique\nminimizer.\n\nAt any such projection transcript put y_i=x_i-g_i and\nf_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Direct expansion gives\n\n Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j>. Since0 belongs to K,\nthe optimum comparisons are included, with Q_(i,star)=0.\n\nThe GD norm telescope is\n\n ||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2\n     =2h_i f_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.\n\nConsequently every trajectory of(2) has\n\n V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n+s g_n||^2. The function\nsatisfies f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2 at all these points, so the same telescope\nas(4) applies. Since x_5+s40=1+a40 b40,\n\n V_h=1-(1+a40 b40)^2<0.                                (7)\n\nNow suppose D40>B40 C40. Put\n\n Kappa40=B40^2 C40+s40(D40-B40 C40)>0,\n t40=B40^2 C40/Kappa40 in(0,1),\n u40=(1,0), w40=(t40,sqrt(t40(1-t40))),\n v40=-(B40 u40+D40 w40)/b40,\n K40=conv{u40,v40,w40}.\n\nThe vectors u40,w40 are independent. The positive relation\nB40 u40+b40 v40+D40 w40=0 proves0 is interior to the triangle K40.\nUse F_K40 from(2), starting at x_0=u40. The actual proposed gradients\nare\n\n (g_0,...,g_5)=(u40,v40,w40,w40,w40,w40),\n\nand the proposed positions are\n\n x_0=u40, x_1=-B40 u40, x_2=D40 w40,\n x_3=(D40-1)w40, x_4=(a40+1)w40, x_5=w40.             (8)\n\nEvery physical GD update holds, by the defining relation for v40.\nIt remains to check all projection normals. We have\n\n ||u40||^2=1, <u40,w40>=||w40||^2=t40,\n <u40,v40>=-(B40+D40 t40)/b40,\n <v40,w40>=-(B40+D40)t40/b40.\n\nAt x_0 the normal is zero. At x_1 the normal y_1=-B40 u40-v40 obeys\n\n <w40-v40,y_1>\n     =[-B40^2 C40+Kappa40 t40]/b40^2=0,\n <u40-v40,y_1>=-B40(1-t40)C40/b40<0.                  (9)\n\nTo check the coefficient in the first equality without an omitted\nidentity, substitute v40 and the displayed inner products. Its t40\ncoefficient is\n\n D40(2B40+D40)+b40(D40-B40 D40+B40)-B40 b40^2\n  =B40^2(b40-1)\n      +(B40+b40+D40)(D40-B40(b40-1))\n  =Kappa40,\n\nwhere B40+b40+D40=s40. At every later query the normal is a\nnonnegative multiple of w40, whose pairing with u40-w40 is zero and\nwhose pairing with v40-w40 is\n-t40[1+(B40+D40)/b40]<0. This proves every normal in(8), not only the\ntwo-step prefix. Formula(3) gives all ordered interpolation inequalities\nincluding the optimum and the terminal sample. The initial distance to\nthe unique minimizer is exactly1.\n\nBy(4), the exact energy is\n\n V_h=1-A40^2 t40\n     =-s40 Delta40/Kappa40,\n Delta40=B40 C40[1+(A40+1)B40]-D40.                    (10)\n\nThe last equality follows by expanding\nA40^2 B40^2 C40-Kappa40=s40 Delta40, using A40=s40+1.\nThus Delta40<=0 is necessary in the triangle regime. We next show the\nopposite sign throughout the entire larger-endpoint region.\n\nWrite\n\n Phi40(A,B)=B(A/2-B-3)[1+(A+1)B]-(A/2+1),\n\nso Delta40=Phi40(A40,B40). Hence(10)--(11) give\n\n V_h<-909/200000<-1/250.                               (12)\n\nNo numerical test, sign-unchecked array, or restriction on the PSD\nremainder has been used.\n\n## 5. The smaller-endpoint region a40<8/5\n\nAssume all global cuts pass and a40<8/5. Then\n\n 157/40<=P40<25/6, 49/20<R40<13/5,\n 29/4<T40<473/60<8. (15)\n\nThere is no numerical parameter selection: the angle and all coefficients\nare fixed exact expressions throughout the entire surviving region.\n\n### 5.1 The triangle contains the true minimizer\n\nSolving w40=-alpha40 u40-beta40 v40 in(15) gives\n\n beta40=3mu40-(4/5)nu40,\n alpha40=eta40[3mu40-(6/5)nu40].\n\nBounds(14) give\n\n 11/25<beta40<77/100,\n 0<alpha40<159/500<11/25<beta40.\n\nIn detail,3mu40-(6/5)nu40>1-21/25=4/25, and\nalpha40<(3/5)(5/4-18/25)=159/500.
