The original all-horizon,\nall-nonnegative-word exact-profile problem remains OPEN.\n\n## 1. Scope, accepted inputs, and the changed law\n\nThe former T1/T2 laws held the gradient constant inside each original H2\nhalf. The law here replaces that assumption by affine projection motion\non an exposed segment: the tangent component changes at every physical\nquery. A remote support condition obstructs an orthogonal-face version.\nAn actual triangle then realizes the correct face transition, with every\nsubsequent physical query given explicitly. The latter construction is\nlegal for every enclosing integer m>=100, but its entire specified\nfamily is quantitatively weaker than the complete same-word references.\n\nThe accepted mathematical inputs are:\n\n* fact1252b8823bb5a290: necessity of every ordered smooth-convex\n  interpolation inequality, including the optimum; its proof also gives\n  the normalization used here.\n* factb98bb807d514e80d: the full K_n reference and the exact all-split\n  benchmark estimate B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3).\n* fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a: the complete alternating-ray maximum F_ray_n,\n  with K_n<=F_ray_n.\n* factdfde2fa326c908bf: the FULL schedule-dependent complex cycle\n  domain D_h and its payoff Gap_h. We use a legal point in this full\n  domain to bound its complete supremum from below.\n\nAccepted e9a55f57461f253c was read in its stated finite scope: actual H6,\nepsilon1/6, dimension66, unique minimizer0. Its dimension is not its\nradius, and it supplies no changing-enclosing-m child or asymptotic\ntransfer. The accepted finite triangle62a7d9329121fd75 concerns the\ndifferent word(2,2,1); it is not used as an iteration premise here.\n\nShared frozen diagnostic9a299b9a2f951782 was read without rerunning it.\nIt reports substantial internal H2 changes and asymmetric directed\nslacks, with H6 labeled optimal_inaccurate. Those observations motivate\nthe changed mechanism only. No floating-point equality, exact rank or\nnew-m law is inferred. Earlier unverified T1/T2 drafts are not proof\npremises; all new calculations used below occur in this joint draft.\n\nThe supporting literature search returned Theorem4 of Jai Moondra,\nHassan Mortagy and Swati Gupta, \"Reusing Combinatorial Structure: Faster\nIterative Projections over Submodular Base Polytopes,\" arXiv:2106.11943,\nv3(2023). Its general-polytope portion distinguishes tangent feasibility\nfrom the normal-cone condition. The PDF was downloaded, and Section3,\nLemma1, Theorem4 and its proof were read through browser-extracted text\nafter the local extractor failed. Here both conditions are proved\ndirectly from the three vertices; no external theorem is a proof premise.\n\n## 2. Original words and local notation\n\nFix an integer m>=100 and use the SAME enclosing parameters in every\nchild:\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/m, beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1.\n\nThus the actual first seven steps are\n\n    (ell,1+beta,ell,1+b,ell,1+beta,ell),\n    ell=1+a, b=beta*c.\n\nThe following are local scalars, denoted A48,eta48,b48,k48,r48,chi48,\nd48 in the shared glossary when necessary:\n\n    A=1+sum H2=4+2a+beta,\n    eta=a^2*beta,\n    k=A/(b*eta),\n    r=sqrt(b(b+1)/(A(k-1))),\n    chi=1+(A-1)(b+1)/(b*k+1),\n    d=chi-ell.                                             (1)\n\nIn particular k is not a horizon, a checkpoint count, or K_n; b is a\nbridge EXCESS, not the original benchmark B_n. Define the partial sums\nand tangent coefficients of the actual H2 by\n\n    (p0,p1,p2,p3)=(0,ell,ell+1+beta,A-1),\n    (t0,t1,t2,t3)=(1,-a,a*beta,-eta).                       (2)\n\nFor any integer i>=0, s2(i) is its number of binary1 digits, with\ns2(0)=0. For a positive integer j, nu2(j) is the exponent of2 dividing\nj. These are combinatorial definitions, not numerical routines run here.\n\nAll vectors and inner products below are Euclidean. An exposed-face\nnormal q means that the relevant face lies in the hyperplane\n<q,v>=||q||^2 and the whole convex set lies in its lower halfspace.\nThe tangent vector w is perpendicular to q. A core reset means x=g\nat the stated physical query, with optimum x_star=g_star=f_star=0.\n\n## 3. Uniform bounds on the actual enclosing parameters\n\nAll inequalities in this section hold for every m>=100. Squaring positive\nrationals gives\n\n    12/5<lambda<29/12,\n    99/100<=beta<1,\n    12/5<c<(29/12)(101/100)=2929/1200<5/2.\n\nConsequently\n\n    2/5<a<5/12,\n    19/8<b=lambda(1-epsilon^2)<5/2,\n    23/4<A<6,\n    99/625<eta<25/144,\n    b*eta=beta^3*a<5/12.                                  (3)\n\nFor the lower a bound, (99/100)/(2929/1200)=1188/2929>2/5.\nFor the lower b bound, (12/5)(9999/10000)>19/8.\nThe other bounds follow by substitution; for example\nA>4+4/5+99/100=579/100>23/4. In particular k>A and k>1.\n\nUsing b*k=A/eta rewrites chi as\n\n    chi=1+eta(b+1)(A-1)/(A+eta).                           (4)\n\nThe middle ratio satisfies\n\n    4/5<(A-1)/(A+eta)<5/6.\n\nThe first inequality is equivalent to A>5+4eta, which follows from\nA>23/4 and 5+4eta<5+25/36<23/4. For the second,\n(A-1)/(A+eta)<(A-1)/A<5/6 because A<6. Thus\n\n    1+2673/6250<chi<1+875/1728,\n    0<d<1/8, chi>17/12>sqrt(2).                           (5)\n\nFor positivity, 2673/6250>5/12 and ell<17/12. For the upper bound,\nell>7/5 and 875/1728<21/40, so chi-ell<1/8.\nThese estimates use rational inequalities on the actual parameters,\nnot continuity from a limiting word.\n\n## 4. Exact moving-face H2 block and its remote obstruction\n\nSuppose q is a face normal, w perpendicular to q, and the four proposed\nprojections q+t_i w all lie in that face. Put\n\n    x_i=(A-p_i)q+t_i w,\n    g_i=q+t_i w,\n    y_i=x_i-g_i=(A-1-p_i)q,       0<=i<=3.                 (6)\n\nSince t_(i+1)=(1-h_i)t_i for the three actual H2 steps, subtracting\nh_i*g_i in(6) gives x_(i+1). The gradient changes at every one of these\nsteps whenever w!=0. Every normal reserve A-1-p_i is nonnegative, and\ny3=0. Thus, if the full support conditions hold, these are actual\nprojections throughout the block, with the final core reset\n\n    x3=g3=q-eta*w.                                        (7)\n\nThis replaces constant gradients by tangent reflection and contraction;\nit does not replace H2 by one fictitious large step.\n\nNow consider TWO such consecutive original H2 blocks, with respective\nnormal/tangent pairs (q0,w0) and(q1,w1), each ending in a core reset.\nThe intervening original step is1+b, so exact GD requires\n\n    -b(q0-eta*w0)=A*q1+w1,  <qj,wj>=0.                   (8)\n\nThe putative gradients at the original indices0 and4 are q0+w0 and\nq1+w1. The residual at4 is (A-1)q1. Dotting(8) with q1 gives\n\n    <q1,w0>=A||q1||^2/(b*eta)+<q1,q0>/eta.\n\nHence the ORIGINAL directed interpolation residual for the natural\nprojection values f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 is\n\n Q_(0,4)=(A-1)[(1+1/eta)<q0,q1>\n                         +(A/(b*eta)-1)||q1||^2].        (9)\n\nTo check the convention, direct expansion of these values gives\nQ_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>, where Q_ij is exactly the signed residual in\naccepted1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nIf q0 and q1 are orthogonal and q1!=0, then\n\n    Q_(0,4)=(A-1)(k-1)||q1||^2>0.                        (10)\n\nThus this full-dimensional orthogonal-normal family fails a retained\noriginal row. Tangent directions and additional dimensions are arbitrary.\nNo bound on their norms repairs(10). The zero q1 exception is a quadratic\ncore block, rather than a nonzero exposed-face normal.\n\nMore generally a necessary condition is\n\n    <q0,q1><=-(A-b*eta)/(b(1+eta))*||q1||^2\n             <-(7/4)||q1||^2,       q1!=0.               (11)\n\nIndeed A-b*eta>23/4-5/12=16/3 and\nb(1+eta)<(5/2)(1+25/144)=845/288. Their ratio exceeds\n1536/845>7/4. This is a necessary condition on two particular\ncore-reset blocks separated by the low bridge; it is not a general\nangle obstruction for arbitrary projection trajectories.\n\nArbitrary function values do not repair this particular geometry when\nboth core resets and the stated optimum are retained. At a core sample\nx=g, the two optimum inequalities in1252b8823bb5a290 force\nf=||g||^2/2. For any two samples within one block, (6) gives\n\n    <g_i-g_j,x_i-x_j>=||g_i-g_j||^2.\n\nThus Q_ij+Q_ji=0 independently of the values. If both inequalities are\nnonpositive, both are equalities. Their differences, together with the\nvalue forced at the block's core sample, uniquely determine\nf_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 at every sample of that block. Apply this to\nthe blocks ending at3 and7. Formula(9) is therefore forced for EVERY\nvalue assignment compatible with all interpolation inequalities.\n\nThe obstruction is not an adjacent-row failure. With these forced values,\nevery ordered pair within either individual block has Q=0. Across the\nbridge, Q_(4,3)=0 and\n\n    Q_(3,4)=-(A-1)(A/b+1)||q1||^2<0.\n\nAll optimum rows have Q_(i,star)=0 and\nQ_(star,i)=-(A-1-p_i)||qj||^2<=0 in block j. Thus adjacent and optimum\nrows all pass even though(10) is positive.\n\n## 5. One actual triangle, with its actual first face transition\n\nThe illegal orthogonal second face suggests the following single static\nconvex set, for which we follow the TRUE projection instead. In the plane\nlet\n\n    U=(1,k*r), V=(1,-a*k*r), L=(-b,r),\n    C48=conv{U,V,L},\n    F48(x)=max_(v in C48)[<x,v>-||v||^2/2],\n    X0=(A,k*r).                                          (12)\n\nAll three vertices and the starting point are finite explicit vectors\ndepending only on m. Here L is a vertex label; the smoothness constant\nis1 until the final scaling paragraph. The defining maximum is one\nfixed function, not a Gram feasibility oracle.\n\nThe triangle is the intersection of the three halfspaces\n\n    x <=1,\n    -(k-1)r*x+(b+1)y <=(b*k+1)r,\n    -(a*k+1)r*x-(b+1)y <=(a*b*k-1)r.                    (13)\n\nThe first line is tight at U,V, the second at U,L and the third at V,L.\nThe third right side is positive: a*b*k=A/(a*beta)>69/5>1 by(3).\nAll three inequalities are strict at0, so0 is an interior point of C48.\n\nFor completeness, a maximizer defining F48 exists by compactness and\nis unique by strict concavity. It is the Euclidean projection P_C48(x),\nsince the objective is ||x||^2/2-||x-v||^2/2. The projection condition is\n\n    <x-g,v-g><=0 for every v in C48.                     (14)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating squared distance along each segment\nfrom g to v; sufficiency follows by expanding ||x-v||^2. Applying(14)\nat x and x' and adding gives\n\n    <x-x',P_C48(x)-P_C48(x')>>=||P_C48(x)-P_C48(x')||^2.\n\nCauchy--Schwarz proves the projection is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two\nmaximizing objectives gives\n\n    0<=F48(x+z)-F48(x)-<P_C48(x),z><=||z||^2.\n\nThis proves differentiability with gradient P_C48, as well as convexity\nand global1-smoothness. Since0 belongs to the triangle, F48>=0 and\nF48(0)=0. If x!=0, an adequately small positive multiple of x belongs\nto the triangle's interior and makes <x,v>-||v||^2/2 positive. Therefore\n\n    argmin F48={0}, min F48=0,\n    dist(X0,argmin F48)^2=A^2+k^2*r^2.                   (15)\n\nFor i=0,1,2,3 define\n\n    Xi=(A-p_i,t_i*k*r), Gi=(1,t_i*k*r).                 (16)\n\nEach Gi lies in the vertical segment UV: the four t_i lie between\n-a and1, since eta=a^2*beta<a and 0<a*beta<1. Also\n\n    Xi-Gi=(A-1-p_i)(1,0).\n\nThe first halfspace in(13) proves(14) for every vertex and hence every\npoint of C48. Thus all four Gi are the ACTUAL projections. The three\nindividual GD updates follow from(2), and the true values are\n\n    F48(Xi)=||Gi||^2/2+(A-1-p_i), 0<=i<=3.              (17)\n\nIn particular X3=G3=(1,-A*r/b). The original fourth step1+b gives\n\n    X4=-b*X3=(-b,A*r).                                  (18)\n\nThe orthogonal-face guess would prescribe G4=L=(-b,r), with residual\n(0,(A-1)r). The old gradient U invalidates its normal condition by\n\n    <U-L,X4-L>=(A-1)(k-1)r^2>0.                        (19)\n\nThis is the concrete instance of(10), at the actual physical indices0,4.\nWe do not use L as the true gradient at4.\n\nLet n_plus=(-(k-1)r,b+1), and D_plus=(b*k+1)r. The aspect ratio in(1)\nmakes X4 a positive multiple of n_plus. Moreover\n\n    <n_plus,X4>/D_plus\n      =[b(k-1)+A(b+1)]/(b*k+1)=chi.\n\nThe perpendicular foot on the upper supporting line is therefore\n\n    q=X4/chi.                                           (20)\n\nIt lies strictly inside segment LU. Indeed the orthogonal projection\nparameter of X4 on its line, measured from L toward U, is\n\n    theta=(A-1)(k-1)r^2/[(b+1)^2+(k-1)^2*r^2],\n    0<theta<(A-1)/(k-1)<1.                              (21)\n\nIts residual is a positive multiple of n_plus, so the second halfspace\nin(13) proves(14) against the WHOLE triangle, including the remote V.\nThus G4=q is the actual gradient, and\n\n    Y4=X4-G4=(chi-1)q,\n    F48(X4)=(chi-1/2)||q||^2.                            (22)\n\nThis is a real transition from the vertical exposed segment to the\nsloping exposed segment. The two first gradients U,V are noncollinear;\nthe law has not become a constant-H2 transcript in different notation.\n\n## 6. The exact full trajectory after the transition\n\nThe next physical step is h4=ell, and(5),(20) give\n\n    X5=X4-ell*G4=d*q, 0<d<1/8.                         (23)\n\nWe prove a sufficiently large segment of the line through q lies inside\nthe triangle. The point q itself belongs to C48. The point -q/2 also\nbelongs to C48, by all three original halfspaces(13). For the right\nhalfspace its horizontal coordinate is\n\n    b/(2chi)<(5/2)/(14/5)=25/28<1.\n\nFor the upper halfspace its inner product with n_plus is -D_plus/2.\nFor the lower halfspace its left side is\n\n    [A(b+1)-b(a*k+1)]r/(2chi)\n        <=A(b+1)r/(2chi)<15r/2,\n\nwhile the right side is (a*b*k-1)r>64r/5. This proves\n\n    [-q/2,q] subset C48.                                 (24)\n\nWe retain every physical step in the remaining word. The defining ruler\nrecursion gives, for 0<=t<n,\n\n    h_t=1+beta*c^(nu2(t+1)-1).\n\nOne way to verify the identity is induction on H_j: the center position\nt+1=2^j has valuation j, and adding2^j to each right-child position\ndoes not change its smaller valuation. For i>=0,\n\n    sum_(j=1)^i nu2(j)=sum_(ell>=1)floor(i/2^ell)=i-s2(i).\n\nThe second equality follows by writing i=sum_l b_l*2^l, with b_l in\n{0,1}, and summing b_l*(2^l-1). Therefore the exact physical prefix\nproduct is\n\n    P_i:=product_(t<i)(1-h_t)=(-1)^i beta^i c^(-s2(i)).    (25)\n\nIn particular P5=-beta^5*c^(-2), and all these products are nonzero.\nFor every 5<=i<=n set\n\n    Ti=(-1)^(i-5)*d*beta^(i-5)*c^(2-s2(i)),\n    Xi=Gi=Ti*q, F48(Xi)=Ti^2||q||^2/2.                   (26)\n\nFor i>=5, s2(i)>=1 and beta^(i-5)<=1, so\n\n    |Ti|<=d*c<5/16<1/2.\n\nEquation(24) proves Xi lies in C48. Its projection is consequently\nitself, giving exactly the gradient and value in(26). Equation(25)\nthen proves Xi+1=(1-h_i)Xi for every i>=5. This establishes(26) by\ninduction starting from(23); it is not a hypothetical quadratic tail.\n\nAll physical queries inside every later H2 block have changing nonzero\ngradients: h_i>1 makes the scalar in(26) change sign at each step, and\nno factor vanishes. In the first H2, changes arise from tangent motion\non the face; in the later blocks, they arise inside the triangle's\nquadratic core. Both coordinate gradients continue changing. Dimension\nis exactly2 and is not used as a substitute for the actual radius(15).\n\n## 7. All remote and optimum interpolation rows\n\nSet X_star=G_star=0 and f_star=0, and at every finite index use the\nsingle actual value f_i=<Xi,Gi>-||Gi||^2/2 established in(17),(22),(26).\nDirect expansion yields\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<Gi,Xj-Xi>+||Gi-Gj||^2/2\n        =<Gi-Gj,Yj>,       Yj=Xj-Gj.                    (27)\n\nEvery finite Gi is in the fixed triangle: (16) handles0 through3,\n(20)--(21) handle4, and(24)--(26) handle the full remaining word.\nFor j<=3, Yj is a nonnegative horizontal normal, so the first\nhalfspace(13) makes(27) nonpositive for EVERY i. For j=4, Y4 is a\npositive multiple of n_plus, so the second halfspace does the same.\nFor j>=5, Yj=0 and every residual with this target index is zero. The optimum gradient0\nalso lies in the triangle; Y_star=0. Thus both optimum directions and\nevery remote pair, including both terminal directions, are covered\nsimultaneously by the same data. No directed row has been dropped.\n\n## 8. Exact terminal gap, true cost, and complete comparisons\n\nSince n=2^m-1, s2(n)=m and n-5 is even. Equations(15),(26) give the\nexact true-distance payoff of this actual function:\n\n V48(m):=2(F48(Xn)-min F48)/dist(X0,argmin F48)^2\n       =d^2 beta^(2(n-5)) c^(4-2m)\n          *(b^2+A^2*r^2)/[chi^2(A^2+k^2*r^2)].          (28)\n\nLet Qunit(m)=P_n^2=beta^(2n)c^(-2m), the exact payoff of the scalar\nunit quadratic on the SAME original word with initial distance1.\nThen\n\n    V48(m)/Qunit(m)=d^2*c^4*beta^(-10)*||q||^2/||X0||^2.\n\nHere\n\n    ||q||^2/||X0||^2\n      =(b^2+A^2*r^2)/[chi^2(A^2+k^2*r^2)]<1/8.          (29)\n\nTo prove(29), its numerator-to-denominator ratio before chi^2 is a\nweighted average of (b/A)^2 and(A/k)^2. Both are less than1/4:\nb/A<10/23<1/2, and A/k=b*eta<5/12<1/2. Also chi^2>2 by(5).\nBernoulli's inequality, proved by induction from(1-u)^(j+1)\n>=(1-ju)(1-u)>=1-(j+1)u whenever its induction lower bound is used,\ngives beta^10>=(1-10/m)>=9/10. Consequently\n\n    V48(m)/Qunit(m)\n       <(1/64)(625/16)(10/9)(1/8)\n       =6250/73728<1/10.                                (30)\n\nAll quantities in(28) are positive. This closes the specified balanced\ntriangle family as a source of improvement, with an absolute margin.\n\nFor comparison with the complete K and ray references, put\nS=sum H_m and M=1+S. Summing the actual multiplicities gives\n\n    M=2^m+(beta/c)(c^m-2^m)/(c-2)\n     =(1-zeta)2^m+zeta*c^m,\n    zeta=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).                          (31)\n\nThe last inclusion follows from c>lambda and lambda(lambda-2)=1.\nThus M<c^m. It follows that\n\n    (2S+1)*Qunit(m)<2c^m*c^(-2m)=2c^(-m)<1.\n\nThe full reference of factb98bb807d514e80d is\n\n K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2sum h),\n            max_j (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+sum_(t<j)h_t)^2\n                                      *(1+2sum_(t>j)h_t)]}.\n\nIt includes the canonical Huber term. Fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a takes the\nmaximum over ALL feasible alternating-ray checkpoint sets, including\nzero and one cut, so its complete F_ray_n is at least this full K_n.\nTherefore, on exactly this original h and physical n,\n\n    0<V48(m)<Qunit(m)/10\n             <1/[10(2S+1)]<=K_n(h)/10<=F_ray_n(h)/10.   (32)\n\nThis comparison does not replace either full reference by a chosen cut;\nusing a lower bound on each complete reference is enough to prove the\ndisplayed domination.\n\nFor the full complex channel of factdfde2fa326c908bf, take z=1. Its\ncycle quantities satisfy alpha-|z|^2=0 and Im(z)=0, so EVERY cycle\ninequality defining the actual D_h holds. Both accepted payoff bounds\ncoincide, and hence\n\n    Gap_h(1)=|P_n|^2=Qunit(m),\n    0<V48(m)<(1/10)*sup_(z in D_h)Gap_h(z).              (33)\n\nThis is the FULL unaugmented complex reference, not just the universal\nlens or a short-cycle restriction. The Huber-augmented lens is also\nlarger than V48(m), by(32), without needing any lens optimization.\n\nFinally use the original all-split benchmark, without substituting a\nbalanced approximation. Accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives, for p=log_2 3,\n\n    B_n<=1+4n^p<5*3^m.\n\nCombining with(30), beta<1 and c>12/5 proves\n\n    0<B_n*V48(m)<(1/2)(3/c^2)^m\n                       <(1/2)(25/48)^m ->0.            (34)\n\nThe equality of actual and chosen minimizer distances is already proved\nin(15). For an arbitrary positive smoothness constant L_s, replace F48\nby L_s*F48 and use physical steps h_i/L_s. The positions, true radius\nand normalized payoff stay exactly(16),(18),(26),(28). This preserves\nthe project's W=2R convention. Equation(34) is a bound on this explicit\nfamily's payoff only, not an unrestricted upper bound on W_n(h).\n\n## 9. What the balanced choice closes, and what it leaves open\n\nThere are two distinct scoped conclusions in this joint draft.\n\nFirst, ANY two fixed-face H2 blocks with core resets, the intervening\nlow original bridge and orthogonal nonzero normals fail the original\nrow(0,4). All adjacent and optimum inequalities passing does not fix\nthat defect. Equation(11) gives the stronger necessary correlation.\nNo failure is asserted for arbitrary oblique normals, intermediate\nface changes, noncore endpoints, or other conjugate height laws.\n\nSecond, the explicit balanced-aspect triangle(12) is globally legal\nbut its true normalized payoff is less than one tenth of each complete\nsame-word reference in(32)--(33), and its original-B ratio tends to0.\nThis conclusion covers exactly the aspect ratio r in(1), arbitrary\npositive spatial scaling and orthogonal embeddings of that instance,\nand the objective scaling just described. It is not a claim about ALL\ntriangles, polytopes, or functions with changing gradients.\n\nOne useful exact interface survives if the aspect r>0 in(12) is released.\nKeep A,b,eta,k fixed, and define the same triangle and X0. All first-block\nformulas(16)--(19) remain valid. At4 the true projection is\n\n    g4=L+theta(U-L),\n    theta=(A-1)(k-1)r^2/[(b+1)^2+(k-1)^2*r^2].           (35)\n\nThis holds for every r>0: 0<theta<1; the residual is perpendicular\nto UL, and its dot product with n_plus has the positive sign because\n<n_plus,X4>-D_plus=(A-1)(b+1)r>0. Thus the residual is outward and\nthe remote V also passes. If q is the perpendicular foot of0 on the\nupper supporting line and w=g4-q, then\n\n    q perpendicular w, X4=chi*q+w,\n    X5=d*q-a*w.                                         (36)\n\nIn(36), chi and d are EXACTLY those of(1), independent of the aspect.\nThe projection at5 cannot lie in the RELATIVE INTERIOR of the old upper\nedge: its affine projection would have normal residual(d-1)q, pointing\ninward, since d<1/8. A projection in the relative interior of that edge\nrequires a nonnegative multiple of its outward normal. Equivalently the\nfirst original low step already uses more normal reserve than is\navailable on this face. This is an explicit normal-cone failure, not\njust an inequality on the desired terminal gap. Endpoint projections,\nwhich have larger normal cones, are not excluded by this argument.\n\nFor the balanced aspect w=0, (24)--(26) prove persistent core entry.\nFor a free aspect w need not vanish; the next point may instead hit\nanother face. No full-word estimate for those true further transitions\nis asserted. Such a law must follow the actual projections at queries\ninside H2, not hold the current edge for a whole block. This is a\nmaterially different remaining interface from the closed T1/T2 carriers.\n\nThe frozen internal-H2 observation has a further qualitative warning:\nmotion on one fixed face makes BOTH directed Q residuals within that\nface zero, as proved in Section4. The saved H6 diagnostics show a\nnearly tight forward row and a substantially slack reverse row. Raw\ndiagnostics are not exact identities, but there is no evidential basis\nfor imposing a single fixed face throughout each original H2.\n\nAn eventual stronger construction on H_m would still leave the original\nall-n/all-nonnegative-word lower comparison BLOCKED. That comparison is\na theorem-level requirement, not a routine induction. Numerical infeasibility is not an exact proof.\n\n## 1. Every\noriginal gradient remains formal and distinct. This is primitive\ncertificate feasibility, not full-objective PEP.\n\nBoth charged calibrations passed: h=(1) has Lambda01=2,Lambda10=4 and\nthe nonzero PSD matrix [[1,-1],[-1,1]]; h=(2) is exactly rejected by\nthe diagonal contradiction in Section2. The backend was existing Wolfram\nLanguage15.0.1/CSDP, tolerance10^-8. No numerical array or eigenvalue\nwas silently converted into a certificate or impossibility theorem.\nSections4--6 independently give exact actual-function rejections of f5c,\nf6c and f5a. The other five rows remain numerical rejection reports only.\n\nThere were10 charged cases:2 calibrations and8 distinct discovery words\nin2 families. Eight cases used one conic solve each; two used none.\nMeasured kernel construction/solve/readback time was1.972763 seconds.\nA conservative0.5-second export/local-postprocessing guard per case,\nwhich is not a claimed actual duration, makes6.972763 accounted seconds.\nDiscovery stopped at case10. With no credible reconstruction target,\nthe remaining2 slots and83.027237 seconds were retired and the ledger\nwas closed. No old ledger changed, and no correction retry occurred.\n\nAll exact witness work below is textual proof construction, with no\nadditional evaluator, numerical check, machine algebra or coefficient\nreconstruction job. No additional physical proposal is assessed here.\n\n## 2. Exact equivalence of the implemented original PSD model\n\nFor a word h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1))>=0 set s=sum h_i and A=1+s. Let e be\nthe all-ones column of length r+1, T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, with\nthe other entries zero. Set\n\n L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,\n D=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r,-s(s+2)),\n M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.\n\nThe original constraints are Lambda_ii=0, Lambda_ij>=0 for every i!=j,\nL^T e=omega, and M>=0 on all of R^(r+1).\n\nUnder the original flow, e^T D e=-sum h_i^2-s^2, while\n\n e^T LT e=omega^T T e\n   =-2sum_i h_i(1+sum_(t<i)h_t)+2s(1+s)\n   =sum_i h_i^2+s^2.\n\nThus e^T M e=0. If M is PSD then M e=0: for every real vector v,\nnonnegativity of (e+t v)^T M(e+t v) for both signs of sufficiently\nsmall real t forces v^T M e=0. Conversely, when M e=0, use the\ninvertible matrix P=[e_0,...,e_(r-1),e], where e_i are coordinate\ncolumns. The exact congruence is P^T M P=diag(M[0:r,0:r],0).\nConsequently the implemented equations M e=0 and the leading r-square\nPSD constraint are equivalent to the FULL original PSD constraint.\nThey impose no extra rank or support restriction. The original complete\nM, every sign and every flow row are retained in the raw readback.\n\nFor h=(1), the array [[0,2],[4,0]] gives flow(-2,2) and\nM=[[1,-1],[-1,1]], with quadratic(u-v)^2. For h=(2), writing\nLambda01=a>=0, flow gives Lambda10=a+4 and M=[[-a,2],[2,a-4]].\nPSD would force a<=0 and a>=4. This contradiction is independent of\nthe solver and completes the two calibration references.\n\n## 3. Full original identity and the actual-function witness method\n\nFor physical GD x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i with g_i=grad F(x_i), write\nf_i=F(x_i) and use the accepted orientation\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nThe primitive energy is\n\n V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)\n       +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_r||^2.\n\nThe cited accepted identity states, under the original flow,\n\n V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.\n\nTherefore any ACTUAL legal trajectory with V_h<0 excludes every\noriginal nonnegative Lambda with full PSD M: the left side would be\nnegative and the right side nonnegative. No support selection is needed.\n\nHere is a common complete construction for the planar witnesses.\nLet K be a compact convex set containing0 in its interior and define\n\n F(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nCompleting the square shows that the unique maximizing g is the\nEuclidean projection P_K(x). The two projection normal inequalities at\nx and x' imply ||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2<=<P_K(x)-P_K(x'),x-x'>, hence the\nprojection is1-Lipschitz. The maximum formulas bound the differentiability\nremainder between0 and ||P_K(x+z)-P_K(x)|| ||z||<=||z||^2. Thus F is\nconvex, differentiable and1-smooth, with grad F=P_K. It has unique\nminimum F(0)=0: for x!=0 a sufficiently small positive epsilon has\nepsilon x in K and gives F(x)>=epsilon(1-epsilon/2)||x||^2>0.\n\nIf the displayed g_i are projections, let y_i=x_i-g_i. Then\nf_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 and direct expansion gives\n\n Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j><=0\n\nfor EVERY ordered pair, by the projection normal inequality at j.\nThe optimum jet is x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Its two directions satisfy\nQ_(i,star)=0 and Q_(star,i)=-<g_i,y_i><=0 because0 belongs to K.\nThis specifies all remote, terminal and optimum rows, not just adjacent\nones. Telescoping ||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2 gives\n\n V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_r+s g_r||^2.\n\nIn the constructions below x_r=g_r, so V_h=||x_0||^2-A^2||g_r||^2.\nThe unique minimizer is0, hence the true squared initial distance is\nexactly ||x_0||^2 in every case.\n\nThe scalar screens used the interval version K=[-eta,1]: the derivative\nis clip(x,[-eta,1]). At a selected step b>1, let P=1+prefix sum and\nD=1+suffix sum, and choose eta=(b-1)/D. Start x_0=P. Gradients are1\nbefore and at b, then -eta through the terminal point x_r=-eta.\nEvery point is on the claimed constant-gradient side of this SAME\nfunction. Its energy is P^2-A^2(b-1)^2/D^2. Thus the original global\ncut is A(b-1)<=PD. Steps<=1 contribute a vacuous nonnegative test.\n\nThe other scalar screens use the exact convex quadratic F_z(x)=z x^2/2,\nz in{1/4,1/2,3/4,1}, x_0=1. With p_0=1 and\np_(i+1)=(1-z h_i)p_i, substitution gives\n\n V_h(z)=sum_i z h_i(1+z-z h_i)p_i^2\n                 -s z[1+A z]p_r^2.\n\nAt z=1 this reduces to1-A^2 product_i(1-h_i)^2. Testing those four\ncurvatures makes no assertion about every curvature or nonlinear law.\n\nFor completeness, the adjacent-pair screen is also an original actual\nfunction. Let the pair be b,c>1, put B=b-1,C=c-1,\nP=1+prefix sum,D=1+suffix sum,U=b+c+D-1. If D>BC, set\n\n kappa=B^2 C+U(D-BC), t=B^2 C/kappa,\n u=(1,0), w=(t,sqrt(t(1-t))), v=-(B u+D w)/c,\n K=conv{u,v,w}.\n\nThen0<t<1 and positive weights B,c,D express0 as an interior\nconvex combination. Prefix queries lie at positive multiples>=1 of u,\nthe two pair updates are u -> -B u -> D w, and suffix queries lie at\npositive multiples>=1 of w. Their gradients are respectively u,v,w.\nAt -B u, with y=-B u-v, the exact comparisons are\n\n <w-v,y>=[-B^2 C+kappa t]/c^2=0,\n <u-v,y>=-B(1-t)C/c<0.\n\nAt a prefix normal a u, a>=0, both comparisons to v-u and w-u are\nnonpositive. At a suffix normal a w, a>=0, the comparisons to u-w\nand v-w are0 and -a t[1+(B+D)/c]<=0. Thus Section3's full row formula\napplies and V_h=P^2-A^2 t, where A=P+U. Expansion gives\n\n A^2 B^2 C-P^2 kappa\n       =U{BC[P^2+2BP+BU]-P^2 D}.\n\nThe resulting necessary inequality is P^2 D>=BC[P^2+2BP+BU].\nThe evaluator also contains a stop at D<=BC, recalled from the earlier\nC80 draft. This document does not import that unverified branch: EVERY\napplicable adjacent pair of EVERY registered word had D>BC, as the\nretained exact raw outputs show. No D<=BC rejection occurred. Only the\nfully proved D>BC branch was used in this finite batch or in this proof.\n\n## 4. Exact scalar rejection of f5c\n\nTake h=(3/2,5/2,23/20,12/5,27/20), A=99/10. At physical index1,\nP=5/2,D=59/10 and eta=15/59. The scalar function is explicitly\n\n F(x)=x-1/2                    for x>=1,\n F(x)=x^2/2                    for -eta<=x<=1,\n F(x)=-eta*x-eta^2/2            for x<=-eta.\n\nIt is convex differentiable1-smooth, with unique minimizer0. The entire\ntrajectory is\n\n x=(5/2,1,-3/2,-285/236,-141/236,-15/59),\n g=(1,1,-15/59,-15/59,-15/59,-15/59).\n\nEach listed update uses its original physical step, and every point\nlies in the stated branch. The values are F(x_i); Section3 supplies\nall original and optimum interpolation residuals. The true squared\ndistance is25/4. The global cut slack is\n\n PD-A(b-1)=(5/2)(59/10)-(99/10)(3/2)=-1/10.\n\nThe exact original primitive energy is\n\n V_h=(5/2)^2-(99/10)^2(15/59)^2\n       =(295^2-297^2)/118^2=-296/3481<0.\n\nThus f5c cannot have any full original primitive certificate.\n\n## 5. Exact planar rejection of f6c\n\nTake h=(3/2,49/20,5/4,103/50,5/2,5/4), A=1201/100, and the\nadjacent pair at physical indices3,4. The parameters of Section3 are\n\n P=31/5, B=53/50, C=3/2, D=9/4, U=581/100,\n D-BC=33/50, kappa=138/25, t=2809/9200.\n\nUse u,w,v,K,F as in Section3; here v=-(53/125)u-(9/10)w.\nThe COMPLETE original trajectory and gradients are\n\n x=((31/5)u,(47/10)u,(9/4)u,u,-(53/50)u,(9/4)w,w),\n g=(u,u,u,u,v,w,w).\n\nEvery update is exact. Section3 checks each projection normal, every\nordered interpolation row and both optimum directions. Its unique\nminimum is0, with true squared initial distance961/25. The adjacent\npair slack is -2660367/500000. More directly, the actual energy is\n\n V_h=961/25-(1201/100)^2(2809/9200)\n       =-515224409/92000000<0.\n\nThis excludes every original42-entry/fullR7 certificate for f6c.\n\n## 6. Exact rejection of the screen-passing word f5a\n\nThis is a textual counterexample construction motivated by a numerical\nrejection, not a reconstruction of floating dual coefficients. Take\n\n h=(3/2,12/5,6/5,12/5,7/5), s=89/10, A=99/10,\n u=(1,0), v=(-2/5,3/10), w=(3/37,-839/4218),\n p=w+u+v, q=w+v, K=conv{p,q,w}.\n\nThe exact scalar products are\n\n ||u||^2=1, ||v||^2=1/4, <u,v>=-2/5,\n <w,u>=3/37, <w,v>=-7/76.\n\nPut alpha=1165/6327 and beta=4195/6327. Coordinate substitution gives\nw=-alpha u-beta v. The three barycentric coefficients alpha,\nbeta-alpha=3030/6327 and1-beta=2132/6327 are positive, so0 is interior\nto K. Section3 makes F(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2) a single\nglobally convex differentiable1-smooth function with unique minimum0.\n\nLet P=39/10, H=15/2 and start at\n\n x_0=A w+P u+H v=(63/37,987/3515).\n\nUse the original five steps and propose g=(p,p,q,q,w,w). Directly\nsubtracting P p+(18/5)q+(7/5)w from x_0 ends at x_5=w. The normals\ny_i=x_i-g_i are specified by\n\n y_1=(37/5)w+(7/5)u+5v,       y_0=y_1+(3/2)p,\n y_3=(19/5)w+(7/5)v,         y_2=y_3+(6/5)q,\n y_4=(7/5)w,                y_5=0.\n\nTheir complete two-direction table is\n\n| i | g_i | <y_i,u> | <y_i,v> |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 0 | p | 189/185 | -337/950 |\n| 1 | p | 0 | 4/475 |\n| 2 | q | -587/925 | 18/95 |\n| 3 | q | -233/925 | 0 |\n| 4 | w | 21/185 | -49/380 |\n| 5 | w | 0 | 0 |\n\nAt p the necessary vertex comparisons are <y,u>>=0 and\n<y,u+v>>=0. Thus every proposed gradient is\nindeed the projection at the corresponding physical iterate. All\noriginal Q_ij and optimum rows follow from Section3, with no suppressed\nsample or identification of formal gradient variables in the certificate.\n\nThe true squared initial distance is exactly\n(63/37)^2+(987/3515)^2>0. The actual primitive energy telescopes to\n\n V_h=||A w+P u+H v||^2-A^2||w||^2\n    =P^2-(4/5)PH+H^2/4+2A[(3/37)P-(7/76)H]\n    =2349/400-1042767/140600\n    =-434187/281200<0.\n\nConsequently f5a is exactly excluded from the entire original30-entry,\nfullR6 certificate cone, even with arbitrary nonzero PSD remainder.\nThis strengthens this ONE row beyond its numerical status.\n\nFor comparison, its complete original global-cut slacks were\n(69/20,57/50,1077/50,39/50,227/50)>0. The original Goal remains OPEN.\n\nThe new family below has its unit step at index1, independent endpoints,\nand two independent middle steps in a band of positive width. It is not\nthe previously considered reflected central-unit family. No reversal\nclosure, lossless reduction, multiplier symmetry, zero remainder, rank\nrestriction, or interpolation-edge restriction is used.\n\nThe only mathematical inputs from the fact graph are accepted\n04219554e41d9750, Section10 (the original primitive identity), and\n1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraph1 (necessary interpolation inequalities).\nAccepted4c92d3fc4eb6153e supplies the capacity motivation, not seed\nexistence. Every other argument used below is proved in this document;\nno earlier unverified finding is a predecessor.\n\n## 1. Original quantities and what a negative energy disproves\n\nFor any nonnegative length-n word h, normalize smoothness to1 and write\n\n s=sum_(i<n)h_i, A=1+s,\n x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)\n       +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nEvery actual smooth convex trajectory satisfies Q_ij<=0, including all\nremote, terminal, and optimum comparisons, by1252b8823bb5a290. We take\nthe optimum jet to be x_star=g_star=f_star=0.\n\nFor a full original multiplier array Lambda, let e be the all-ones\ncolumn, L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, and\n\n T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,\n D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)),\n M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.\n\nThe hypotheses on a certificate are Lambda_ii=0, Lambda_ij>=0 for all\ni!=j, and L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T. Accepted\n04219554e41d9750 gives the exact identity\n\n V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij\n     =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>.                         (1)\n\nHere the right side is the matrix trace inner product. If M_prim is\npositive semidefinite on the whole original R^(n+1), its pairing with\nan actual gradient Gram matrix is nonnegative. Therefore an actual\ntrajectory with V_h<0 excludes EVERY certificate of this form.\nFor n=5, all30 ordered multipliers remain free and all six formal\ngradients remain independent. No dual-attainment claim is needed.\n\n## 2. Static projection functions and the complete normal check\n\nFor a compact convex set K containing0, define\n\n F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.                    (2)\n\nThe unique maximizer is the Euclidean projection P_K(x), by completing\nthe square. It is characterized by\n\n <x-P_K(x),v-P_K(x)><=0 for every v in K.\n\nAdding this inequality at two points yields\n||P_K(x)-P_K(y)||^2<=<P_K(x)-P_K(y),x-y>, so P_K is1-Lipschitz.\nThe two maximizing inequalities imply\n\n 0<=F_K(x+z)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),z>\n      <=<P_K(x+z)-P_K(x),z><=||z||^2.\n\nThus F_K is convex, differentiable, and1-smooth, with gradient P_K.\nIf0 is interior to K, it is the unique minimizer: F_K(0)=0, and for\nx!=0 substituting a sufficiently small positive multiple of x in(2)\ngives F_K(x)>0.\n\nAt a proposed query whose gradient is g_i=P_K(x_i), put\n\n y_i=x_i-g_i, f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.\n\nDirect substitution in the definition of Q gives\n\n Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j>.                                    (3)\n\nHence checking the entire normal cone at EACH original query proves\nALL ordered inequalities at once. Comparisons to the optimum are also\nincluded since0 belongs to K; in particular Q_(i,star)=0.\n\nThe actual GD norm telescope is\n\n ||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2\n      =2h_i f_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.\n\nUsing the actual terminal value in V_h therefore gives\n\n V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n+s g_n||^2.                         (4)\n\nThis identity also applies to the scalar capped function below, since\nits values have the same expression x*g-g^2/2 at every query.\n\n## 3. The original global cuts come first\n\nFor any index j with h_j>1 define\n\n P=1+sum_(i<j)h_i, D=1+sum_(i>j)h_i, eta=(h_j-1)/D.\n\nTake K=[-eta,1] and x_0=P. Every query through j has gradient1,\nquery j equals1, and the step h_j lands at -D*eta. Every remaining\ngradient is -eta: the nonnegative later steps move monotonically on\nthe negative plateau, ending at x_n=-eta. These are actual projection\nqueries for the single static function(2), whose unique minimum is0.\nThe true initial distance is P>0. Formula(4) gives\n\n V_h=P^2-A^2(h_j-1)^2/D^2.\n\nConsequently every original certificate must satisfy, for EVERY j,\n\n A(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+sum_(i<j)h_i)(1+sum_(i>j)h_i).         (5)\n\nA failed cut already supplies an actual negative-energy witness. When\nwe analyze a word below, all cuts are first imposed; we need no further\ntest on a word already excluded by one of them.\n\nFor the first and last physical steps a,d of a length5 word, (5) gives\n\n a,d <= 2A/(A+1) <2,                                  (6)\n\nprovided A>1. If an endpoint is<=1, (6) is automatic; if it is>1,\nits cut rearranges to (A+1)a<=2A or (A+1)d<=2A.\n\n## 4. An adjacent-pair witness with every exterior query retained\n\nFix any two adjacent steps\n\n h_j=1+B, h_(j+1)=1+C, B,C>0,\n P=1+sum_(i<j)h_i, D=1+sum_(i>j+1)h_i,\n U=B+C+D+1=A-P.\n\nAll other physical steps are arbitrary and nonnegative. Define the\nnormalized defect and its unnormalized version by\n\n H=BC[1+B(P+A)/P^2]-D,\n Delta=P^2 H=BC[P^2+B(P+A)]-DP^2.                       (7)\n\nWe prove that D<=BC always excludes the word, and that H>0 excludes it\nwhen D>BC. This is a necessary test, not a completeness assertion.\n\n### 4.1 The capped scalar case D<=BC\n\nUse the static function\n\n F(x)=x^2/2 for x<=1, and F(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1.\n\nIts derivative min(x,1) is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz;\nthe function is convex, differentiable,1-smooth, with unique minimum0.\nStart at x_0=P. The prefix has gradient1 and ends at query j equal\nto1. The next two positions and gradients are\n\n x_(j+1)=-B, g_(j+1)=-B, x_(j+2)=BC.\n\nAll suffix queries are>=1, because their total movement is D-1 and\nBC-(D-1)>=1. Their gradients are1 and x_n=BC-D+1. Every original\nupdate and query is therefore a query of this same static function.\nIts actual values satisfy f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, so(4) gives\n\n V_h=P^2-[P+(1+B)(1+C)]^2<0.                           (8)\n\nHere s=P+B+C+D was used. In fact V_h<-3 because P>=1 and\n(1+B)(1+C)>1. All original interpolation inequalities hold by the\nactual smooth-convex function, including the optimum and terminal.\n\n### 4.2 The planar triangle case D>BC\n\nDefine\n\n kappa=B^2 C+U(D-BC), t=B^2 C/kappa,\n u=(1,0), w=(t,sqrt(t(1-t))),\n v=-(B u+D w)/(1+C), K=conv{u,v,w}.                    (9)\n\nWe have kappa>B^2 C>0 and0<t<1. The independent vectors u,w and the\npositive relation B u+(1+C)v+D w=0 show that K is a nondegenerate\ntriangle containing0 in its interior.\n\nStart at x_0=P u. Prescribe gradient u at every original query through\nj, gradient v at query j+1, and gradient w at every query from j+2\nthrough the terminal n. The positions are\n\n x_i=(1+sum_(r=i)^(j-1)h_r)u for i<=j,\n x_(j+1)=-B u,\n x_i=(1+sum_(r=i)^(n-1)h_r)w for i>=j+2.               (10)\n\nThe sum over an empty range is zero. Formula(9) makes the middle update\nx_(j+2)=D w, so every GD equality in(10) holds.\n\nWe check all normal cones, including every exterior query. The basic\ninner products are\n\n ||u||^2=1, <u,w>=||w||^2=t,\n <u,v>=-(B+D t)/(1+C), <v,w>=-(B+D)t/(1+C).\n\nEvery prefix normal is a nonnegative multiple of u. Its comparisons\nwith w-u and v-u are respectively multiples of t-1<0 and\n-(B+D t)/(1+C)-1<0.\n\nAt query j+1 the normal is y=-B u-v. Its comparisons are\n\n <w-v,y>=(-B^2 C+kappa*t)/(1+C)^2=0,\n <u-v,y>=-BC(1-t)/(1+C)<0.                             (11)\n\nFor an explicit algebra check, write\ny=[-BC u+D w]/(1+C). The coefficient of t in the first numerator is\nD(B+1+C+D)-BC(1+C+D), which equals\nUD-BC(U-B)=B^2 C+U(D-BC)=kappa.\nSubtracting -BC(1-t)/(1+C) from the second expression leaves exactly\n(-B^2 C+kappa*t)/(1+C)^2, hence zero.\n\nEvery suffix normal is a nonnegative multiple of w. Its comparisons\nwith u-w and v-w are respectively zero and multiples of\n-t[1+(B+D)/(1+C)]<0. This proves every cone in(10). Continuing the\nquadratic core through8 fails Q_(8,0); the optimum rows force its value,\nso a value repair cannot save that prescribed gradient.\n\n## 2. Original word and explicit scalar definitions\n\nFix m>=1000000, t>=1000 and define\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/m, beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, ell=1+a, b=beta c,\n    A=4+2a+beta, eta=a^2 beta, k=A/(b eta)=A/(a beta^3),\n    s=(b+1)/(k-1), D=b+s,\n    rho=(a k+1)/(k-1), e=b rho-s,\n    N=(A-1)s, C=(A-ell)s, p=ab=beta^2,\n    delta=t^(-2), omega=epsilon+delta,\n    u=N/(1+delta).\n                                                               (1)\n\nAll symbols in this draft are local aliases; none renames a project\nbenchmark or reference. The sharper lower bound D0+1>12/5+6/23+1>18/5\nproves the stated lower bound on D+1. All margins in(7) exceed\nthe corresponding error in(6).\n\nSince u=N/(1+delta),\n\n    |u-N0|<22epsilon+(4/3)delta,\n    |ell u-ell0 N0|<35epsilon+2delta.\n\nIt follows from |p-1|<2epsilon and(6) that\n\n    |x5-x50|<40omega, |v5-v50|<60omega.\n                                                               (8)\n\nIn particular |x5|<1. In the d5 expression in(2), use rho0<3/5\nto get\n\n    |d5-d50|<3epsilon+(3/5)40omega+60omega+13epsilon\n             <=100omega.\n                                                               (9)\n\nConsequently\n\n    1/27<d5<1/23.\n                                                               (10)\n\nIndeed the larger error allowed in(9) is1/5000, and\n1/26-1/5000>1/27 while1/24+1/5000<1/23.\nAlso rho^2+delta>1/4 and\n|rho^2+delta-rho0^2|<4epsilon+delta. The quotient difference gives\n\n    |E5-E50|<4[100omega+(3/20)(4epsilon+delta)]<410omega.\n\nSince E50<3/20, this implies0<E5<1/6. Substitution into(2) now yields\n\n    |j5-j50|<300omega, |w5-w50|<61omega,\n    |x6-z0|<650omega, |v6-z0|<190omega.\n                                                               (11)\n\nFor the first pair, the respective bounds before rounding are\n40omega+(1/6)3epsilon+(3/5)410omega and60omega+delta/6.\nFor x6 and v6 use1+beta=2-epsilon, |j5|<3/4 and |w5|<2/3;\nthe bounds are40omega+2*300omega+(3/4)epsilon and\n60omega+2*61omega+(2/3)epsilon. They prove(11).\n\nFinally |gamma-lambda|<5epsilon, since\nbeta^2(1+epsilon)=1-epsilon-epsilon^2+epsilon^3. With(11),\n\n    |x8-T0|<1700omega, |v8-T0|<500omega,\n    |x9-x90|<1701omega, |v9+z0|<252omega.\n                                                               (12)\n\nFor x8 the bound is(2/3)5epsilon+(5/2)650omega;\nfor v8 replace650 by190. For v9 use v8<8/5 and a0<1/2.\nEquations(5),(8)--(12) prove the following margins, which will be\nused to select the actual faces:\n\n    -3/4<j5<-2/3, -2/3<w5<-3/5,\n    3/5<x6<2/3, 3/5<v6<2/3,\n    3/2<x8<8/5, 3/2<v8<8/5,\n    1/10<x9<3/25, -2/3<v9<-3/5.\n                                                               (13)\n\nFor the narrowest x9 margin, its error is at most1701/500000,\nand4/37-1701/500000>1/10, while5/46+1701/500000<3/25.\nFor x8, 35/23-1700/500000>3/2 and\n29/19+1700/500000<8/5. The other intervals follow using the\nsmaller errors in(11)--(12) and29/46<z0<12/19.\nThus(13) does not rely on a qualitative continuity assertion.\n\n## 5. The fixed function and the actual first block\n\nThe identities D+1=ks and e+rho=a(D+1) show that T49 is exactly\nthe intersection of the three halfspaces\n\n    X<=1,\n    -t X+Y<=t D,\n    -rho t X-Y<=t e.\n                                                               (14)\n\nThe upper line contains U,Lvert, the lower contains V,Lvert,\nand the vertical line contains U,V. The other vertex in each case\nis strictly on the interior side. All three inequalities are strict\nat0 because D,e>0. Thus T49 has nonempty interior containing0.\n\nFor any X the maximum in(3) exists and is unique, by compactness of\nT49 and strict concavity of the objective in g. Completing the square\nshows that its maximizer is the Euclidean projection of X. A vector\ng in T49 is that projection if and only if\n\n    <X-g,v-g><=0 for EVERY v in T49.\n                                                               (15)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating the squared distance from X along\neach segment from g to v; sufficiency follows by expanding the squared\ndistance. Adding(15) at two inputs proves\n||g(X)-g(Z)||^2<=<g(X)-g(Z),X-Z>, hence g is1-Lipschitz.\nThe two maximizing-objective inequalities give\n\n    0<=F49(X+d)-F49(X)-<g(X),d><=||d||^2.\n\nThis proves differentiability with gradient g, as well as convexity\nand global1-smoothness. Because0 is an interior point of T49, the\nsame formula in(3) is strictly positive at every nonzero X: take a\nsufficiently small positive multiple of X as its feasible g. Therefore\n\n    min F49=0, argmin F49={0},\n    R49^2:=dist(X0,argmin F49)^2=A^2+t^2(D+1)^2.\n                                                               (16)\n\nFor the first actual H2, put\n\n    (P0,P1,P2,P3)=(0,ell,ell+1+beta,A-1),\n    (T0',T1',T2',T3')=(1,-a,a beta,-eta).\n\nThe prime distinguishes these four tangent coefficients from T0 in(4).\nFor0<=i<=3 define\n\n    Xi=(A-Pi, t(D+1)Ti'),\n    Gi=(1,t(D+1)Ti'),\n    Yi=Xi-Gi=(A-1-Pi,0),\n    fi=||Gi||^2/2+(A-1-Pi).\n                                                               (17)\n\nEach Ti' lies in[-a,1], so Gi lies on UV. Each Yi is a nonnegative\nmultiple of the outward normal of the first inequality in(14).\nThus(15) holds against EVERY vertex and Gi is the actual gradient.\nThe tangent relation Ti+1'=(1-h_i)Ti' and the partial sums Pi prove\neach of the three original updates separately. Because a,beta>0 and\neta<a, all these gradients are distinct at consecutive queries.\n\nAt index3 the residual is zero. Using D+1=ks and b eta k=A gives\n\n    X3=G3=(1,-A s t/b),\n    X4=-b X3=(-b,A s t).\n                                                               (18)\n\n## 6. Upper edge4, lower edge5, and the return at8\n\nThe upper normal is n_plus=(-t,1). The difference between its\ninner product at X4 and its supporting value tD is Nt. Subtracting\nthe corresponding normal component gives exactly\n\n    G4=(-b+u,t(s+u)),\n    Y4=X4-G4=(Nt/(1+t^2)) n_plus,\n    f4=<X4,G4>-||G4||^2/2.\n                                                               (19)\n\nThis is a point in the RELATIVE INTERIOR of Lvert U. Its horizontal\nincrement u is positive and smaller than N<4/3<b+1, and it lies\non the upper supporting line. The residual in(19) has positive\ncoefficient. Hence(14)--(15) check the remote vertex V and the\noptimum as well as the two edge vertices. G4 is the actual gradient.\nThe physical step h4=ell gives\n\n    X5=(x5,t v5).\n\nIts violation of the LOWER supporting inequality is exactly t d5>0.\nThe lower normal is n_minus=(-rho t,-1). Its true projected candidate is\n\n    G5=(j5,t w5),\n    Y5=X5-G5=(-rho E5,-delta t E5)\n                 =(t d5/(1+rho^2 t^2)) n_minus,\n    f5=<X5,G5>-||G5||^2/2.\n                                                               (20)\n\nThe definitions in(2) give rho j5+w5+e=0. Also -b<j5<1 by(7),(13).\nThus G5 lies in the relative interior of Lvert V, rather than being\nan endpoint or an affine projection outside the segment. The positive\nnormal coefficient and(14) verify(15) against the REMOTE U as well.\nThis is the second genuine face transition, at an internal query of\nthe second original H2 block.\n\nThe next original step h5=1+beta gives\n\n    X6=(x6,t v6).\n\nBy(13), x6<1, v6-x6<1/15<D, and rho x6+v6+e>0. Therefore\nX6 is in the interior of T49, and G6=X6, f6=||X6||^2/2.\nThe original step h6=ell gives\n\n    X7=-a X6.\n\nFor this point its horizontal coordinate is negative, and its upper\nline expression divided by t is a(x6-v6)<1/3<D. Its lower slack,\nalso divided by t, is\n\n    e-a(rho x6+v6)>9/10-(1/2)[(3/5)(2/3)+2/3]>0.\n\nThus G7=X7 and f7=||X7||^2/2. The physical bridge at index7 is\n1+beta c^2. Consequently\n\n    X8=-(beta c^2)X7=gamma X6=(x8,t v8).\n                                                               (21)\n\nIts horizontal coordinate is greater than3/2. Its projection is\nNOT itself. Since3/2<v8<8/5<D+1 and the bottom endpoint of UV\nhas negative vertical coordinate, the exact projection is\n\n    G8=(1,t v8), Y8=(x8-1,0),\n    f8=<X8,G8>-||G8||^2/2.\n                                                               (22)\n\nIt lies in the relative interior of UV, and the positive horizontal\nresidual proves(15) for the entire triangle, including Lvert and0.\nThe next original step h8=ell now gives\n\n    X9=(x9,t v9).\n                                                               (23)\n\nIn the second H2, G4 has positive vertical coordinate, G5 negative,\nG6 positive, and G7 negative. The gradients vary at EVERY one of its\nthree actual updates. No constant-block compression has been used.\n\n## 7. Permanent core from9 with every physical query retained\n\nBoth endpoints X9 and -c X9 lie in the strict interior of T49.\nFor X9, the right and upper inequalities in(14) follow from(13),\nand its lower slack divided by t is greater than e-2/3>0.\nFor -c X9, the right inequality holds because its horizontal coordinate\nis negative. Its upper expression divided by t satisfies\n\n    c(x9-v9)<(5/2)(3/25+2/3)=59/30<D.\n\nIts lower slack is\n\n    e+c(-rho x9-v9)>e+c[3/5-(3/5)(3/25)]>0.\n\nBy convexity,\n\n    [-c X9,X9] subset interior(T49).\n                                                               (24)\n\nHere [P,Q] denotes the closed Euclidean segment, including its endpoints.\nFor a positive integer j let nu2(j) be the exponent of2 dividing j,\nand for a nonnegative integer i let s2(i) be the number of binary1\ndigits in i, with s2(0)=0. The original word recursion gives\n\n    h_i=1+beta c^(nu2(i+1)-1), 0<=i<n.\n\nIndeed the central position in each word has the stated valuation,\nand adding its power of2 to a right-child index preserves the smaller\nvaluation of that child index. Induction on the recursion proves the\nformula. Counting multiples of2^j gives\n\n    sum_(j=1)^i nu2(j)=sum_(r>=1)floor(i/2^r)=i-s2(i).\n\nThe last equality follows by writing i in binary and summing the\ncontribution 2^r-1 of each binary1 digit. Thus the EXACT prefix product\non the original physical word is\n\n    Pprod_i:=product_(j<i)(1-h_j)\n             =(-1)^i beta^i c^(-s2(i)).\n                                                               (25)\n\nFor EVERY9<=i<=n define\n\n    sigma_i=(-1)^(i-9) beta^(i-9)c^(2-s2(i)),\n    Xi=Gi=sigma_i X9, fi=||Xi||^2/2, Yi=0.\n                                                               (26)\n\nFor odd i>=9, s2(i)>=2, so0<sigma_i<=1. For even i>=10,\ns2(i)>=1, so-c<=sigma_i<0. Hence(24) proves every Xi in(26)\nlies in T49 and is its own actual projection. Equation(25) proves\nXi+1=(1-h_i)Xi for every remaining physical step. This is induction\nfrom the already established X9, not an assumed quadratic suffix.\nAll factors are nonzero, so the gradients continue to change sign at\nevery physical query, including all later original H2 blocks.\n\n## 8. Every remote and optimum interpolation row\n\nAt every finite sample use the single value\n\n    fi=<Xi,Gi>-||Gi||^2/2.\n\nThis agrees with(17),(19),(20),(22),(26) and the core values at6,7.\nPut X_star=G_star=0, f_star=0, Y_star=0. Direct expansion of the\naccepted convention gives, for every distinct pair of indices,\n\n    Q_ij:=fi-fj+<Gi,Xj-Xi>+||Gi-Gj||^2/2\n         =<Gi-Gj,Yj>.\n                                                               (27)\n\nEvery Gi is in the SAME triangle. For j=0,1,2,3 and8, Yj is a\nnonnegative right normal; for j=4 it is a positive upper normal;\nfor j=5 it is a positive lower normal. Equations(14) and(19)--(22)\ntherefore make(27) nonpositive for EVERY possible source i, including\nall remote samples and the optimum. For j=6,7 and j>=9 it is zero.\nFor j=star it is also zero. Since G_star is in the triangle, the\nreverse optimum inequalities are included too. In particular no\nincoming or outgoing terminal row, earlier remote row, or original\nquery has been omitted.\n\nTogether with Sections5--7 this supplies a full static interpolant,\nits true minimum set, all original points, gradients, values and\nnormal conditions. Dimension2 and the paid radius in(16) are separate\nquantities. Orthogonal embedding preserves the same closest distance:\nthe minimizer set in the larger space is the orthogonal complement\nof the original plane.\n\n## 9. Two exact original-row obstructions\n\n### 9.1 Continuing the lower affine face through query6\n\nRetain all actual points, gradients and values through5. Define the\nclosest point to0 on the lower supporting LINE by\n\n    q=(e t/(1+rho^2 t^2)) n_minus,\n    w=G5-q.\n\nThen q is perpendicular to w, and(20) gives\n\n    X5=(1+d5/e)q+w,\n    X6=(d5/e-beta)q-beta w.\n\nThe affine-line continuation would prescribe\n\n    G6_aff=q-beta w,\n    Y6_aff=[d5/e-(1+beta)]q.\n                                                               (28)\n\nBy(7),(10), the scalar in square brackets is negative. Using the\nnatural projection-form value at6, direct expansion(27) gives\n\n    Q_(0,6_aff)\n       =(1+beta-d5/e) e ell(D+1)t^2/(1+rho^2 t^2)>20.\n                                                               (29)\n\nTo see this exact numerator, U=G0 and\n\n    <q,U-q>=-e ell(D+1)t^2/(1+rho^2 t^2).\n\nThis follows from <n_minus,U>-e t=-ell(D+1)t, using\ne+rho=a(D+1). The candidate G6_aff lies on the same supporting\nline as q, so <q,G6_aff>=||q||^2. Substitution into(28) proves\nthe equality in(29). For its quantitative bound, d5/e<10/207<1/20,\n1+beta>19/10, ell>7/5, D+1>18/5, e>9/10, and\n1/(rho^2+delta)>5/2. Their product is greater than\n(37/20)(9/10)(7/5)(18/5)(5/2)=209790/10000>20.\n\nAllowing a free value f6 cannot repair this prescribed gradient while\nthe prior value f5 is retained. In fact\n\n    G5-G6_aff=(1+beta)w,\n    X5-X6=(1+beta)G5,\n    <G5-G6_aff,X5-X6>=||G5-G6_aff||^2.\n\nTherefore Q_(5,6_aff)+Q_(6_aff,5)=0 independently of f6. If both\nrows were nonpositive, both would be equalities and they would uniquely\nforce f6. The projection-form value used in(29) makes both equalities\nhold, since both normals are perpendicular to w. Thus it is the forced\nvalue and the original remote row(0,6) remains positive. This rejects\nonly this same-line continuation with the specified retained jets;\nit does not reject arbitrary new gradients or a new whole transcript.\n\n### 9.2 Declaring a permanent core at query6\n\nRetain the ACTUAL construction through7, including its optimum and\ninitial jet. The physical query X8 is fixed by(21). The false quadratic\ncontinuation would set G8_core=X8. The two optimum rows in accepted\n1252b8823bb5a290 force f8_core=||X8||^2/2. Since Y0=(A-1,0),\n\n    Q_(8_core,0)=(A-1)(x8-1)>(A-1)/2>2.\n                                                               (30)\n\nThus even with free new values the stated core continuation is\nimpossible for ANY smooth convex interpolant retaining these earlier\njets and optimum. The actual projection(22) repairs this row by\nreturning to the old vertical face. Both source and target in(30)\nare original physical queries, not added support points or compressed\nblock endpoints.\n\n## 10. Exact true-distance payoff\n\nSince n=2^m-1 is odd and s2(n)=m, formulas(16),(26) yield the exact\nnormalized payoff of this actual function:\n\n    V49(m,t):=2(F49(Xn)-min F49)/dist(X0,argmin F49)^2\n      =beta^(2(n-9)) c^(4-2m)\n             (x9^2+t^2 v9^2)/(A^2+t^2(D+1)^2)>0.\n                                                               (31)\n\nLet Qunit=Pprod_n^2=beta^(2n)c^(-2m). This is the scalar unit\nquadratic's exact payoff on the SAME word. Using t>=1 and(7),(13),\n\n    (x9^2+t^2 v9^2)/(A^2+t^2(D+1)^2)\n       <[(3/25)^2+(2/3)^2]/(18/5)^2\n        =2581/72900<1/25.\n\nBernoulli's inequality beta^18>=(1-18/m)>=9/10 follows by induction\nfrom (1-v)^r>=1-rv for0<=v<=1. Thus\n\n    V49/Qunit<beta^(-18)c^4/25\n              <(10/9)(5/2)^4/25=125/72<2.\n                                                               (32)\n\nThe bound by2 Qunit has a precise large-aspect limit. Fix any allowed\nm and let t tend to infinity, so delta tends to0 while the enclosing\nword stays fixed. All coefficients in(1)--(2) remain finite and have\nlimits. At4 the projected vertical coefficient tends to A s; at5\nit tends to -a A s because delta E5 tends to0. Hence successive\nvertical coefficients give\n\n    lim_(t->infinity) v6=a beta A s,\n    lim_(t->infinity) v8=beta^4 A s,\n    lim_(t->infinity) v9=-a beta^4 A s\n                       =(D+1)Pprod_9.\n\nThe last identity uses D+1=k s=A s/(a beta^3) and\nPprod_9=-beta^9/c^2. Divide numerator and denominator of the radius\nratio in(31) by t^2. Since x9 and A remain bounded, it follows that\n\n    lim_(t->infinity) V49(m,t)/Qunit(m)=1.                (32a)\n\nThus arbitrarily increasing the aspect does not improve its asymptotic\nscale, even though the same three-face pattern remains present at\nevery finite allowed t. No claim that V49<=Qunit at every finite t\nis needed or asserted.\n\nThe payoff is paid at the closest actual minimizer by(16). For any\npositive smoothness constant L_s, replace F49 by L_s F49 and use\nsteps h_i/L_s. All positions, closest distances and normalized payoffs\nstay the same. The convention is W=2R throughout.\n\n## 11. Complete same-word references and original B_n\n\nPut S=sum H_m and M=1+S. The exact multiplicities of the recursively\ndefined steps give\n\n    M=2^m+(beta/c)(c^m-2^m)/(c-2)\n       =(1-zeta)2^m+zeta c^m,\n    zeta=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).\n                                                               (33)\n\nIndeed summing the unit portions gives2^m-1, and summing the excesses\ngives (beta/c)sum_(j=0)^(m-1)2^(m-1-j)c^j. The geometric sum is\nthe displayed quotient. Since c>lambda>2 and lambda(lambda-2)=1,\none has c(c-2)>1>beta. Consequently M<c^m.\n\nThe COMPLETE reference from accepted b98bb807d514e80d is\n\n    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S),\n      max_(0<=j<n) (h_j-1)_+^2/\n         [(1+sum_(i<j)h_i)^2(1+2sum_(i>j)h_i)]}.\n\nDefine Huber=1/(1+2S)=1/(2M-1). The complete F_ray_n of accepted\n1fca3e5dd555cb4a maximizes over ALL feasible chronological checkpoint\nsets, retaining the offsets and accumulated value areas specified\nthere; in particular its zero-cut option is Huber and its one-cut\noptions are all the displayed K terms. Thus Huber<=K_n<=F_ray_n,\nwith no checkpoint choice suppressed.\n\nUsing(32)--(33), beta<1 and c>2,\n\n    V49/Huber=V49(2M-1)<4 c^(-m)<1/10.\n                                                               (34)\n\nThe final strict inequality already follows for m>=6 because2^6>40;\nour stated domain has m>=1000000. This proves the full K/ray comparison.\nIt uses a lower bound on each complete reference, rather than replacing\nthe reference by a chosen checkpoint.\n\nFor completeness, the FULL complex reference in accepted\ndfde2fa326c908bf uses Poly_i(z)=product_(j<i)(1-h_j z), the real-plane\npoints xi_i=Poly_i(z), gradients gamma_i=z Poly_i(z), and the optimum0.\nFor every directed simple cycle C on{star,0,...,n}, it imposes\n\n    (Re(z)-|z|^2) Energy_C>=2|Im(z) Area_C|,\n    Energy_C=sum_(j->i in C)|xi_i-xi_j|^2,\n    Area_C=sum_(j->i in C)Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i).\n\nD_h is the set satisfying ALL these cycles. Gap_h(z) is the maximal\nnormalized terminal objective among interpolants of these specified\npoints and gradients with f(0)=0, using initial point1 and denominator1.\nWe compare with the entire supremum over D_h.\n\nTake the real parameter theta=1/(2M). It is in[0,1], so all cycle\ninequalities hold: their right sides vanish and their left coefficients\nare nonnegative. The explicit convex1-smooth scalar quadratic\nf_theta(x)=theta x^2/2 produces the exact specified real polynomial\npoints and gradients. It can be embedded in the real plane by making\nthe function independent of the second coordinate. Its minimum is0\nand its initial squared distance is1. Therefore the maximality in the\naccepted fact implies\n\n    Gap_h(theta)>=theta Poly_n(theta)^2.\n\nAll factors are positive and\nsum_j theta h_j=S/(2M)<1/2. Induction on the factors, using\n(1-u)(1-v)>=1-u-v for u,v>=0, proves\n\n    Poly_n(theta)>=1-theta S>1/2,\n    sup_(z in D_h)Gap_h(z)>1/(8M)>1/(8c^m).\n                                                               (35)\n\nTogether with(32),\n\n    V49/[sup_(z in D_h)Gap_h(z)]<16 c^(-m)<1/10.\n                                                               (36)\n\nThe final inequality follows for m>=8 because2^8>160. This is a\ncomparison to the FULL unaugmented complex channel, not only its\nuniversal lens. It also dominates V49 after any Huber augmentation.\n\nFinally retain the ORIGINAL all-split recurrences. In the notation of\naccepted b98bb807d514e80d they are Cbench_0=B_0=1 and\n\n    Cbench_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(Cbench_j,Cbench_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) Gjoin(Cbench_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n    Gjoin(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2.\n\nCbench is only a local alias for the accepted primitive mass. Using\nits proved bound B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3)<5*3^m at n=2^m-1, and(32),\n\n    0<B_n V49<10(3/c^2)^m<10(25/48)^m ->0.\n                                                               (37)\n\nThis closes the entire stated large-aspect family uniformly, even if\nt grows arbitrarily fast with m. It is not an upper bound on W_n(h),\nand does not transfer the finite accepted H6 result to another word.\n\n## 12. Exact remaining boundaries\n\nThe first new conclusion is a complete legal three-face construction\nfor every stated(m,t), with changing gradients inside physical H2\nblocks, every ordered original row, and true minimum/radius accounting.\nIt enters the core permanently only AFTER the remote-forced return8.\nIts payoff is quantitatively below every complete reference above.\n\nThe two row obstructions(29)--(30) retain specific earlier jets and\nthe optimum. They close exactly the corresponding proposed next\ngradients, including arbitrary reassigned new values. They do not\nexclude whole-transcript redesign, different extra vertices, conjugate\nheights, a moved initial state, or a changing closest-minimum state.\n\nTogether with max48 as awareness only, the two drafts identify a\nbalanced-aspect failure and the present large-aspect failure. This\njoint draft alone does NOT close the intermediate/small aspect region,\nall triangles, all polytopes, or all changing-gradient laws. It does\nnot assert optimality of its phase partition outside its stated domain.\n\nA stronger family on these H_m words would still leave the original\nall-n/all-nonnegative-word lower comparison BLOCKED.
